# Copper Gulch: plan

The design is in `GDD.md`. This file is the roadmap the hourly scheduled task works through.

## How the task works through this roadmap
1. **Pick the next box.** Take the first unticked box in the first unfinished milestone. Work boxes in order for as long as the run lasts.
2. **Release.** Run the release checklist below.
   - Green: publish, tick the boxes you finished, and add a one-line `Notes:` under each.
   - Red: fix it, or stash the broken part and publish what is green. Never push red.
3. **Targets.** The last box of each milestone is its target: one measurable line, proved by a bot or a test. The milestone is done when its target passes.
4. **Target missed.** Add the fix box it needs just above the target and keep going.
5. **Decisions.** Decide sensibly and write the decision in the box's note. Never stop to ask the owner.
6. **Fit the release in the run.** Start the bots with `node tools/bots.js <scratch dir>` (balance, firstplay and idle side by side, each into its own file, one verdict line each) in the background as soon as the simulation change is settled (a release with no simulation change can start them at once), and commit once every check is green, before taking up more work. Two runs in a row (2026.11.21-3 and -4) built a box and stopped before the commit, and the next run spent its first hour checking it again.
7. **Machine load.** Before judging a perf gate, look at the machine's CPU load. Over about 40% from other programs, find out why and wait for it to settle; a reading taken under load is not a regression. A gate that flips red and green with nothing changed points at the machine first (2026.11.21-4: an orphan test browser from five days before kept four cores busy and put every gate 3-8 ms up). Look for any headless Edge with no live parent, not only this repo's (2026.11.21-6: one from another session's screenshot tool had run six days). Never stop smoke or shots with `timeout`; run them in the background. Two browser tools never run at the same time, not even from a second worktree: each one stops the headless browsers left over when it starts, so the second kills the first (2026.11.24-6: a seen-red smoke run started beside the release smoke crashed at its first step). Run a seen-red check after the build's own run, one at a time.
8. **Release-sized boxes.** A new box, above all an owner item, is split into parts that each fit one release (one system, its checks and its shots) when it is added, not halfway through the run that works it. Five boxes had to be split mid-run (the Towns look, the level-20 fork, Market depth, its part 2, sharper models on 2026-11-22), and each time the split cost the run its first hour of planning. A box whose gate covers several separate stretches of play (three gaps, three towns) gets one part per stretch when it is added (2026.11.23-5: fix 3b's one gate covered three gaps that need three different fixes, found only after its first probe).
9. **Audit before every long check.** Run `node --test test/*.test.js` and `node tools/audit.js` after every edit and before starting smoke, shots or the bots. A patch script that puts a // comment mid-line swallows the code after it; the audit catches it in a second, but in 2026.11.22-4 it was found only after a smoke and shots run had started on the broken build (the reputation row was gone), the third time this bug was written. A patch script that holds a regex or "\n" loses its backslashes on the way through the shell (a raw backspace in place of `\b`, a line break in place of `\n`); make regex edits with the Edit tool, and the audit fails any control character left in src, tools or test (2026.11.23-1). A patch script puts a new comment on its own line above the code, never at the end of a line it adds to: in 2026.11.23-7 a comment added mid-line swallowed code twice (a Goods sheet line, so the page did not load, and a boss bar line), the sixth and seventh times, and one of them was missed because the audit was skipped after that edit. The audit now also parses every file in src, tools and test (`parseRules`), so any syntax error fails it. A patch script holds no backslash at all: in 2026.11.23-9 the shell dropped one from a quoted heredoc three times (a regex's `\.` became `.`, and a `\u00a0` escape became the raw character in two files), so text with a backslash is changed with the Edit tool, or the script builds it with `String.fromCharCode(92)`; the audit now fails a raw no-break space. The Edit tool is no way round it for a `\u` escape either: it wrote `\u00a0` as the raw character (2026.11.24-3, caught by the audit), so such a character is built with `String.fromCharCode` in the code itself; and a patch script never writes an escaped apostrophe (`\'`), which lost its backslash and ended a string early in the same run: reword, or quote with the other kind of quote.
10. **Smoke is green only by its exit code.** Write `echo "smoke exit $?"` after it and read that line and every FAIL line, never only the last lines of its output. A new smoke check is run once on the build it ships with before the commit. 2026.11.23-13 shipped with two of its own new checks red in every run (the To arms width and the dot check), found only by the next run. A check that hit-tests a shape samples points inside the shape: the dot check sampled the corners of a round dot's box, where the browser finds what is under it.
11. **A changed tool runs once on a short case before a long run.** In 2026.11.24-2 two edits to `tools/fork-runs.js` (a helper it never imported, a field the `--noruns` rows lacked) each crashed three seeds' runs after their 2-minute empire run, and the first cost 12 minutes before anyone looked. After editing a probe, run it on one seed with the smallest case it has (one set, `--noruns`, `--seed 1`) and read its first rows before starting the parallel runs. A judge of a new kind of option is checked for what it measures before tuning on it: fork-runs judged every fork by its tower's own damage, and both Frost Lens sides trade that for help to the others, so its first tuning round ranked them by the wrong number.
12. **A fix box from an older review starts with a probe on today's build.** Later fixes often close part of it. Measure each finding on the current build with a core probe first (a minute or two, no browser), and build only what is still open (2026.11.24-8: of clarity fix 4's four findings, `tools/broke-probe.js` found only the first 35 seconds of the game still open, since the fixes after the review had closed the rest). The remaining clarity fixes (6, 7, 8, 9) start this way. A spotlight on a world object is checked against where that object is on screen, never only that the box shows: the Mortar card's box sat on Hold waves for weeks with every check green, since the pit it pointed at lay under the top bar.

## Rules from the owner
The rules for every game in this folder (free forever, no personal details, American spelling, the words players see, more than one way to win, teach as it appears, smooth and direct touch, installable, release notes, saves) are in `CLAUDE.md` in the parent folder. They all apply here. These are Copper Gulch's own rules on top, and how this repo enforces the shared ones.

**Copper Gulch**
- The look: a crisp, flat pixel UI with hard 2px borders and pixel bevels, no glossy gradients or glass. Modern motion is welcome.
- Maps (GDD.md, Town maps):
  - every lane winds through the town and doubles or triples back past the towers, with buildings along it;
  - every lane has a real entrance, and raiders stay hidden until they come out of it (`tools/audit.js` fails a lane without an entrance model);
  - every map has 3 to 5 mortar pits, too far from the lane for normal towers (`tools/audit.js`, `mapRules`).
  - every pad stands at least 1.15 from the road's middle, so its footing never sits on the road's edge, and every building model stays within 0.96 of its plot's middle (`test/pads.test.js`, `FIT` in `src/render/board.js`; look pass, 2026.11.21-2).
  - a new pad in a town with the Frost Lens keeps 2.5 back from the first 3 units of a lane past its entrance, where raiders come out bunched (`test/pads.test.js`; 2026.11.20-17: a Frost Lens there did 40-46% of the damage).
- A new tower option (a level-5 path or a level-20 fork) gets its numbers from runs of the waves, not from the bots' worth guess: `node tools/fork-runs.js --kind K --seed N --sets "a.dmg=.6;..."` puts a level-20 tower of each side on every pad of the bots' late Ridge and Falls and plays the next waves, and the worth guess is then set so its picks split the pads about as the runs do (`--noruns --against <an earlier run's output>` tries worth numbers in 3 minutes and prints how many pads each agrees on). Seen three times: the Still's guess picked Last Call on 34 of 36 Ridge pads against the runs (2026.11.21-7), the Cannon's picked Big Shell on 35 of 36 while the runs split them (2026.11.21-9), and a changed Last Call put the old guess on 33 of 36 against a 19-17 split (2026.11.22-6). A change to a fork's numbers or effect re-checks the guess the same way, not only a new fork.
- A change that makes the bots keep more towers of one kind to high levels is checked for what their reserve holds back: a tower's goods grow 1.35 times a level, so a few towers at level 18 keep thousands of a good, and a workshop that needs the same good starves (2026.11.21-8: the Ridge Still's iron held back the Blast Furnace, the Capital's contracts slowed and the Empire tree missed its pace). `node tools/late-towers.js --seed N` prints the tree pace, the contracts and the Wire Mill for one seed in about 2 minutes; try numbers there, six seeds side by side, before a full bot run.
- Auto-sell keeps a tower reserve from the start of the game: goods the next tower builds, upgrades and path choices need are never sold. Auto-sell keeps enough that building and upgrading towers is never waiting on goods once the coins are there, and the default works with no tuning (owner, 2026-11-19).
- A new research item, perk or fork that trades one stat for another is run on and off on two seeds before the full bots (`tools/progress.js --one active <seed> <file>` or an empire run with the item taken out, 2 minutes each), since a guessed trade has been too strong each time it was checked (2026.11.23-5: Telescope Sights at 15% farther and 10% slower put Silver Falls under its window; reach is worth more on a road that doubles back).
- A new system that moves a bot gate is probed on and off with the same seeds before anything is tuned (`tools/boom-probe.js` does it for booms), and a strategy gate is a median over at least 6 seeds (the Silver Falls table still takes 3 and moves to 6 with the minmax box below; 2026.11.22-3: booms tipped two gates, and the A/B showed one was a 3-seed median moving about 10% with any change and the other a bot that flips on the war wagon).
- The newcomer bot breaks a save at a line the game shows, not a private number: a named tower for a tower goal, the fight step's 40% vault (`FIGHT_LOW`) for the Capital's first building. Its own 70% (`VAULT_LOW`) broke a save the strip asked for twice (part 2c, a tower goal; part 2d, 42K of a 43.5K save), so a new kind of coin goal checks this first, with a trace of what spent the save (`tools/spend-probe.js`).
- A gate that takes the worst of 6 seeds (the quiet probe's longest stretch) moves 3-6 minutes on some seed with any change to the newcomer's path, so a try is judged by A/B on the same 6 seeds with the minutes over the limit summed, never by which seed is worst (2026.11.23-10: five tries for part 2c each moved the worst stretch to another seed, 11 on seed 2 became 14 on seed 1, 18 on seed 6, 20 on seed 1).
- A new system that changes the economy opens on a won wave, never on a clock (2026.11.22-4: town orders on a 10-minute clock from the second town opened while the bot was still in Pine Hollow and tipped seed 4's Ridge wave 10; booms on a clock tipped the Gulch table in 2026.11.22-3). Its probe (`tools/order-probe.js` or `tools/boom-probe.js`, on and off, same seeds) shows the first-hour gates unchanged before a full bot run.
- A gate that tips on one seed after a change that should not touch it is traced on that seed, minute by minute, on this build and the last release, before anything is tuned (2026.11.22-8: the Wire Mill gate fell to 50% on seed 4 with the Crossbow's fork; the trace showed a wire boom moved into that seed's Falls hour and a foreman buying 15 levels its copper could not feed, a game bug the fork had only uncovered). Both builds run side by side from a `git worktree` of the last release.
- A gate that prints a close line in 3 releases in a row gets its own box in the current milestone, with the cause found by a probe, not one more note (roadmap review, 2026.11.22-2: the idle growth gate on seeds 1 and 4 was close for about six releases and tipped when Auto Call to arms joined; a probe found in 20 minutes that the Gulch sat on hold 45-60 minutes an hour. The economy Gulch wave 10 has sat exactly at its 25% limit since 2026.11.21-2).
- A model built for the close town view is checked from the map's height before the map reuses it (2026.11.21-5: the boards' block-built entrances read as crates, a brick fence and a fortress on the map, so the map draws its own in rounded rock). New map land gets a before and after look review (`tools/look-shots.js <dir> map`).
- Building tags are quiet by default, a small dark pill with the level. A full tag shows only when it matters: the goal's building. The building whose card is open has no tag, since its card names it and its glow marks it (owner, 2026-11-19; review of 2026.11.20-10: "Level 10" sat over the card's "Level 10").
- The wave bar keeps one width at every wave and phase: the wave number, the vault, one fixed timer and Call now. What the next wave brings is on a card the wave number opens (owner, 2026-11-19).
- Outposts (GDD.md, Outposts): the game starts in Dry Creek Outpost (ore and stone, Crossbow only, one loop road, a small boss at wave 10), and winning it opens Copper Gulch. Each later town opens one more outpost with two raw goods for its era. Outposts keep working in the background and feed raw goods to the towns and the market. They have an entrance but no mortar pits, and the chain rules exempt them.
- Pacing numbers (GDD.md, Pacing targets): the first tower under 1 minute, Dry Creek won in 10-15 minutes, Copper Gulch wave 10 at 7-9 minutes after it opens for the active bot, no breach in waves 1-3, the first charter offered at 35-45 minutes, and the rest of the table. `tools/balance.js` proves them.
- A goal strip step that needs another place (its wave, its building) sends the player there with Map or the back button, and its count shows only in that place (seen twice: "9 / 30" in the Gulch read as the Gulch's wave, 2026.11.23-2; "Reach wave 30 in Iron Ridge" kept the newcomer in the Gulch for 2 hours, 2026.11.23-4). The Capital leads the strip in every town (not an outpost still being won) once only its coins are missing, since the town's own goals spend the same purse (`goalNow` in `src/core/goals.js`).
- A toast about one place's own things (its research, its charter, its goals) goes when the player leaves that place, and one that only applies where a button exists shows only there (seen twice: the lost toast over the map's labels, 2026.11.19-3; Iron Ridge's "New research" toast in Pine Hollow and "Tap Perks" where Pine Hollow has no Perks, 2026.11.23-6). Such a toast has `kind: 'here'`, dropped when the town's screen is taken down.
- Range and blast read Short, Medium or Long and Small or Wide; a Mortar's range reads "Long, not up close".
- The player can always see how much of each good they have (owner, 2026-11-17): a goods strip on the play screen with a count for every good the town's towers and next upgrades use, and every cost on a card shows have / need, red when short. `tools/smoke.js` checks it.
- Time away always counts, whether the app was closed or only in the background (owner, 2026-11-17). Coming back from the background catches up the time it was hidden, like a cold start, unless the player pressed Pause. `tools/smoke.js` checks it.
- Coins are one shared purse for the whole empire (owner decision, 2026-11-17): every town, outpost, bounty and sale adds to it and every purchase spends from it; a charter resets a town but never the purse.
- Call now is never locked. The player decides when the next wave comes (owner, 2026-11-17). If calling early ever makes a strategy too strong, fix it with the bonus or the wave, never by graying the button.
- Boss waves are gates (owner, 2026-11-17). A boss wave is beaten only when its boss is killed. If the boss reaches the vault or stockpile, the town drops back like a fallen vault and the next wave number never passes an unbeaten boss. Every town and outpost.
- Live numbers never change the size or place of anything on screen (owner, 2026-11-17: the goods strip "twitches back and forth" as counts grow). Every live count has a fixed-width cell, tabular digits, and a formatter of at most 4 characters.
- Every town keeps playing when you are not looking at it, unless you hold its waves (owner, 2026-11-17). Out of play and during catch-up a town fights its waves with the background battle.
- A town that loses a boss wave or its vault while you are away holds its own waves until you go there and tap Resume waves or Call now, so it pays the coin loss at most once (owner, 2026-11-17). In the town in play a lost boss also holds the waves.
- The top bar never grows with the number of towns (owner, 2026-11-17). It shows the place's name, a Map button and one back button to the last place; all other switching goes through the empire map.
- Every object opens with one tap anywhere on screen (owner, 2026-11-17: towers and buildings were hard to tap, depending on where they were). Smoke taps every tower, building and pad at every size and camera angle.
- Only the map zooms; the page never does (owner, 2026-11-17: zooming sometimes zoomed the overlay too).
- The game fills the screen after every rotation or window resize (owner, 2026-11-18: turning the phone left the right half blank). Nothing pins the canvas to a pixel size, every view measures it each frame, and `tools/smoke.js` (rotate) flips portrait to landscape and back on the title, in a town and on the map.

**How this repo enforces the shared rules**
- Storage keys are only `cg-*`. The old game's key `copper-gulch-save-v1` may be read, never written. Saves bump `v` with a migration for any change to their shape.
- `tools/audit.js`: a pure core, no other hosts, `cg-*` keys, American spelling, no personal details, an explainer for every feature, and `textRules` (no narrator lines, no bare decimals, no fact said twice on a card).
- `tools/perf.js` (headless Edge, phone size, CPU slowed 4x): median frame under 33 ms, plus the raider smoothness check (no frame-to-frame jump over 3 times the median, never standing still on most frames). It reports draw calls and triangles. `--town empire --script 10 --calls 300` is the M3 frame gate: Iron Ridge in play with three towns working behind it, the frame under 33 ms, the game's own work in it (the frame less its wait for the GPU) under 10 ms and fewer than 300 draw calls. `--profile` prints the functions that took the most time. A gate within 10% of its limit prints "close" (frame, script, draw calls). A run with a close script gate is measured twice, and a change is judged by an A/B run with `--eval` switching it off, never by one run (2026.11.21-3: the Silver Falls script part read 8.9-10.8 ms run to run with nothing changed). perf.js measures a near miss (within 15% over) once more and keeps the better run, for the frame and for the script part alike. `--report "js"` prints a value read from the page after measuring (for counting calls with `--eval`).
- `tools/audit.js` `swallowRules`: no code after a // comment on the same line (it never runs, and it shipped twice).
- `tools/smoke.js`: Pause and Hold waves on the play screen, the selection highlight following the card, the camera keeping the grabbed ground within 4 px of each finger, the What's new card, save slots, the explainers and offline play, at phone, landscape and desktop sizes.
- `tools/playthrough.js` plays a newcomer for hours on the core and shoots every moment that matters in the real game; it runs at every evaluation box and whenever the owner asks how intuitive the game is, and its shots and `moments.md` go to the fresh-eyes reviewer. A tool that loads saves into the game writes them from a page with no game on it, since the game saves itself as it unloads (2026.11.23-2).
- `tools/capital-probe.js [--price N]` prints the newcomer's Ridge wave, purse and income every 15 minutes and when it opens the Capital (about 2 minutes for 6 seeds); `tools/quiet-probe.js --seed N` prints the playthrough's quiet stretches on the core alone, without the browser (about a minute). Use them before a full playthrough or bot run (clarity fix 3).
- Every step the goal strip can show that the player can take now is a tap the newcomer bot makes (`goalTap` in `tools/firstplay.js`, `test/firstplay.test.js`): a step with no tap is never taken in any bot run, so no gate sees what comes after it (seen twice: in Evaluation 2 nothing sent the newcomer to Pine Hollow, and until 2026.11.23-4 no newcomer ever founded the Capital, since "Tap Map, then tap the Capital to build it" had no tap). A step that names two acts is taken in full: under "Build more towers, then tap Resume waves" the bot builds first (`buildFirst`, `test/quiet.test.js`; 2026.11.23-6: it tapped Resume at once and the newcomer's Ridge lost its wave-10 boss for 30 minutes, the third strip step a bot did not follow as written).
- `tools/progress.js <dir>` runs every bot through the Falls with a watcher on the core and writes the progression table (the longest wait with nothing to buy or see, the gaps between unlocks, each strategy's stall, each layer by hour, charters); it runs with the playthrough at every evaluation box (2026.11.23-3: it found 1-3.5 hour gaps with nothing new and a strategy that never reached the Falls, which no gate measured, since every gate counts times to milestones).
- A crash in any bot run is a core bug, fixed in the core with a test that sets up that very step, never worked around in the bot. `test/mix.test.js` runs each town's first boss wave against every tower finish at four levels; a new tower finish or boss stage joins it by itself (2026.11.23-3: a raider spreading poison fell in the step a boss dropped its crew).
- A new kind of strip step is proved with a bot trace that it clears when the newcomer follows it, gated on how long it stays up in a row, not only on whether it shows (2026.11.24-9: "upgrade the Steel Mill" showed for the right reason and stayed up 3.7 hours, since the building that really fed it was one step further back; `tools/stall-probe.js` now fails a stall step up over 10 minutes in a row). Seen before with the Smelter goal held 20 minutes and the Capital's count for hours, each found only by a review.
- `tools/install-test.js` prints ok for every release, locally and against the live site (Chrome on Android must offer "Install app").
- Word tests check what a function returns or what the screen shows, not a line of source text (roadmap review, 2026-11-20: 2026.11.20-18 changed nine old review tests that pinned source strings, three of them for one map phrase). An old test that pins source is rewritten that way when a change touches it. The exact words on screen are pinned in one file, `test/words.test.js`; older review tests check what the words must do (short, plain, no colon) and never the words themselves, so a reworded label changes one test, not ten (roadmap review, 2026.11.20-19: "Won before signing" was pinned in eleven tests).
- A placement check runs in every state the player can put the screen in, not only the home view: with each card open, at phone and landscape (2026.11.24-6: smoke checked the map's labels only with no card open, while three reviews in a row found the problems with one open). A placement gate (labels, cards, a camera frame) that two layout tries only move from one place to another is fixed in what places things, the placer or the camera, not by a third layout (2026.11.24-4: six ring layouts moved the worst map label from the Gulch to Sandy Bend to Silver Falls and held 1a back a whole run; a steeper map camera and one more spot in the label placer took the worst share from 0.37 to 0.24 at once).
- A label that two screenshot reviews flagged is not reworded a third time: change the control (two plain buttons in place of a pill), let a picture say it, or drop it (roadmap review, 2026.11.20-19: the old-waves row was reworded in six reviews and the Goods stock switch in five).
- Release notes: `src/data/news.js` gets an entry per release (the audit fails without one), then a GitHub release and a push notification (see the checklist).

## Release checklist (every release; stop and fix on any failure)
1. `node --test test/*.test.js` passes (Node 24 does not take a bare folder).
2. `node tools/audit.js` prints `audit ok`.
3. Run the bots that the current milestone's target names. From M1 on that is `tools/balance.js` (6 seeds by default, with the strategy and variety checks); from M2, `tools/firstplay.js`; from M4, `tools/idle.js`. Write their numbers in the notes.
4. `node tools/smoke.js` ends with phone, land and desk `ok`, and `node tools/perf.js` prints ok (median frame under 33 ms; write the numbers in the notes). From M3 on, `node tools/perf.js --town empire --script 10 --calls 300` prints ok too (the M3 target: Iron Ridge in play with three towns working behind it). From M5 on, `--town falls-empire --script 10 --calls 300` prints ok too (Silver Falls in play with every town, outpost and the Capital working behind it).
5. `node tools/shots.js <scratch dir>` (and, after the version bump, `node tools/news-shot.js <scratch dir>` for the What's new card): look at every screenshot and fix anything ugly or broken. Then a subagent that sees only those screenshots reads every visible line and flags anything that sounds like a manual or a narrator; fix what it flags.
6. Add the release's entry at the top of `src/data/news.js` (plain words), `node tools/bump-version.js "note, no apostrophes"`, then `node tools/update-test.js` and `node tools/install-test.js` print ok.
7. Commit, push `main`, wait for the Pages build, and confirm the live `version.json` shows the new version. Then `node tools/install-test.js https://jonathanwessel.github.io/copper-gulch/` prints ok (the live manifest and icons).
8. Tell the owner (changed 2026-11-17 by the owner's main session: GitHub never emails you about releases made with your own account): `git tag -a v<version> -m "<2-5 plain lines>"` and `git push origin v<version>`. `.github/workflows/release.yml` then makes the release as github-actions, which emails the owner. Check it with `gh run list -R jonathanwessel/copper-gulch -L 3` and `gh release view v<version> -R jonathanwessel/copper-gulch`. Do not run `gh release create`. Then a push notification of one line under 200 characters.

Until a tool exists, the box that creates it is the first to use it; skip the steps for tools that don't exist yet.

## Roadmap

### M1. Copper Gulch, playable
- [x] **Bootstrap.**
  - `index.html` with an import map, `sw.js`, `manifest.webmanifest`, icons, and `package.json` set to `{"type":"module"}` with no dependencies.
  - Vendored three.js, copied from `../tycoon/vendor/three`.
  - Tools copied from `../tycoon/scripts`: `cdp`, `serve`, `update-test`, `bump-version`, `make-icons`.
  - `audit` v0, and a test harness.
  - Shipped when the live site shows a title over a Gulch model and works offline.
  - Installable as an app (owner requirement, added after the first release), above all in Chrome on Android:
    - `manifest.webmanifest` linked from `index.html`: name, short_name, id, start_url and scope `./`, display standalone with display_override fullscreen, orientation any, theme and background colors matching the page, a description; PNG icons 192 and 512 with purpose any, separate 192 and 512 maskable icons with the art inside the safe zone, and an apple-touch-icon. Every path relative (the site lives under `/copper-gulch/`).
    - `sw.js` registered with scope `./`, with a real fetch handler that serves the game offline.
    - An Install button: catch beforeinstallprompt, show the button only while the prompt is available (on the title, and in Settings once the Phone shell builds it), call prompt() on tap, hide it after appinstalled or when already running as an app. On iPhone and iPad, a one-line "Share, then Add to Home Screen" hint instead.
    - `tools/install-test.js`: CDP Page.getAppManifest with no errors and every icon loading at its size, and Page.getInstallabilityErrors returning an empty list; run on the served repo and on the live URL after publishing.
  - Notes (install): shipped 2026.11.16-6. `tools/install-test.js` on the served repo: no manifest errors; icons 192, 512, maskable 192, maskable 512 and the 180 apple-touch-icon all load at their sizes; installabilityErrors `[]`; headless Chrome fired beforeinstallprompt and the Install button showed. A broken copy (no icons, no fetch handler) fails it with `no-acceptable-icon`, so the check bites. The maskable icons are the scene from further back so the saloon sits inside the safe zone. `index.html` catches beforeinstallprompt in a tiny inline script before the modules load; `src/ui/install.js` owns the button (`installButton()`), counting fullscreen and minimal-ui as already installed because of display_override. Live check after publishing (https://jonathanwessel.github.io/copper-gulch/): version.json 2026.11.16-6, service worker in control, no manifest errors, all five icons at their sizes, installabilityErrors `[]`, the prompt offered and the Install button shown.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.16-2. Tools are ES modules now (`tools/*.js`); also added `smoke` (phone, land, desk, then offline with the server stopped) and `shots`. The title scene (`src/render/title-scene.js`) uses the old buildings, adapted into `src/assets/models/gulch.js`; the Board box replaces it with the real board. Logo is a code-drawn 5x7 pixel font (`src/art/pixfont.js`), no web fonts. Version lives in `src/version.js`. Caches are named `cg-shell-*` because the old game on the same origin deletes its own `copper-gulch-*` caches. `update-test` also checks the new version boots offline. Audit v0: pure core, no other hosts, cg-* keys, American spelling, no emails or LAN addresses, matching versions, no missing cached files.
- [x] **Core.**
  - `src/core/`: `rng` (a seeded stream per town), `clock` (fixed 0.25 s steps and offline catch-up), `events` (the bus), `mods` (named sources with caps), `town` (`class Town`, with no global state), `save` (v1 with migrations), `game`.
  - Must be deterministic: no DOM, no three.js, no `Math.random`.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.16-3. rng is sfc32 with its state in the save; `fork(name)` gives side streams. Offline catch-up steps the first 10 min finely, then coarse chunks (systems may give a `coarse` step), capped at 24 h. Systems are plain objects (`init/step/coarse/load`, plus `setup/tick` for empire-wide state in `game.shared`). Mods: a stat can be narrowed (`prod:saw`) and shares its family's cap; a stat with no published cap throws. Save v1 is `{ v, at, game }`; storage and slots are left to the Phone shell box. The audit also refuses wall-clock reads in the core. `src/core/setup.js` builds a new or loaded game for the UI, bots and tests.
- [x] **Economy.** The Gulch chain and the market with saturation, as data in `src/data/`.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.16-3. Goods, buildings and `towns/gulch.js` are data; `src/core/economy.js` makes goods (suppliers first, starved buildings flagged) and sells the surplus, keeping 10 s of each workshop's inputs; `src/core/market.js` prices with `rec / (rec + sellRate / depth)` (rec 1, recovery 60 s). A sale pays the average price over the saturation it causes, so splitting a sale gains nothing and a coarse offline chunk settles like fine steps. Milestones at levels 10, 25, 50, 100, 150, 200 each double output. Building ids `toolsmith` and `wagonworks` so no id is shared with a good. Every good sells by default (`town.sell[good] = false` holds it); sell modes come in M6. Costs and rates are first guesses; the M1 target box tunes them with the balance bot. The audit checks the chain rules; the first town is exempt from "the top good needs an import".

While a card is open, `src/render/select.js` draws a pulsing gold ring under the tower or pad (a gold square around a building's plot) and the tower's range as a circle on the ground (an empty pad shows the Crossbow's range), and the tower lifts a little. It clears when the card closes; the smoke test checks that, and `tools/shots.js` has a "selected" frame. In 2026.11.16-13 a soft column of light rising around the selected tower, pad or building joined it (the owner asked for a glow on the object), one additive mesh pulsing with the ring.

The board layout (plots, road, vault) is data in `towns/gulch.js`; the road is a crisp ribbon mesh with ruts. New models: clay pit, kiln, toolsmith, wagon works; buildings change model at levels 10, 25, 50, 100. The home view frames the plots that are built or can be built (at least three wide), so a phone sees the early yard large and the view widens as plots unlock; the Defense box must widen it to the whole road. Tall screens get a 50 degree lens and a steeper view. Tags over plots show levels (copper when affordable, a red edge when short of inputs); the card shows output with a tap-to-explain breakdown, inputs, needs, Build/Upgrade, and a sell or hold toggle for its good. Without WebGL the plots are listed as buttons. Verified headless: picking by tap, drag pans without tapping, buying through the card; pinch and twist are adapted from the old game and not yet tried on a real phone. `units.js` (people, foes, towers) is left for the Defense box.

  - The road, crossbow, cannon, frost lens and snake oil still, the vault, foes, waves and the war wagon.

  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.16-5. `src/core/defense.js` with `data/towers.js` and `data/foes.js`. Towers stand on 12 fixed stone-pad spots beside the road (data in `towns/gulch.js`); tap a pad for its card. Costs: crossbow timber (so the first tower comes in under a minute), cannon copper, frost lens bricks and copper, still bricks and tools; the town holds back the goods its next tower needs instead of selling them. Paths at level 5: Repeater or Longbow, Mortar or Grapeshot, Deep Freeze or Brittle Ice, Plague Cloud or Spreading Fever. Raiders: bandit, rider (wave 3+), armored brute (wave 6+), the war wagon every 10th wave; wave kinds mixed, rush, swarm, heavy. 30 s before wave 1, 12 s rests, and Call now pays for the time skipped. The vault holds 20; when it falls the town loses 10% of its coins and drops back two waves. Hits land at once in the core and the renderer draws the flight. Defense rests during offline catch-up until M4 brings background battles. The home view now frames the yard down to the far-side spots. A scratch greedy bot gave: first tower at 17 s, wave 10 at 6.5 min, no breach in waves 1-3, first breach at wave 15; the M1 target box tunes this with `tools/balance.js`. Pinch and twist are still untried on a real phone.
- [x] **Performance (owner feedback: "looks awesome but runs at like 0.5 frames per second" on the phone).**
  - Profile first, then fix properly: capped pixel ratio and a render scale, merged static geometry and shared materials, instancing for repeats, cheap or no shadows on Low, no idle lights, quality tiers with an automatic step-down when frames stay slow, and the fixed-step sim with a cap on steps per frame.
  - `tools/perf.js`: frame time in headless Edge at phone size with CPU throttling 4x, failing above 33 ms median, reporting draw calls and triangles. It joins the release checklist.
  - Note: this box and the next two jump the queue at the owner's request (2026-11-16), ahead of the Phone shell.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.16-7. Profile (`tools/perf.js`, phone 400x860 at pixel ratio 2, software WebGL so drawing costs like a weak GPU; CPU 4x): as shipped 371 ms a frame (2.7 fps) at 800x1720; without post-processing 72 ms; at pixel ratio 1 110 ms; without lamps 334 ms; without shadows 345 ms. Cause: the bloom, 4x-multisampled half-float target and grade passes at full phone resolution, then the pixel count; 182 draw calls with every rock and cactus its own draw (and shadow draw); 8 idle point lights in every pixel's shader; a 62k-triangle ground. Fixes: quality tiers in `src/render/quality.js` (low: ratio 1 at 85%, no shadows, no post; med, the phone default: ratio up to 1.25, antialiasing, 1024 shadows, no post; high, the desktop default: ratio up to 2, bloom and grade, 2048 soft shadows, lamps); lamps exist only on high in the title; static scenery and tower pads merged into one mesh per material (`mergeStatic` in `kit.js`); a coarser ground; "auto" steps down a tier when the median frame stays over 40 ms for 6 s, remembered under `cg-gfx`; the clock runs at most 8 steps a frame and drops the rest. After: med 23 ms median (43 fps), 93 draw calls, 50k triangles; low 16.5 ms. Headless Edge with a real GPU hid all of this (9.5 ms), so `perf.js` forces SwiftShader and waits for the GPU each frame; it also fails if the game had to step itself down. Not yet measured on a real phone. Foes are still a few meshes each (instancing them waits until waves get bigger). A Graphics setting (Auto, Low, Med, High) belongs in the Phone shell's Settings.
  - Follow-up (owner feedback after installing on Android: "super jittery", and no way to pause the waves). Notes: shipped 2026.11.16-10. Cause of the stutter: raiders were drawn straight from the 0.25 s simulation, so they jumped four times a second at any frame rate. Fix: render interpolation; the clock exposes `alpha` (how far into the next step it is) and `src/render/battle.js` draws each raider between its last two simulated places; shots aim where the target is drawn. Tower aim, projectiles, smoke and the camera already moved per frame. Effects now come from pools (meshes and fading materials made once, never cloned per hit), and the per-frame quaternion clone is gone. `tools/perf.js` gained a smoothness gate: it follows one raider on screen every frame and fails on a jump over 3 times the median or on a raider that stands still on most frames. Now: 0.77 px median, 0.84 px max, never still; the old drawing fails it (still on 88% of frames, 6.3 px jumps). Frame time 28.5 ms median. New on the HUD: Pause (stops the whole game; it also waits while the app is in the background) and Hold waves (the wave on the road finishes, the next waits, the town keeps working), each with an explainer card; the smoke test checks both at every size. Save v2 (migration: `waves.hold`, and the explainers seen). Not yet tried on the owner's phone.
  - Bug fixed in 2026.11.16-12 (owner screenshot on -10: solid orange blobs stacking up on the road). The pooled effects in -10 added a cannon's blast (and a still's cloud) to the effect list while that list was being filtered, so the new effect was dropped from the list: shown at full size and never faded or returned to the pool. Follow-up effects now join the list after the pass. The smoke test now fires cannons and a still through a wave, waits 2 s of quiet, and fails if any blast or cloud still shows; against the -10 code it finds 2 left behind, with the fix none. Slip: -12 went out with one `perf.js` run at 34.9 ms (over the 33 ms gate) because the release command piped the output and lost the exit code. Three re-runs gave 29.4, 29.6 and 30.0 ms, so the build was green, but it should not have been pushed on that run. Since then `perf.js` measures a near miss (within 15%) once more and keeps the better run, and release commands run with `set -o pipefail`.
  - Second follow-up (owner on 2026.11.16-9: "still very jittery"; -10 was not yet installed). Checked the other suspects. Camera easing was already frame-rate independent, and the frame time was already capped at 0.1 s after a hitch. Fixed in 2026.11.16-11: plot tags moved in whole pixels (now sub-pixel, so they glide with the camera); the HUD rewrote its text five times a second even when nothing changed (now only on change); per-frame garbage from smoke matrices, tag projections and the board's level check (now reused objects, and the check runs only when the simulation steps).

Old saves keep their towers (spot numbers are unchanged; the spots moved). - [x] **Tower reserve in auto-sell (owner feedback).**
  - Each good keeps a reserve that is never auto-sold: the next upgrade of every tower that uses it, the cheapest tower it could build next (a Mortar once pits are open), and the next path choice at level 4. Recomputed when towers, unlocks or prices change. Coins still gate the purchase.
  - Shown on the building and tower cards ("Keeping 12 copper for towers"), on the stock line, and a short tower card says which building to upgrade and about how long ("Copper 6 of 10, about 40 s"). A per-good "Keep for towers" switch, on by default. An explainer the first time it kicks in.
  - Tests: the bot can pay each next upgrade's materials when it has the coins; no workshop starves; balance.js wave 10 still 7-9 min and income within about 10%.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.16-17. `towerReserve` in `src/core/defense.js` now adds up, per good, the next upgrade of every tower that uses it (so every next upgrade can be paid at once, not just the biggest one as before), plus the cheapest open tower that uses that good and has a free pad, plus the same for a free mortar pit (a Mortar once the Mortar is open). Decision: "cheapest" is per good, so copper is kept for a Cannon and bricks for a Frost Lens, not only timber for the Crossbow. The workshops' 10 s input buffer stays on top. `town.reserveOff[good]` turns a good's reserve off (`setReserve`); save v4 adds it (empty, so every switch starts on). `shortfall(town, cost)` gives the good, the amount in stock, the building that makes it and about how long at today's rate after the workshops' own use. Shown: the building card has a row "Saving 8 timber for towers" with a "Keep for towers · On/Off" switch; the Goods list adds "12 saved for towers" to a selling good's stock line; a tower card short of a good says "Copper 2 of 9, ready in about 17 seconds. Upgrade the Smelter to make copper faster." (or "Build a Smelter to make copper."), and a pad's tower options say the same under their cost; an affordable upgrade says "Copper saved for this upgrade" (or "for this path"). Explainer "Saved for towers" once the town is holding a good back and has a tower, spotlighting that building. The market explainer no longer tells players to hold copper for a Cannon by hand. Tuned with the bot: GDD's first guess (wave 25, 100K) offered the charter at 26-37 min on a trial (wave 25 comes early), so the wave mark went up and the coin mark down; now offered at 40-44 min over 6 seeds, always from the coins. Each deed adds 25% to coins earned (sales and bounties) in every town, as the source "Deeds" (decision: 10% made the next charter barely faster; at 25% one deed plus Boomtown gets back to the old best wave in 33-40 min, about 85% of the first time, because early waves come on a clock the bot never calls early; the M1 target box may want the bot to use Call now). Signing resets the town in place (same object, so the screen and renderer keep their reference; its random stream and clock carry on), runs every system's init, clears perk picks and keeps the explainers seen. The game then saves and reloads into the new charter. Four perk pairs at 1, 2, 3 and 5 deeds: Boomtown or Fort Town, Big Guns (upgrades 30% cheaper) or Many Hands (new towers 40% cheaper, new narrowed stats `cost:towerUp` and `cost:towerNew`), Quick Study or Hard Bargain, Bounty Hunters or Stone Walls; each a named source ("Boomtown perk"). Decision: a pick stays until the next charter, which clears all picks. Save v6 (`game.shared.charter`). A toast at the offer. Explainers "Your charter" and "Pick a perk"; the audit requires `system:charter`, `perks` and `deeds`. - [x] **Strategy bots (more than one way to win).**
  - `tools/balance.js` runs named strategies over several seeds: active (the current bot), min-max (few towers pushed to high levels and paths), swarm (many cheap towers at low levels), economy-first (buildings first, the fewest towers that hold) and defense-first.
  - Each reaches wave 10 and wave 20 within about 25% of the best strategy's time, and none breaches in waves 1-3. Per strategy it reports what it bought and the damage by tower kind.
  - Tune until min-max and swarm both work (swarm needs level-1 towers that are worth their price, min-max needs upgrades that scale).
  - Notes: done 2026-11-17 (tools only, released with the next game change). `STRATEGIES` in `tools/balance.js`: each strategy is a tower count and a level it keeps for the coming wave, plus how it picks. active is the old bot, unchanged. minmax: at most 6 towers (1 more every 5 waves), level 2 + wave/2, upgrades before builds. swarm: 2 + wave/1.5 towers (all 12 pads by wave 15), level 1 + wave/4, always raising the lowest tower. economy: the town first (research, foremen, buildings) while no raider got through in the last two waves, 1 + wave/3.5 towers at level 1 + wave/3.3, and half a level more for good after each wave that leaks. defense: 1 + wave/2.5 towers at level 1.5 + wave/2.8, towers before the town. Decision: a "share of coins on towers" rule was tried first and breached everyone at wave 13, so each strategy keeps a schedule. `strategies()` runs each over the seeds for 40 min and fails a median wave 10 or wave 20 more than 25% behind the best, or any breach in waves 1-3; this is now part of `node tools/balance.js` and so a release gate. Over 6 seeds (median): active wave 10 at 8.5 min and wave 20 at 23.8 min, minmax 8.1 / 25.0, swarm 8.0 / 23.2, economy 8.8 / 22.2, defense 7.3 / 21.2 (the best on both, since a strong defense ends waves sooner); coins at 30 min 30-35K for all. A thin defense is slower to wave 10 because each wave takes longer to die, so economy sits closest to the line (8.8 of 9.1 min). Every strategy still buys almost only Crossbows (94-100% of damage); that is the target box's variety work. `test/strategies.test.js` checks the five exist, all hold waves 1-3, and min-max stays few and high while swarm stays many and even.

Changes: the Cannon costs 100 coins + 6 copper ore (dmg 14, was 16), the Still 100 + 4 ore + 3 planks, the Frost Lens 100 + 4 planks + 3 clay (was 130), the Smelter 500 coins (was 900) and the Kiln 600 (was 1000), so four kinds can be built before minute 7. The coin deed is at 60K (was 52K; more coins came early). Bot (`tools/balance.js`): direct hits lose the coming waves' armor share (`armorShare`, poison ignores it); a raider carries one dose of poison and one chill, so a Still's poison and a Frost Lens's slow are shared with other towers of its kind that reach the same road; a new tower of a kind already standing is worth a little less (`MIX` 0.15, a careful player keeps a mix; without it a 10-coin change swung the Still from never bought to over half the damage); it saves for a new tower worth 1.4 times the best affordable buy when that tower's goods are in the yard and it is at most 100 coins away. ### M1b. Outpost 1, the start of the game (owner request, 2026-11-17)
Decisions (written when the milestone was added): there is only one outpost before Copper Gulch (the owner's correction); each later town opens one more (Pine Hollow with Iron Ridge, Sandy Bend with Silver Falls, Tar Seep with Thunder Mesa, Glowrock Camp with Radium Springs, none with the Capital). Pine Hollow makes timber and clay, as the owner suggested, so the Gulch makes more bricks for the Ridge's boilers. Outposts have no mortar pits. The Gulch's own targets (wave 10, first charter) count from when it opens. Iron Ridge moves from 45-70 to 55-80 min of play to make room for Dry Creek; the hour-scale targets from the Capital on stay as they are, since the outposts' extra raw goods make up the time. M1b comes after the M1 target as asked, so the Town layer and the strategy bots come first.
- [x] **Outposts in the core.**
  - Town data gets `kind: 'outpost'`. Dry Creek Outpost (`src/data/towns/drycreek.js`): a Mining Camp (copper ore) and a Stone Camp (stone, a new raw good with its price and market depth), the Crossbow only, paid with coins plus stone.
  - Its board: the road enters through a dry wash, loops once around the camp and leaves; about 6 stone pads; the stockpile is its vault, where the loop closes. No mortar pits.
  - Waves 1 to 10 with a bandit wagon (a smaller boss) at 10. Winning wave 10 marks it won and opens Copper Gulch (`game.addTown('gulch')`, and a 'town:open' event).
  - After it is won, its raids stay at wave-10 strength, one every 2 minutes or so, and its towers fight them in the background. A breach there takes part of its stockpile and never drops its waves back.
  - Its goods sell on the shared market. A town in play draws the raw goods its workshops are short of from the outposts first, up to the 10-second buffer, then the rest sells.
  - The audit: outposts are exempt from mortar pits and from the chain rules on imports; `mapRules` checks an outpost's loop and entrance. Every later town names its outpost in data (GDD.md, Outposts) and the audit fails a town past the Gulch without one.
  - A save migration (the next save version): a save already in Copper Gulch gets Dry Creek as won and running (both buildings at level 5, two Crossbows). A new game starts in Dry Creek.
  - Tests: the outpost's chain, Crossbow only, the wave-10 win opening the Gulch, the light background raids, the Gulch drawing ore from Dry Creek before the market, the save migration.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-4. `src/data/towns/drycreek.js` (`kind: 'outpost'`, `towers: ['crossbow']`, `boss: 'bandits'`, `goal: 10`, `opens: 'gulch'`, `raid: 120`, `waves: { speed: .9 }`) and `src/core/outpost.js` (a seventh system: `winOutpost`, `grantOutpost`). Data: stone (1.2 coins, depth 3), the Stone Camp `quarry` (0.8 a second, 10 coins, built at the start) and the Mining Camp `dmine` (copper ore, 0.6 a second, 30 coins, opens at Stone Camp level 2), the Crossbow at 20 coins + 6 stone there, and the bandit wagon (10 health, half the war wagon's). Board: the road comes down the wash, loops clockwise round the two camps and ends at the stockpile beside where the loop began; 6 pads (between the camps, inside the two bottom corners, outside the bottom, west and east), no pits. The `wash` entrance model (a rock ridge with a gap, a rock arch where raiders wait, a broken windmill) is in `entrances.js` now, because the audit needs a model for every entrance; box 2 looks at it on screen. Defense: `wavesOf(town)` merges a town's wave overrides, `bossWave` (a won outpost never brings its boss again), `towerIn` (an outpost's kinds; `towerOpen` and the tower card follow it). A won outpost keeps its wave at 10, raids every 120 s (the wave's age counts), and a breach there takes 25% of each good (`RAID_LOSS`) and keeps its coins and waves. Decision: while an outpost is out of play its sale coins go to the town in play (`sell(..., payee)`, at the payee's prices), and it keeps only its bounties, so the Gulch feels it at once (written in GDD.md); before that the outpost sells for itself. The draw (`feed` in `economy.js`): the town in play takes what its workshops are short of, up to their 10-second buffer, from the outpost's surplus above its own tower reserve, recorded in `town.drawn` with a one-time `outpost:draw` event. Outposts have no charter (no offer, `signCharter` pays 0). `game.go(id)` switches the town in play (`town:go`). Save v8: a save with the Gulch and no Dry Creek gets `shared.grant`, and `loadGame` opens Dry Creek won and running (both camps at level 5, two Crossbows on pads 0 and 1). `newGame(seed)` now starts in Dry Creek; `newGame(seed, 'gulch')` is the Gulch alone, which the tests and `tools/balance.js` use until the bot box. Decision: until box 2 puts the outpost on screen, the title's new game still starts in the Gulch alone (`main.js`), and old saves get Dry Creek in the background. Audit: outposts are exempt from the one-maker rule, the research count and pits; an outpost's road must pass its stockpile at least 12 units before it ends there (`LOOP`); outposts make raw goods only; every town after the first names its outpost (`town.outpost`). `src/render/entrance.js` reads `bossWave`. Explainer "Dry Creek Outpost" (covers `system:outpost`) once the town in play has drawn from it, spotlight on the coins. Tests: `test/outpost.test.js` (10: the start, the chain, Crossbow only and no pits, the loop rule, the bandit wagon and the win opening the Gulch, light raids every 2 min with no boss, the breach taking goods, the draw and the payee, selling for itself in play, no charter, save v8), and the chain tests leave outposts out of the one-maker rule. A scratch bot (build to 1 + wave/2 Crossbows at 1 + wave/3 levels, then the cheaper camp) won Dry Creek in 7.8-11.2 min with the first tower at 8 s over 4 seeds, with 0-1 breach; box 3 tunes it. Note for box 2: the Cannon and Still explainers fire on `made.ore`, which Dry Creek makes, so they need a check for the town's towers; the breach toast and the boss toast name the Gulch's war wagon and the wave drop; `bandits`, `quarry` and `dmine` need models. - [x] **The outpost on screen, and switching towns.**
  - Models: the Mining Camp, the Stone Camp, the stockpile, the loop road and the dry-wash entrance with a broken windmill (merged geometry, the same perf budget).

  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-5. A new game now starts in Dry Creek (`main.js`; test tools pass `?start=gulch` to get the Gulch alone, which the old smoke, perf and shots runs use). Models in `src/assets/models/gulch.js`: the Stone Camp (`quarry`: a stepped rock face, a boom crane whose pulley turns while it works, rows of cut blocks, a crew tent), the Mining Camp (`dmine`: a timbered adit in a red bank, a cart on short rails, an ore heap, a rocker box, a tent) and `makeStockpile()` (crates, barrels and sacks under a tarp in a rail fence); `town.def.store: 'stockpile'` picks it on the board and names it in the HUD and toasts. The road now runs on into the stockpile (`[1.6, .5]` added), so its end no longer stops short of it. The `wash` entrance from box 1 is unchanged. Switching towns: `game.go(id)` plus `CG.go` in `main.js`, which takes down the board, HUD and cards (`play.destroy()`, `coach.destroy()`, `townView.stop(true)` which also lets the GPU context go) and builds them again for the other town on a fresh canvas. Decision: in place, not a page reload, so it is quick; 24 switches in a row all drew with one canvas and no warnings. The town chip (`#townChip`): once a second town is open, the town name at the top becomes a row of buttons, one per open town (Dry Creek, Copper Gulch, `town.def.short`), the one in play gold. Decision (written in GDD.md, Outposts): a town out of play that is not an outpost waits for the player. The wave on its road finishes, but the clock to its next wave stops (`waitsAway` in `defense.js`), so nothing breaks while the player is away and arriving leaves the time it showed. A won outpost keeps its raids. The win card is an explainer with a second button (`go`, `button` in the explainer data; the coach shows Stay here and Go to Copper Gulch). Explainers can name their `town`: Dry Creek has its welcome, the Stone Camp, the stockpile, the Mining Camp, the bandit wagon and the win card. The Gulch's welcome, vault and war wagon cards are Gulch only. The Cannon, Frost Lens and Still cards wait until the town can build them. In the Gulch there are new cards for the town buttons and for Dry Creek working on its own, plus "Ore from Dry Creek" on the Smelter when it first draws ore. The waves card no longer says vault. Perks are hidden in an outpost. The boss and breach toasts name the town's own boss and its vault or stockpile, and a won outpost's breach says it took a quarter of the goods. Audit: the required features now include a welcome for every town, every boss, the stockpile, the town buttons, the outpost win and the draw. Tests: `test/towns.test.js` (go both ways and the save, the clock stopping away and the goods still coming, a won outpost keeping its raids, the first card in each kind of game, cards for one town never showing in another, the win card, the Gulch's chip and outpost cards). The coach run now checks the Dry Creek welcome and the Stone Camp spotlight. Shots: `outpost`, `outpost-entry`, `outpost-fight`, `won` and `chip` at every size. `perf.js --town drycreek` measures the outpost. The screenshot-only review flagged 10 lines. Fixed: the wave countdown cut off on a phone (tighter spacing, and now a smoke check) and the win card wording. Left as they are, kept by earlier reviews: the title lines, "Your town kept...", the Selling switch, copper beside copper ore, Busy research buttons and the foreman line. The raid and Dry Creek names were also left (the chip uses the short name once two towns are open). Not yet tried on a real phone. Dry Creek's pacing is box 3.

The Gulch's own clock starts when it opens, so its targets count from then. The main report and the strategy check now play this whole start: a Dry Creek table (first tower, won, breaches, Crossbows bought, camp levels), the Gulch table (with the coins Dry Creek paid in, and Dry Creek's breaches after), and per strategy the time to win Dry Creek and its Crossbows and their level, gated within 25% like waves 10 and 20, plus no breach in Dry Creek's waves 1-3. First readings: Dry Creek won at 7.3-8.3 min (waves came every 40 s), and Dry Creek paid the Gulch about 6.5 coins a second, a third of its early income, so the first charter came at 28-30 min. Changes: Dry Creek's waves start at 30 s and come at least 65 s apart (`waves: { first: 30, pace: 65 }`); coins an outpost's sales pay in are counted in `town.fromOutposts` (save v9) and left out of the deed coin count (`town.ownEarned`, also the Perks sheet's Coins earned), and the deed marks are now wave 33 and 85K coins (were 30 and 60K). Decision: a deed measures the town's own work, and the outpost's coins still pay for things and show in the income. With only that change the offer came at 31.5-34.8 min; 70-80K still had seeds under 35 min, and at wave 30 one seed was offered at 33.6 min from its wave. Decision: the bots stay in the Gulch once it opens (a player would visit Dry Creek now and then). `test/strategies.test.js` runs a campaign, and `test/towns.test.js` checks the outpost coins, the deed rule, save v9 and Dry Creek's wave pace.

Dry Creek after the switch: 1-3 breaches in the first 90 min, stockpile raids only. ### M2. Onboarding, then Evaluation 1
Decision (2026-11-17): Onboarding is split into three boxes so each ships on its own.
- [x] **Buttons that appear when useful, and alerts.**
  - The rules for what the play screen shows live in one pure module (`src/core/reveal.js`), so the newcomer bot uses only what a player can see. Pause, Hold waves and Menu are always there (owner rule). Goods appears once the town makes two goods (or once its explainer was seen), Research when research opens, Perks at the first charter offer, the town buttons when a second town opens.
  - Dots: Research when nothing is running and an item can be paid, Perks when a perk waits or the charter would pay more deeds than last time you looked, Goods when a good is new to the list, a town button when that town was raided while you were away, Menu when an update is ready.
  - Toasts wait their turn: one at a time, the same line never twice in a row.
  - The Perks sheet wording left from M1b.

Goods comes out once the town's Goods list has two goods (in Dry Creek when the Mining Camp is built, in a fresh Gulch with the Sawmill), or once its explainer was seen, so a player arriving in the Gulch keeps it. Decision: Menu stays out from the start (Graphics and saves matter on a slow phone), as do Pause and Hold waves (owner rule). Dots: Goods for a good new to its list (cleared by opening Goods), Research while nothing runs and an item can be paid, Perks while a perk waits or the charter would pay more deeds than when the sheet was last opened, a town button when that town was breached since your last look, Menu while the Update ready bar shows. The dot is a small square that blinks red and gold. The Goods explainer now waits for the Goods button; a new "Red dots" explainer shows at the first dot (the audit requires `dots`). Toasts wait their turn: one at a time, at most three waiting, a line already up or waiting is dropped, and a toast with others behind it stays 2.5 s. Perks sheet: "Your best wave is 34. That gives 1 deed. Next deed at wave 38.", a decided pair is headed "You picked one" with the other side dimmed, and "cost 30% less". Tests: `test/reveal.test.js` (8). Smoke checks Pause, Hold waves and Menu always out, Goods hidden with one good, out with a dot with two, the dot gone once opened, and toasts one at a time; shots has a `dots` frame. The screenshot-only review flagged 8 lines and the dots; fixed the deed lines (units, plain grammar), the dimmed perk with no reason, the What's new line, and the dots (now round and red, pulsing in size, matching the cards). Left as they are, kept by earlier reviews: Holding, Busy, the title lines, and Dry Creek as the button name. Left for the tap-to-explain box: "1.80K" and the research wait wrapping on a phone. Decision: a goal counts the first time it is true, in any order, and a goal that can't be done yet is passed over (`ready`), so a player who runs ahead is never stuck on an old goal; once Dry Creek is won only Go to Copper Gulch is left there. Done goals and the towns visited are in `game.shared.goals`. Save v10: a game already in the Gulch has Dry Creek's goals done, and one past wave 10 or after a charter has the strip off. Tapping the strip flashes the frame. Decision: the frame follows the camera from projected points each frame and reads the page only five times a second, so it adds no layout per frame. The Dry Creek welcome card now spotlights the strip and covers `system:goals`. `tools/balance.js` reports when each goal is done and fails a goal the careful bot has not done within 40 minutes of a new game: all done by 20.5-24.6 min (Dry Creek's by 11.0-11.3, the Mortar last). Income is a sum: `Town.earn` now files every coin under a key (`sale:<good>`, `from:<outpost>`, `bounty`, `call`) in `town.tally`, which is not saved (no new save version), and the Income sheet shows the coins a second from each over the same 5 seconds as the number at the top (the parts add up to it), then the bonuses already in them (Deeds, price and bounty bonuses). On screen (`src/ui/explain.js`): tap the coins for Income; tap the wave number and green bar (now one button) for the Vault sheet (full strength and what adds to it, hits left, and how many hits each raider takes); on a tower card Damage, Shots and Range are buttons with a small arrow and open their breakdown under the list, one at a time (Range stays in words, Short to Long); the building card's Makes line uses the same box; in Goods, tapping a good shows its price parts ending in You get, and hides the "sells for" text while open. - [x] **Bots and checks.**
  - `tools/firstplay.js`, the newcomer bot: it waits 3-8 s before acting, makes a wrong buy 30% of the time, and uses only the tabs it can see.
  - The full `tools/audit.js` (the list is in GDD.md and in Notes).
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-10. `tools/firstplay.js` plays a new game straight on the core for 60 min: after each tap it waits 3-8 s (its own seeded stream), uses only the side buttons `reveal.js` puts out (Research once its button is out, Perks to pick a side at random), follows the goal strip 70% of the time when it can pay for what the goal asks (it taps Copper Gulch at the top as soon as it is open), and otherwise buys towers when it sees trouble (no tower, a raider reached the vault in this wave or the last, the vault bar under 70%, or fewer than one tower per 4 waves) and the best-paying building when it does not; 30% of its buys are any other thing it can pay for, on any pad, with any path. It never saves up, calls a wave, hires a foreman or signs a charter. "Stuck" is the longest stretch in the first hour with nothing it can pay for and nothing new on screen (a goal done, a tower kind, a town, a button). First readings, with the newcomer buying towers on the active bot's wave curve: Dry Creek won at 11.0-11.3 min and Gulch wave 10 at 7.5-7.7 min, the same as the careful player, because waves come on a clock and the early waves hold even with sloppy buys. Making it react only to what it sees changed little (11.0 and 8.0 min). Decision: the newcomer limits become upper ones, Dry Creek within 20 min and Gulch wave 10 within 14 min after it opens (GDD.md, Pacing targets, and the M2 target below). A newcomer who keeps the expert's pace in the tutorial is good, and making early waves hard enough to slow it would break "no breach in waves 1-3". Left for the fresh-eyes review: skill barely matters before wave 20 (the newcomer's first Gulch breach is at wave 19-26, the careful player's at 23-27). Results (6 seeds): first tower 8 s, Dry Creek won 10.9-11.3 min, Gulch wave 10 7.7-8.1 min after it opens, longest stuck 0.8-1.3 min, 254-299 buys of which 47-64 wrong and 20-30 from the goal strip, Research and Goods used on every seed. The full audit adds three rules: `nameRules` (no two goods, buildings, towns or town buttons, tower kinds, paths, raiders, research items or perks share a name), `capRules` (every perk, research item and `mods.set/factor/value/explain` in the core names a stat with a published cap) and `modelRules` (every building's model recipe exists, and every tower kind and raider has its own model, read from the model files' text). The model rule found that Dry Creek's boss, the bandit wagon, had no model and drew as a big raider. It now has one (`FOE.bandits` in `units.js`: a patched canvas wagon, a driver in a red bandana, one horse, wheels that turn), and shots has a `bandits` frame. GDD.md says what the audit checks under Names, Multipliers and Models. Tests: `test/firstplay.test.js` (7: slow taps, wrong buys and goal taps and the win, buttons, the misses, and the three audit rules catching a clash, an uncapped stat and a missing model). Checks: 157 tests, audit ok, balance unchanged (Dry Creek won 11.0-11.3 min, first tower 11 s; Gulch wave 10 about 8 min after it opens, charter offered 37.4-41.8 min, variety ok, all strategies within 25%), firstplay ok, smoke all ok, perf Gulch 30.2 ms median (104 draw calls, 58k triangles, 0.78 px raider step), Dry Creek 28 ms (85 draw calls, 51k triangles), update and install tests ok. The screenshot-only review flagged 7 items. Fixed: the What's new line (shorter) and a goal frame on the board covering the goal strip's words (the frame now hides while its target sits under the strip, measured with the other reads five times a second). Left for the fresh-eyes box below: goal lines that name a building or a Crossbow with no label on screen ("Tap the Stone Camp" points at its Lv tag), Lv tags crowding the frame, the title's second line repeating the tagline, "Dry Creek Outpost" at the top beside "Dry Creek" on the town buttons, "Mixed raid", and the wave line saying "in 120s" (the won card frame fakes a win, so its stale goal is the shot, not the game). Not yet tried on a real phone.
