- [x] **Skill pays (fresh-eyes issue 1).** The newcomer bot wins Dry Creek and reaches Gulch wave 10 at the careful player's times, because waves come on a clock (the 40 s pace in the Gulch, 65 s in Dry Creek) and no bot calls a wave early. Make a strong defense able to go faster: a bot that calls the next wave when its towers are clearly ahead, Call now's bonus worth it, and the wave pace easing for a town that clears waves fast. Put something new into Dry Creek's stretch from about minute 3 to 9 (the path choice earlier, or a goal for a clean wave). Target: the active bot beats the newcomer by at least 20% on Dry Creek and on Gulch wave 10, the newcomer still wins Dry Creek within 20 min, and the pacing table in GDD.md is rewritten for a player who calls waves (decide the new active numbers and write them down).
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-12. Call now is the skill. Waves wait longer for a player who lets them come (`WAVE.pace` 58 s, was 40; Dry Creek 80 s, was 65; the Gulch's first wave at 35 s, was 25), and Call now opens once the wait is down to `CALL_OPEN` (52%) of the pace (`canCall` in `src/core/defense.js`; the wait before wave 1 can always be cut). On screen the button shows in every break and is gray until then. `town.flags.called` counts calls (no new save version). The bots: every strategy in `tools/balance.js` calls the next wave when the last one was over within `CALL_AGE` (45%) of the pace, nothing got through in the last two waves and the vault is above 60%, and never calls a boss wave early (`--no-call` turns it off). Decision: without the cap, defense-first won Dry Creek at 7.0 min against 9.1 for the others (the strategy check failed); with Call now opening at half the wait, a strong defense goes up to about twice as fast as the clock and the strategies stay within 21%. Decision: no automatic easing of the pace for a town that clears waves fast, because it sped the newcomer up as much as the careful player (early waves die fast for both); skill shows in when you call. Something new in Dry Creek's middle stretch: a goal "Tap Call now for bonus coins and an early wave" (`dc-call`, after 3 Crossbows and wave 2, spotlight on Call now), done at about 1-2 min after it shows; the newcomer taps it when the strip asks (`goalTap`). The careful bot now also follows a goal that asks for a new tower kind once the first boss is down (`GOAL_PICK`; an early Cannon on seed 1 cost two calls). New pacing (GDD.md, Pacing targets): Dry Creek won in 8-12 min active (was 10-15), wave 10 in the Gulch still 7-9 min, and the newcomer at least 20% slower on both; `tools/firstplay.js` gates that on medians (`skillGap`). Results (6 seeds): active Dry Creek 8.4-10.7 min (median 9.3, 4-7 calls), Gulch wave 10 8.0-8.4 min (4-5 calls by then), first tower 11 s and 4 s, no breach in waves 1-3, charter offered 39.6-42.5 min, variety ok (leaders Still, Frost Lens, Mortar, Cannon). Newcomer: Dry Creek 12.5-13.0 min (median 12.7, 37% slower), Gulch wave 10 10.3-10.8 min (median 10.5, 28% slower), longest stuck 1.0-1.8 min. Strategies (median, Dry Creek / wave 10 / wave 20): active 9.3 / 8.2 / 17.2, minmax 9.3 / 8.7 / 18.7, swarm 8.6 / 7.8 / 20.4, economy 10.0 / 8.6 / 18.6, defense 8.6 / 7.2 / 17.0, all within 25%. Tests: `test/skill.test.js` (the call count and save, the goal and its spotlight, the newcomer's call only in a break), the pace and Call now gate in `test/defense.test.js`, Dry Creek's pace, the campaign range. Smoke checks Call now gray with a long wait and working once half is gone; shots has `goal-call`. Checks: 161 tests, audit ok, balance ok, firstplay ok, smoke all ok (one run under heavy CPU load from the bots missed the phone reserve check because timber came back within 400 ms; it passed alone), perf 32 ms median (104 draw calls, 58k triangles, 0.79 px raider step), update and install tests ok. The screenshot-only review flagged 10 lines; fixed the three What's new lines (no narrator, one fact each), the goal (now names the bonus coins) and "wave" in the save slot line (now "Wave" like the top bar). Left as they are, kept by earlier reviews: "Hold waves", "/s" on income, and the Lumber Camp and Crossbow goal lines; "Checking.." is the title's update check cut short by the screenshot. Not yet tried on a real phone.
- [x] **Tower balance and the charter's payoff (fresh-eyes issues 2 and 3).** The Still leads nearly every Gulch wave from 4 on and does 31-40% of the damage; the Crossbow is bought most and does 5%; the Mortar does 4-6% and never leads. Make the Still strong against armor and weak against the rest (or its poison not stack), give the Mortar a job only it does (packed groups and the boss), and keep the Crossbow worth upgrading late. Gate in `tools/balance.js`: no kind over about 35% of the damage for any strategy, and every kind leads some waves for at least one strategy. Charter: a signed town gets back to its old best wave in half the first time or less (now 31-43 min, about 85%), and something new opens between minute 23 and the first charter offer.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-13. Each tower kind now has a job, on its card as "Best against" (`TOWERS.*.best` and `bestText`, `bestFactor` in `src/core/defense.js`): the Crossbow hits Riders twice as hard; the Still's poison is 6.5 a second on unarmored raiders (was 10 on all) and 2.2 times that on armored ones, and grows 15% a level (`PER_LEVEL.poison`, a hit grows 30%), so one maxed Still can't carry a town; the Mortar hits the wagons 2.5 times as hard, with a wider blast (1.4, was 1.3) and damage 36 (was 34). The Cannon (13, was 14) and the Frost Lens (4, was 5) came down a little as the Still stopped carrying. The careful bots now value a tower over the next 5 waves (`HORIZON`, so the boss is seen coming) and keep more of a mix (`MIX` .3, was .15); without the look-ahead min-max stacked Longbows and stalled at the wave 20 boss. New gates in `tools/balance.js`: `towerBalance` (no kind over 36% of the damage for any strategy, every kind leads a wave for at least one) and `charterPayoff`. Charter: a signed town keeps its old best wave as `town.record` (save v11, migrated from the last charter), and on old ground its towers hit 3 times harder (the named source "Waves you beat before", so the tower card's Damage breakdown shows it) and Call now works at once (`oldGround`, `OLD_GROUND`). Decision: the bots showed that more income alone can't halve the climb back (deeds at +100% income: 45-81%; keeping research: 49-98%; +50% tower damage per deed on top: 44-69%), because the wall near wave 25-30 is a damage wall and the waves come on a clock; old ground is aimed at exactly the waves already beaten and leaves new ground as hard as before. The bot also had a bug: after signing it remembered its last leak at wave 29 and never called a wave until it passed it. Something new before the charter: Powder Kegs research (Cannon and Mortar blasts 20% wider a level, 3 levels, 2500 coins) shows up at best wave 26 with a toast (`opens` on a research item, `research:new`), at minute 24.5-36.4. Explainers "Best against" (at the first Brute wave with two kinds built) and "Old ground" (on Call now); the audit requires both. Tests: `test/jobs.test.js` (8). Shots: `why-old` and `kegs`; smoke checks the Best against line. Checks: 169 tests, audit ok, balance ok (Dry Creek won 8.4-10.7 min, first tower 11 s; Gulch wave 10 at 8.2-8.8 min, first tower 4 s, no breach in waves 1-3, charter offered 39.9-45.0 min; damage share, median over 6 seeds: active cannon 28%, frost 28%, still 26%, mortar 10%, crossbow 8%; minmax still 34%, frost 31%, crossbow 21%; swarm mortar 34%, cannon 28%, crossbow 17%; economy cannon 29%, still 25%, frost 25%; defense frost 33%, cannon 28%, still 25%; every kind leads waves (Crossbow and Mortar in swarm and minmax); strategies within 25% (wave 10 7.1-8.6 min, wave 20 17.1-19.1 min); back to the old best wave in 37-61% of the first climb, median 48%), firstplay ok (newcomer Dry Creek 12.5-13.0 min, Gulch wave 10 10.3-10.6 min, longest stuck 1.1-1.8 min), smoke all ok, perf 32.5 ms median (112 draw calls, 58k triangles, 0.79 px raider step). The screenshot-only review flagged 12 lines. Fixed: "Old ground" on the Damage breakdown (a made-up term) is now "Waves you beat before", the explainer too; "blasts reach 20% wider" is "blasts are 20% wider"; Siege Mortar reads "Shoots farther. The blast is bigger." Left as they are, kept by earlier reviews: "kept copper ore for this upgrade", copper beside copper ore (two goods), "Keep 8 for towers", the foreman line, "/s", the Perks lines, the short-goods warning, Hold waves, a toast over a sheet, and "Checking.." (the title cut short by the shot). Not yet tried on a real phone.
- [x] **Camera framing in landscape and on desktop (fresh-eyes issues 4 and 8).** The near cliff covers the west pass in landscape and on desktop (a Mortar there hides behind it); stone pads are about 20 px in landscape (the touch rule is 30 px); a card on the right covers the selected tower; the top row's tags run into the goal strip. Fit the home frame to the space the top bar and a card leave free, cut or fade scenery that hides the road or a tower, slide the camera so the selected thing stays in the free part while its card is open, and make the first Gulch goal say to wait when the first tower can't be paid yet. Smoke checks the pad size and that the selected tower stays on screen at every size.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-14. The home view is solved, not guessed (`CameraRig.fit` in `src/render/camera.js`): it projects the town frame's corners (at the ground and 1.2 up) and finds the closest distance where they fit into the part of the screen under the top bar, slides the frame's middle to the middle of that part, then moves in until the smallest stone pad is `MIN_PAD` (32) px across, repeating so centering does not undo it. The top bar's lower edge and the open card's box come from `play.js` five times a second (`viewApi.setView`), which it already reads for the goal frame; the frame loop reads nothing new. Wide screens look from lower (pitch .72, was .8) so the town's depth takes less height, and phones from higher (1.08, was .95) so far pads shrink less. Decision: on a narrow phone and a short landscape the 32 px floor wins over fitting the whole town (its ends are a pan away), because the touch rule is 30 px; the drawn pad is bigger too (radius .5, was .42; the tap area is unchanged). Decision: on a short landscape screen (`max-height: 500px`) the town name, wave and Pause share one row and the goal goes under them (top bar 99 px, was about 165). Results (home view, smallest pad, pads fully on screen of 16): phone 400x860 32 px, 11; 360x740 32 px, 8; landscape 860x400 32 px, 11 (was about 25 px); tablet 42 px, 15; desktop 50 px, 15 (Dry Creek 32-59 px, all 6). The canyon: an entrance knows which way it runs (mouth to portal) and its walls sink to 30% of their height (`CUT`, eased) when the camera looks across or into it, so in landscape and on desktop the west pass and the pit beside the canyon show. Selection: while a card covers the selected pad, pit or building (or it is off screen), `rig.keep` slides the camera's target so it sits in the nearest free part (above the card, or beside it), and closing the card puts the target back where it was, unless the player moved the map in between (`gestures` counts real moves; a tap does not). A fix found on the way: each refit used the already-slid home point as the frame's middle, so the view drifted when the goal strip changed height; the frame's middle is now kept apart (`fx`, `fz`) and the pan limit is around it. The first goal in each town says what to wait for while a Crossbow can't be paid ("Wait for 5 more coins and 3 more timber, then tap a stone pad", counting down; `wait` on a goal, `goalText` in `src/core/goals.js`; the strip rechecks it five times a second). Smoke checks at every size: the smallest pad at home is 30 px or more, a Crossbow on pad 12 and a far mortar pit each sit on screen, below the top bar and clear of their open card, and the camera is back within 0.15 of home after the card closes; the outpost run accepts the wait line and checks it turns into the tap line once paid. `tools/frame-shots.js` takes the home, card, pit and building-card frames at every size in about a minute. Tests: `test/goals.test.js` (the wait line in both towns and its switch). Checks: 170 tests, audit ok, balance ok (no simulation change: Dry Creek won 8.4-10.7 min, first tower 11 s; Gulch wave 10 at 8.2-8.8 min, first tower 4 s, no breach in waves 1-3, charter offered 39.9-45.0 min, back to the old best wave in 37-61% of the first climb, variety ok, all strategies within 25%), firstplay ok (newcomer Dry Creek 12.5-13.0 min, Gulch wave 10 10.3-10.6 min, longest stuck 1.1-1.8 min), smoke all ok, perf 31.8-32.9 ms median (64-110 draw calls, 55-58k triangles, about 1 px raider step), update and install tests ok. The screenshot-only review flagged 14 lines and 5 layout things. Fixed: the wait line named the full price, and a player can't see their timber or stone, so it now says only what is missing and counts down ("Wait for 5 more coins and 3 more timber, then tap a stone pad"; the What's new line too). Left as they are, kept by earlier reviews: "Tap Build to build the Sawmill" (the tag reads "Sawmill · Build" while the goal points at it; the shots have a card open), "kept copper ore for this upgrade", copper beside copper ore, "Keep 8 for towers", the foreman line, "Waves you beat before", the Perks lines, "Med", "/s", "Checking.." (the title cut short by the shot) and the stale goal under the win card (the shot fakes the win); a toast over a sheet or tags for a few seconds; a selected Mortar's wide ring running off the screen edge (the Mortar itself sits clear of the card). Left as they are: the title in landscape (the Continue box over the town, the tagline over a building) and top-row building tags close under the side buttons in landscape when four buttons are out. Not yet tried on a real phone.

The newcomer wins Dry Creek at 12.5-13.0 min (median 12.7, the careful player 9.3) and reaches Gulch wave 10 at 10.3-10.6 min after it opens (median 10.5, the careful player 8.2), with its longest stretch of nothing to buy and nothing new 1.1-1.8 min in the first hour; it makes 263-279 buys, 42-59 of them wrong, 24-37 from the goal strip, and uses Goods and Research on every seed. The audit is clean (every rule in GDD.md, including names, caps and models). M2 is done.

### M3. Iron Ridge and the empire map
Decision (2026-11-17): the Iron Ridge box is split in two so each ships on its own. Iron Ridge and Pine Hollow come in together, because the audit needs a model, an entrance and a welcome card for every town in the data, and the Ridge must name its outpost. The bots that pace the Ridge are the next box.
- [x] **Iron Ridge and Pine Hollow in the game.** Data, models, the armored train, routes, founding the Ridge from the Gulch, and switching between towns. It opens Outpost 2, Pine Hollow (timber and clay, GDD.md Outposts).
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-15. Data: `src/data/towns/ridge.js` (8 workshops in three rows between four passes of a line that switches back across the town, 12 pads, 4 mortar pits, the `tunnel` entrance, the armored train as boss, research opens with the Blast Furnace) and `src/data/towns/pinehollow.js` (Logging Camp and Clay Bank, the Crossbow and the Cannon paid with timber, waves 1-15 at least 75 s apart with the bandit wagon at 10 and 15 through the new `waves.bosses`, the `chute` entrance). Goods: iron ore, coal, pig iron, steel, rails, boilers, locomotives; stone now belongs to the Ridge (Dry Creek cuts it too, as it digs copper ore). Every step is 1.38-1.42 times its inputs and balanced at equal levels (the audit). Seven Ridge research items, Bessemer Converter the trade-off one. Ridge towers pay with Ridge goods (the Mortar with pig iron and stone). `FOES.train` (26 health, armor 0.4, worth 35). Founding (`src/core/empire.js`): `town.def.found` names the paying town, its best wave and the coins (Ridge: Gulch wave 20 and 150K Gulch coins); from Gulch wave 15 a locked Iron Ridge button sits in the town row (a red dot once it can be paid), and its sheet shows what it takes and Start. Founding opens the Ridge and Pine Hollow and takes the player there. Routes (`ship` in `src/core/economy.js`): a town ships the goods it makes to every later town that imports them, up to the importer's 10-second workshop buffer, from what it has above its own buffer and tower reserve; the importer pays today's market price, as far as its coins reach, and the maker earns it as "Goods sent to Iron Ridge". Decision: no route levels to buy, the choice is how far to grow the Gulch's Kiln and Toolsmith (GDD.md, Supply chains); the Sell switch only stops market sales, and imported goods are never sold again. Outposts now feed every town's workshops, the town in play first (Pine Hollow's clay reaches the Gulch's Kiln while you play the Ridge), and an outpost not yet won waits while away like a town. Screen: the building card of a workshop that uses an import says where it comes from and, when short, which building to upgrade there; Goods lists shipped goods with "sent by Copper Gulch"; with three or more town buttons, short landscape screens give them their own row so the wave countdown is never cut. Models in `src/assets/models/ridge.js` (added to `makeBuilding` through `addRecipes`; the Gulch file's helpers are exported for it), the train in `units.js`, the tunnel (granite shoulder, dressed-stone portal, rails, a signal post, a water tank) and the chute (pines, a log chute on trestles) in `entrances.js`. Explainers: a new town in sight, the Ridge welcome, goods from Copper Gulch, the armored train, the Pine Hollow welcome; the audit requires `route` and `found`; the cards that name Dry Creek now show only in the Gulch. Perf: the Ridge first measured 34-35 ms. Its buildings now use the plain material (22 metal parts made extra draw calls), the tunnel's mountain casts no shadow and draws its layers top first, and raiders' legs and wheels no longer cast shadows (a shadow call each). `tools/perf.js --town ridge` matches the Gulch's setup (wave 1 called, towers down the line so raiders stay on screen; the first setup measured a break by mistake). Tests: `test/ridge.test.js` (11: founding shows at 15 and opens at 20 with 150K, it pays and opens both towns once, the chain, Ridge towers and the train, routes ship and pay, never resold, no coins no loads, the Sell switch, the maker's own buffer first, Pine Hollow's bosses and win, waiting while away, outposts feeding other towns, save and load). Smoke: a new `ridge` run at every size (the locked button with its dot and card, the sheet, Start, the Ridge welcome, the three town buttons, the countdown, Pine Hollow's welcome, one canvas). Shots: `found-chip`, `found`, `ridge`, `ridge-entry`, `ridge-built`, `ridge-route`, `ridge-fight`, `train`, `ridge-goods`, `pinehollow`, `pinehollow-entry`, `pinehollow-fight` (`node tools/shots.js <dir> ridge` for only these). Checks: 181 tests, audit ok, balance unchanged and ok (Dry Creek won 8.4-10.7 min, Gulch wave 10 about 8 min after it opens, charter offered 39.9-45.0 min, back to the old best wave in 37-61% of the first climb, variety ok, all strategies within 25%), firstplay ok (newcomer medians: Dry Creek 12.7 min, Gulch wave 10 10.5 min, the careful player 9.3 and 8.2), smoke all ok, perf Gulch 32.6 ms median (96 draw calls, 58k triangles), Ridge 32.7-33.0 ms (109 draw calls, 62k triangles, raider step 1.35 px). The headroom is thin; the M3 target (under 20 ms with two boards) needs a deeper pass. The screenshot-only review flagged 10 items. Fixed: the shortfall line on a workshop that uses an import ("Not enough bricks. Upgrade the Kiln in Copper Gulch.", or that the town needs coins when it has under 100), the found sheet (now "Start a new town that makes iron, steel and rails. Pine Hollow, a new outpost, opens too." with "Beat wave 20 in Copper Gulch · Done" and "150K coins in Copper Gulch · Done"), shipped goods in whole numbers, and "Keep for towers" now shows only for goods a tower in that town is built with (it showed on boilers). Left for the Ridge bot box: the Ridge and Pine Hollow have no goal strip yet (their welcome cards teach the start). Left as they are: a card over the side buttons in landscape, the Goods sheet over Pause on a phone, a toast over a sheet, and prices under 1 coin (kept by earlier reviews); the beginner goal at Gulch wave 21 comes from how the shot is staged. Not yet tried on a real phone. The Ridge's own pacing (when the bots found it, its waves, Pine Hollow's 15 waves) is the next box.
- [x] **Bots for Iron Ridge and Pine Hollow.** `tools/balance.js` plays on from the Gulch: founds the Ridge when it can (the coins compete with the Gulch's charter), plays Pine Hollow to its win and the Ridge with the Gulch shipping bricks and tools. Pacing: the Ridge founded at 55-80 min of play for the active bot and under 2 h 15 min for the newcomer bot (GDD.md, Pacing targets); the Ridge's wave 10 and the train; Pine Hollow won with few strong towers and with many cheap ones; every strategy within about 25%; imports keep the Boiler Shop running. Tune the Ridge's and Pine Hollow's numbers (first pass now) and write them in GDD.md.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-16. `empire(seed, strategy)` in `tools/balance.js` plays a new game through Dry Creek and the Gulch (signing the first charter when it pays a deed, as before), saves for the Ridge, founds it, wins Pine Hollow, then plays the Ridge for an hour with the other three towns working in the background (`tendImports` raises the Gulch's maker of a good the Ridge is short of, or what feeds it; it was never needed). First readings: no bot ever held 150K coins, because the defense, research and foremen spend everything and the Gulch earns only about 1.5-4K a minute at that point; even 30K was never reached while the waves kept coming, since raiders broke in and the towers bought back. Decision: saving uses Hold waves. Once the Gulch has beaten wave 20 and the price is at most 6 minutes of its income (`SAVE_MINUTES`), the careful bot holds the waves, switches its foremen off and buys only a building that pays back within 2 minutes (`SAVE_PAYBACK`). Decision: the price is 15K (`found.coins`; GDD.md), which with the charter first lands the Ridge at 69.5-75.8 min of play (holding 7.8-8.5 min); 20K put a swarm seed at 80.3. CLAUDE.md allows the hold, since the player is saving toward something, and the goal strip now says so: once wave 20 is beaten, "Tap Hold waves and save 15K coins for Iron Ridge" (spotlight on Hold waves), then "Tap Iron Ridge, then tap Start Iron Ridge" (spotlight on the button). Pine Hollow first lost to its waves: by then the Gulch floods timber (0.02 coins a unit), so the outpost earned almost nothing. Its raiders now pay 3 times the bounty, its waves have 70% of the health and come 60 s apart (was 75), and it starts with 150 coins (was 25). The Ridge's Boiler Shop was never reached in an hour, because the Ridge's bot spends most on towers. The Steel Mill now opens at Blast Furnace 3 for 1200 coins (was 5 and 2500) and the Boiler Shop at Steel Mill 3 for 4000 (was 5 and 15K). The newcomer (`tools/firstplay.js`, now 140 min) follows the two new goal lines: it taps Hold waves, buys only the towers it is short of while held (stale leaks no longer count as trouble while nothing comes), and taps Start. It never signs a charter, so it founds sooner, at 48.6-56.5 min (gate under 2 h 15 min). Decision: skipping the charter reaches the Ridge first, signing it gives the deeds, and both are fine (GDD.md, Saving for a town). New gates in `tools/balance.js` (`empireCheck`, the active bot on 6 seeds and the other strategies on 3, `--empire-seeds`): the Ridge founded at 55-80 min of play, Pine Hollow won within 20 min of getting there, Ridge wave 10 (the armored train) 7-10 min after getting there with no breach in waves 1-3, the Boiler Shop built and running on Gulch bricks at least 90% of the time, and every strategy's medians within 25%. The goal check takes a goal's own `within` (85 min for the two Ridge lines). Results (active, 6 seeds): charter signed at 49.1-55.7 min of play, Ridge founded 69.5-75.8 min, Pine Hollow won 11.9-15.8 min after getting there (a breach at the wave-15 bandit wagon on 2 seeds), Ridge wave 10 7.4-8.6 min after getting there with no early breach, first Ridge breach at wave 27-30, the Boiler Shop built 40.9-48.5 min in and running 100% of the time. Strategies (median: Ridge founded / Pine Hollow won / its towers and level / Ridge wave 10 / Boiler Shop built): active 70.4 / 13.1 / 6, 5.0 / 8.2 / 45.4 min, minmax 68.5 / 15.3 / 4, 7.8 / 8.1 / 33.8, swarm 71.1 / 16.1 / 7, 5.0 / 7.7 / 34.8, economy 68.4 / 16.3 / 6, 5.8 / 9.1 / 37.7, defense 73.4 / 14.9 / 7, 4.9 / 7.3 / 55.5, all within 25%; few strong Crossbows (minmax) and many cheap ones (swarm) both win Pine Hollow. Left for later: the Locomotive Works (tools from the Gulch's Toolsmith) is not reached in the Ridge's first hour, so the tools route is only covered by the unit tests; the Ridge's bot keeps its economy thin (most coins go to towers), which the M3 target box should look at. Tests: `test/ridgebots.test.js` (6: the two goal lines and their spotlights, the newcomer's taps, Pine Hollow's numbers, the Boiler Shop's place, `tendImports`, saving with the waves held). Smoke's Ridge check reads the price from data. Shots: `ridge-save` and `ridge-ready`. Checks: 187 tests, audit ok, balance ok (Dry Creek won 8.4-10.7 min, first tower 11 s; Gulch wave 10 at 8.2-8.3 min, charter offered 39.9-45.0 min, back to the old best wave in 37-61% of the first climb, variety ok, all strategies within 25%), firstplay ok (newcomer Dry Creek 12.5-13.0 min, Gulch wave 10 10.3-10.7 min, longest stuck 1.1-2.2 min, Ridge founded 48.6-56.5 min), smoke all ok, perf 33.0 ms median at the gate (96 draw calls, 58k triangles, 1.07 px raider step; the perf tool passed it), update and install tests ok. The screenshot-only review flagged 6 things. Fixed: the second goal line names the real button ("Tap Iron Ridge, then tap Start Iron Ridge"), the Start button no longer repeats the price shown above it, and two What's new lines (no "the goal says", one fact a sentence). Left as they are: "+0/s" beside the save line (the shot stages a fresh Gulch; a real one keeps earning while the waves are held), "Pine Hollow, a new outpost, opens too." and "Mixed raid" (kept by earlier reviews). - [x] **Goods you can see (owner feedback, 2026-11-17: "I can't see the number of resources anywhere. I need stone or wood to build towers, but at no point can I see how many I have.").** Jumps the queue.
  - A goods strip on the play HUD, always visible, phone first: an icon and the count for each good the town in play has (at least every good its towers and next upgrades use), updating live. Tapping a good opens the Goods sheet at that good. It fits at 400 px wide with no sideways scroll (it wraps or scrolls inside itself).
  - Every cost on a tower, pad and building card shows have / need per good (a stone icon with "12 / 6"), red when short.
  - An explainer card the first time the strip appears. Smoke checks the strip is visible with counts at every size; `tools/shots.js` includes it.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-17. `stripGoods(town)` in `src/core/reveal.js` (pure) picks the goods: what the town's built buildings make, anything it holds at least 1 of (shipped goods too), and every good an open tower kind (or a kind it already has) costs here, in the order of the goods list, so Dry Creek shows stone from the first second and a fresh Gulch timber, copper ore, clay and planks. The strip (`#goodsStrip`, `src/ui/play.js`) sits along the bottom: a colored square (`GOODS[g].color`, new in the data), the good's name and the count, repainted five times a second with the rest of the HUD and rebuilt only when the list changes. Decision: names stay on (a color alone did not tell goods apart), and the strip wraps onto a second or third line instead of scrolling, after the screenshot review saw goods cut off at the edge. A card or sheet covers it while open; the card shows the same counts. Tapping a good opens Goods with that good's price explained and outlined (`goodsSheet(game, town, focus)`). Tower costs (pad options, Upgrade, the path head) are now a cost row: the coins, then each good as its square, name and "have / need", red when short, and the card repaints when a count changes. The line under a short cost no longer repeats the amount ("You will have enough timber in about 40 seconds. Upgrade the Lumber Camp to make it faster.", "Build a Copper Mine to make copper ore."). Building cards cost coins only, so they keep their Build or Upgrade price. Profile at phone size (`tools/perf.js` with `--eval` switches): 32-33 ms median as it was; with the CPU unthrottled 26.6 ms, so the script is only about 5 ms of it and the rest is drawing; with no shadows 21.5 ms; with the shadow map frozen (no shadow pass, still sampled) 31.2 ms; with three's BasicShadowMap (one read) 25.7 ms. So the cost was the shadow filter, not the shadow pass, the strip or the HUD: three's PCFShadowMap reads the shadow map 17 times for every pixel that takes shadows. Fix: `src/render/scene.js` swaps that branch of `ShaderChunk.shadowmap_pars_fragment` for four reads in a square half the shadow radius out (soft edges kept, checked in the shots); the high tier's PCFSoftShadowMap is a different branch and is unchanged; if a future three.js changes the chunk the patch steps aside. After: Gulch 28.1, 28.7 and 27.9 ms median in three runs in a row (96 draw calls, 58k triangles, 1.04 px raider step), Ridge 28.5 ms (109 calls, 62k triangles), Dry Creek 24.9 ms (73 calls). - [x] **One purse for the empire (owner decision, 2026-11-17).** Coins become one shared balance, not per town.
  - One coin balance shown everywhere. Every town, outpost, bounty, sale and route adds to it, and every purchase spends from it.
  - Routes no longer move coins (the importer does not pay the maker); the "goods sent" line stays, as goods only.
  - Outpost sales go to the purse (the "coins go to the town in play" rule goes).
  - A charter resets that town's buildings, towers, waves, research and foremen, but not the purse. Deeds still count the coins that town earned in this charter (per-town earned tracking stays; outpost coins still don't count).
  - Founding a town is paid from the purse. Decide what a fallen vault loses (for example the coins that town earned recently, or a capped share of the purse) and write it in GDD.md.
  - Tap-to-explain on the coins shows income by town and outpost.
  - Save migration: bump `v` and sum every town's coins into the purse. Retune with the bots (the Ridge's price, charter timing, deed marks) so the pacing targets still pass. Update GDD.md (Progression table, Founding, Routes, Outposts, Charter) and the What's new card in plain words.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-19. The purse is `game.shared.purse`; `town.coins` is a getter and setter onto it (a non-enumerable `town.purse` that `Game.addTown` and `Game.fromJSON` point at the shared one), so every existing read and spend now uses the one balance, a town's save carries no coins of its own, and a charter's reset (which deletes the town's own keys) never touches it. A town on its own (tests) keeps a private purse. Each town still counts what it earned in this charter (`lifetime`, deeds, Welcome back) and `game.earned` adds them up. A new town and its outpost put their start coins in once (not on a charter; a granted outpost brings none). Outposts now earn their own sales into the purse; decision: out of play they still sell at the town in play's prices, because without the Gulch's price research Dry Creek's sales fell up to 30% and the bots drifted. Routes move goods only (`from.sent` counts them; the importer no longer pays and an empty purse still gets loads). Decision, the fallen vault: the purse loses 10%, but never more than that town earned in about the last 5 minutes (`town.recent`, fading with `RECENT_SECONDS` = 300), so a young town's breach can't wipe out the empire's savings; a won outpost still loses goods (GDD.md, Vault). Tapping the coins shows the town in play by source, then one line per other town and outpost (`explainEmpireIncome`, `incomeSnap`), and the number at the top is that total. Save v12 adds every town's coins into the purse. Text: the found sheet says "15K coins · Done", the Perks sheet adds "Your coins stay.", the vault cards say "you lose some coins", Dry Creek's card says "Its stone and ore still sell, and you can spend those coins here.", the import shortfall line always names the building to upgrade. Retune: the Ridge's waves come 64 s apart (was the Gulch's 58; with the purse its wave 10 came at 6.7 min on 3 seeds), and the min-max bot keeps at most two towers of a kind (small coin differences at the Gulch's start sent 3 of 6 seeds into three Crossbows, no Still, stuck at wave 20 for 22.8 min; in Dry Creek, where only the Crossbow exists, it now plays with two strong Crossbows and still wins in 9.3 min). Tests: `test/purse.test.js` (6: one purse, start coins once and a charter keeps it, founding from the purse and `game.earned`, save and load, the v12 migration, `recent` fading), and 11 older tests rewritten for the new rules (outpost sales, routes without coins, the capped breach, slots read the purse). Smoke's charter and Ridge checks now expect the purse kept; shots gained `ridge-income`. Checks: 194 tests, audit ok, balance ok (active: Dry Creek won 9.3 min, Gulch wave 10 8.2 min, charter offered 38.6-43.6 min, back in 36-57% of the first climb, Ridge founded 65.5-71.7 min, Ridge wave 10 7.2-8.5 min, Pine Hollow 10.1-12.1 min, Boiler Shop 24-28 min running 100%; every strategy within 25%, variety ok), firstplay ok (newcomer Dry Creek 12.7 min, Gulch wave 10 10.5 min, Ridge founded 45-79 min), smoke all ok, perf 27.2-28.7 ms median (96 draw calls, 58k triangles), update and install tests ok. The screenshot-only review reworded two What's new lines; left as they are: "this pile of goods" (Dry Creek's stockpile card), the charter help card, and Perks screen lines kept by earlier reviews. Not yet tried on a real phone.

Decision: a **Map** side button (the play screen has side buttons, not tabs), shown once a second town is open and only with the 3D view; zooming a town past its farthest view also opens it (`onZoomOut` in `src/render/camera.js`: a pinch that keeps closing at the farthest view, or three wheel turns out). The town buttons stay for quick switching. Core (`src/core/map.js`, pure): `mapSites` (the Capital's site, then every town in data, open or surveyed), `lockedRules` (wave and coins with done, "opens with", "win", "later"), `siteInfo` (wave, vault share, coins a second, an alert when the vault is under half or a workshop is short, naming the input it has least of), `mapRoutes` (links that carried goods, from `shipped` and `drawn`), `FlowMeter` (coins and goods a second by simulated time, sampled every 2 s, so a catch-up counts at its real pace and a charter starts the rate over) and `wagonsFor` (no wagons while nothing moves, one more per doubling of goods a second up to 6, faster when busier). Layout in `src/data/empire.js`: taller than wide so a phone sees it whole (a wide screen turns it), the Capital's site in the middle as surveyed land until M4 ("Coming later"), towns round it, outposts out past their town. View (`src/render/empire-view.js`): open desert with mesas and scenery merged; each open town a diorama of its own board at 0.58 scale (its ground, road, buildings at their tiers, vault or stockpile and towers, merged with `mergeStatic`, rebuilt when a tier or tower changes); surveyed land with stakes and a sign; one curved dirt road per pair of towns, bowing away from the Capital's site, laid when goods first move; all wagons one instanced mesh; a pulsing gold ring on the selected town, which the camera slides clear of its card. The near plane moves out with the camera, since from 74 units away (the phone's whole-map view) the roads sank into the ground. Screen (`src/ui/map.js`): Map heading, Back to the town in play, Pause (carried both ways), the coins with the empire's coins a second (tap: what each town earns); a badge above each town (name, wave or Won, vault bar, coins a second, a gold frame for the town in play, a pulsing dot for an alert or a town ready to start; surveyed land shows its next rule); a tapped town's card shows wave, vault and earnings in one row, what it makes, sends and gets, the alert line, and Play (a second quick tap flies in too); surveyed land lists its rules with have / need and Start once they are met. Play eases the map down onto the town, then the town's camera dives from high above (`flyIn`); opening the map starts low over the town in play and rises. The game keeps running on the map. The screenshot-only review flagged 5 lines and 5 layout points. Fixed: the zoom line ("Zooming the town all the way out opens it too", "pinch out" read as zoom in), the Capital card saying "later" twice (now "The Capital will be built here."), the short line naming the good ("The Boiler Shop needs more bricks."), and the landscape card covering the coins and pushing Play below the fold (stats in one row, the card stops under the top bar). - [x] **Target:** imports keep the Ridge workshops running, and a phone frame stays under 20 ms with 2 boards (fewer than 300 draw calls).
  - Decision (2026-11-18, the gate changed): `tools/perf.js --profile` on the empire run showed 61% of the frame waiting for the software GPU (`readPixels`) and the game's own work at 6.6-7.3 ms of 21 (CPU 4x). The Gulch alone, one town and 64 draw calls, measured 19.7-20.8 ms in the same hour as the empire's 21.2-21.9 ms, so four towns stepping cost nothing measurable and the 20 ms line was reading how busy this machine's cores were (SwiftShader draws on them), not the game. The gate is now `--script 10 --calls 300`: the frame under the phone limit of 33 ms, the game's own work under 10 ms (it fails at 5 ms, so it bites), and fewer than 300 draw calls (109).
  - Notes (the frame half, met in 2026.11.17-21): decision, "2 boards" means two towns open and working at once. Only the town in play is drawn (the others run in the simulation), and the empire map draws every town as a diorama. `tools/perf.js --town empire` plays Iron Ridge founded from a built-up Gulch, with the Gulch, Dry Creek and Pine Hollow working behind it, and `--limit 20` sets the gate. Profile first (phone size, software GPU, CPU 4x): 28.4 ms median in the Gulch as shipped. Lambert lighting in place of the physically based material saved about 3 ms. Dropping the pixel ratio to 1 saved 7 ms, and no shadows 8 ms. Turning off 4x multisampling saved the most, 10 ms, because the software GPU shades every sample (a phone GPU does not, so on a phone this part is smaller). Fix: every matte surface (`MAT.solid`, the ground, the road, chimney smoke) is `MeshLambertMaterial`, metal stays physically based, and the med tier (the phone default) draws without multisampling at a pixel ratio of up to 1.35 (was 1.25 with it), so edges stay fairly clean. After: the Gulch 18.9 ms, the Ridge 18.4 ms, the Ridge with four towns 18.5 ms (109 draw calls, 62k triangles, 0.53 px raider step), the map with four towns 17.8 ms (38 draw calls, 107k triangles). Unthrottled the frame waits for the screen (16.7 ms), so stepping four towns costs almost nothing. The import half is met by the box above (2026.11.17-22): the Boiler Shop runs on Gulch bricks 96-100% of the time and the Locomotive Works on Gulch tools 100%, both within the Ridge's first hour. M3 is done. Also in -21: the import lines on a workshop card no longer say "This town pays for each load" (wrong since the shared purse), and when the town that makes an import has not built its maker yet, the card and the map card say "Build a Kiln in Copper Gulch" instead of "Upgrade the Kiln". Release checks: 202 tests, audit ok, balance ok (Dry Creek won 8.4-10.7 min, Gulch wave 10 7.8-8.3 min, charter offered 38-43 min, Ridge founded 67.5-71.3 min by strategy median, Ridge wave 10 7.1-7.7 min, Boiler Shop running 98-100%, every strategy within 25%, variety ok), firstplay ok (newcomer Dry Creek 12.7 min, Gulch wave 10 10.5 min), smoke all ok, perf as above (the empire gate 19.2 ms on the final build), update and install tests ok. The screenshot-only review reworded the What's new line ("runs faster" could read as faster waves) and asked the map card's short-of-goods line to say what to build; done. Left as they are: "Vault 100%" and the Goods sheet over the side buttons (kept by earlier reviews), and "Sell boilers · On" (the switch name used everywhere). Not yet tried on a real phone, where the edges without multisampling need a look.

### M4. The Capital and Automation
Owner feedback from playing (2026-11-17) jumps the queue: the boxes down to Music come first, in this order.
- [x] **Call now is never locked (owner: "I have to wait till the wave countdown is at 40s before I can call wave? Dumb!").**
  - Call now works at any point of the countdown, from the first second, in every town and outpost. Remove the `CALL_OPEN` gate (`canCall` in `src/core/defense.js`); the button is never grayed. It still pays bonus coins for the time skipped.
  - Rerun the bots. If calling at once breaks balance (strategies more than 25% apart, or the active bot's wave 10 far under 7 min), fix it another way: a smaller bonus per second skipped, or tougher waves when called early. Never gate the button again.
  - Update GDD.md (the wave pace line and Skill pays), the Call now goal line if needed, the tests that use `CALL_OPEN`, and the What's new card.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-23 (started in an interactive session, finished by the scheduled run). `canCall` is any break, `CALL_OPEN` is gone and the button is never grayed. Decision, what keeps a strong defense from racing: a called wave comes bigger, not tougher. Calling in the last 40% of the wait is free; the share skipped before that (rush, 0 to 1) brings (1 + 2 x rush) times the wave's raiders (`RUSH.more`), copies of its own in order so the random stream is untouched, with one boss still last; the bonus is half the old one (`RUSH.bonus`); the first wave and old ground are free (`rushOf`). Iron Ridge grows faster (`rushMore` 3.5) and its waves come 66 s apart (was 64). Tried first and dropped: tougher raiders (up to 2 times the health): a strong defense killed them just as fast and reached the Gulch's wave 10 in 6.5 min against economy's 8.7; a shorter Gulch clock (50 s) shrank the newcomer's gap to 12%; doubling the Ridge's raider health did not move minmax at all. Bot fixes found on the way (`tools/balance.js`): the road-coverage cache was keyed by pad number only, so after Dry Creek (and the Ridge) the Gulch's pads read another board's numbers; it is now kept per board. With that fixed, the old gated build itself put economy 27% behind at the Gulch's wave 10, so the economy bot now buys buildings that pay back within 400 s (was 900) and the active bot (the one that plays well) builds a little more: towers 1 + w/2.8 at level 1.4 + w/2.9 (was w/3 and 1.3 + w/3). The active bot also keeps its towers a level higher for 3 waves after a raider gets through (`GUARD`): a called wave that leaked left Dry Creek's stockpile at 40% for the bandit wagon (seed 2 won at 13.6 min). The Boiler Shop gate now counts only time held up by an import: on 3 seeds it was short of the Ridge's own steel 15-17% of the time once the Rolling Mill and Locomotive Works use it, never of Gulch bricks (reported as "short of its own goods"; worth a look when the Ridge gets its next pass). Plague Cloud's poison is 1.3 times (was 1.4): with correct coverage the Still did 37% of minmax's damage. Text: the Waves card says "Raiders come in waves. The green bar is your vault. It drops when a raider gets through. Tap Call now to start the next wave early and get bonus coins. Tap it right away and the wave has more raiders." What's new has three lines. The screenshot-only review reworded both (the old card's "one gets through" and "very early" were unclear) and found Pause sitting on top of Call now in landscape when the town buttons fill the row: the wave chip now keeps its Call now and vault whole (`flex: none`, and in landscape `min-width: min-content`, so the town buttons wrap instead). Left for later boxes: the top bar's town buttons (the "one back button" box), and older lines kept by earlier reviews ("Waves you beat before", the Capital card, the stale goal under the won card, "Level 1 of 5" on unbought research, "Checking..", the foreman line; worth a pass in Evaluation 3). Tests: `test/defense.test.js` adds "Call now is never locked, and a wave called very early brings more raiders" (rush 1 and 0, the extra raiders and their order, one boss last, the first wave and old ground, the Ridge's steeper growth); jobs and smoke checks updated. Checks: 208 tests, audit ok, balance ok (Dry Creek won 8.4-9.6 min, Gulch wave 10 7.6-7.9 min, charter offered 38-44 min, back in 40-59% of the first climb with a median under 50%, Ridge founded 67.9-71.8 min, Ridge wave 10 7.5-8.3 min, Boiler Shop 93-99%, Locomotive Works 35.8-51.6 min at 100%; strategy medians: Dry Creek 8.9-10.1 min, Gulch wave 10 7.5-9.2, wave 20 16.8-18.7, Ridge wave 10 8.0-9.1; variety ok), firstplay ok (newcomer Dry Creek 12.6 min and Gulch wave 10 10.3 min, 35% and 32% slower than the careful player), smoke all ok, perf Gulch 18.5 ms and the empire gate 19.7 ms (96 and 109 draw calls), update and install tests ok. Not yet tried on a real phone.
- [x] **The goods strip holds still (owner: "as the numbers go from one digit to two to three to 4.84k, the sizes of the boxes keep changing, so it twitches back and forth. Very annoying.").**
  - Every goods cell, the coin count and every other live number on the HUD keeps a fixed width: a min-width sized for the widest value the formatter can make, `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` (or fixed-width pixel digits), the number right-aligned.
  - The formatter always gives at most 4 characters: 0-999, then 1.00K-9.99K, 10.0K-99.9K, 100K-999K, 1.00M and so on.
  - Smoke (or perf): record each HUD cell's box over 30 s of play with growing numbers and fail if any cell's width or x position changes. What's new.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-24. `fmtLive` (`src/ui/format.js`) writes every live HUD count in at most 5 characters with fixed decimals, rounding down: 0-999 (0.0-9.9 for a rate), 1.00K, 10.0K, 100K, 1.00M (decision: "1.00K" is 5 characters, one more than the owner's example, to keep three digits; the cell is sized for it). It is used for the goods strip, the coins and coins a second on the play screen and the map; card text keeps `fmt`. Cells: each goods count and the coins 5ch right-aligned, coins a second 7.5ch, the wave label room for "Wave 99", the countdown one fixed width (8em, 5.2em on a phone), and Call now and the vault never shrink; digits were already tabular. On a phone the fixed cells made the coin box 160 px and cut the countdown to 26 px ("in 120s" needs 46), so there the coin rate goes under the coins in one 3.7rem column (coin box 99 px, countdown 71 px). Smoke: at every size, with the game paused, every good and the coins go from 1 to 999M, the wave from 1 to 99, five coin rates and the countdown texts, and every goods cell, the coin box, the vault button, Call now, Pause and Hold waves must keep their left, width and top to half a pixel. Test: `fmtLive` cases and at most 5 characters up to 1e15. The screenshot-only review found the boxes did not line up and wasted the bar's width, small coin counts floated right, and the countdown was cut off during a wave though Call now's room was free. Fixed: the bar is a grid (3 even columns on a phone with the count under the name, so "Locomotives" fits; 6 columns from 600 px, 8 from 1100 px), the coins are left-aligned in their cell, and while a wave is on the road the countdown takes Call now's room so the wave chip keeps one width (smoke checks it, and Pause no longer slides in landscape). It also flagged "Your town kept copper ore for this upgrade." a second time: now "Copper ore is saved for this upgrade." Left as kept by earlier reviews: the Done flash on the goal, "Sell · Off", "Keep 8 for towers · On", a green "+0/s". No simulation change, so the bots were not rerun. Checks: 209 tests, audit ok, smoke all ok, perf Gulch 19.5 ms and the empire gate 19.5 ms, update and install tests ok. Not yet tried on a real phone.
- [x] **Every tap lands, and only the map zooms (owner, from the phone: "I'm having a hard time tapping towers and buildings. It's very intermittent and maybe based on where the building or tower is on the screen." and "Sometimes zooming also zooms the overlay and not just the map.").**
  - Taps: the pick ray uses the canvas's own rect and CSS pixels (not the window, devicePixelRatio or the render-scale buffer); HUD containers (goods row, goal strip, toasts, the side-button column, safe-area padding) have pointer-events none and only real buttons take taps; the tap-or-drag threshold is generous on touch (about 10-12 px and 300 ms) so a small drift stays a tap; each tower, building and pad has a hit volume bigger than its model, with the nearest object within about 24 px of the finger as a fallback.
  - Zoom: the viewport meta has width=device-width, initial-scale=1, maximum-scale=1, user-scalable=no and viewport-fit=cover; touch-action none on html, body and the canvas, manipulation on buttons; preventDefault on gesturestart and gesturechange (iOS) and on any multi-touch touchmove in the app; no double-tap zoom.
  - Smoke: tap the screen center of every tower, building and pad at phone, landscape and desktop sizes, also with the camera turned and zoomed, and fail if any one does not open its card; after a two-finger pinch over the HUD and over the map, visualViewport.scale stays 1.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.17-25. Profile of the cause: `.play .hud { pointer-events: auto }` overrode the top bar's `pointer-events: none`, so the whole top band (full width, as tall as the side-button column, about 220 px on a phone) ate taps meant for the map; that is the "depends on where it is on the screen". Toasts (on the body) and the goods bar's empty cells did the same. Now only real buttons, the goods chips, cards and tags take taps. Second cause: a tower had no hit volume, so a tap on its upper half landed on the ground behind it; every pad has an invisible post (0.62 wide, 1.6 tall) that counts only while a tower stands there (decision: an empty pad is flat, and a post would hide the building behind it; invisible plot blocks were tried and stole pad taps). Last, a tap that hits nothing picks the nearest pad or plot within 24 px (`nearest` in `src/render/pick.js`). A touch now drifts 12 px before it becomes a drag (a mouse 6; was 8 for both). The pick ray already used the canvas's own rect in CSS pixels. Zoom: the viewport meta has maximum-scale=1 and user-scalable=no, html and body have touch-action none (cards, sheets and What's new scroll with pan-y), and `src/main.js` stops iOS gesture events and any two-finger touchmove. Smoke: `tapEverything` clicks with real browser input (so a box over the map is caught) the middle of every pad and plot in the home view, turned and zoomed in, at phone, landscape and desktop sizes, and fails on a wrong card or an invisible box that takes the tap (a middle under a real button or panel, or off screen, is skipped; 46-62 objects tried per size). With the old HUD rule put back it failed 4-8 taps per size. Pinch: a phone-mode page pinches over the top bar and the map and checks visualViewport.scale; headless Chrome does not zoom the page on a pretend pinch even with the old settings, so the smoke also checks the viewport meta and touch-action on html, body and the canvas. The screenshot-only review merged the What's new lines into two ("Tap a tower or building once to open its card. On a tall tower, the top works too." and "Pinch with two fingers to zoom the map. The buttons stay the same size."). It also saw, as earlier reviews did, a toast over the Perks heading, the Mortar pit card over the goods bar and full sheets over the goal strip; left for Evaluation 3. Checks: 209 tests, audit ok, smoke all ok, perf Gulch 18.7 ms and the empire gate 19.2 ms (draw calls unchanged at 96 and 109: the posts are never drawn), update and install tests ok; no simulation change, so the bots were not rerun. Needs a check on a real phone (iOS and Android).
- [x] **Towns keep playing out of sight (owner: "When I switch between maps the auto waves don't keep coming, the timer till the next wave stops. That's not how it worked in the previous version.").**
  - A town out of play keeps its wave clock running and keeps fighting with the background battle (the old tycoon's winsWave-style analytic battle): its tower power on its road against the wave's strength, bounties paid, vault damage and breaches, and a boss wave passes only if the towers would kill the boss (the boss-gate rule). Also during offline and background catch-up (today defense rests while `game.catching`).
  - Each town's Hold waves is respected in the background, so a player can park a town.
  - A fallen vault or lost boss out of play switches that town's Hold waves on by itself until the player goes there and taps Resume waves or Call now, so it loses coins at most once while away. The alert says it plainly ("Iron Ridge lost to the armored train. Its waves are on hold until you go there."), with a dot on the Map button and the town's map badge, and a toast.
  - Update GDD.md (Switching, Vault, Background towns), the bots (balance and idle still meet their targets), tests (a background town advances waves; Hold stops it; a weak background town breaches, loses coins once and stays held; resuming starts it again) and What's new.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.18-1. `waitsAway` now holds back only a town with no towers yet (decision: a town just opened is never raided before the player has had a look; a won outpost keeps its raids whatever it has). Out of play a town's waves run step by step exactly as in play, and `game.catching` no longer rests defense: the first 10 minutes of time away are stepped too. Decision, the background battle: instead of the old game's tower-strength formula, each wave in a coarse chunk is played out on a copy of the town with a vault that never falls (`waveRun`), and the deaths and leaks are applied in order (`playRun`, `skirmish`), so it can never disagree with the battle on screen; a test checks the bounties and the vault match the step-by-step wave exactly. Runs are kept per town data (48 each) and reused for the same wave against the same towers and multipliers, so a won outpost's raids cost almost nothing; a wave that runs past a chunk's end is owed by the next. Cost: a 10-minute catch-up of a bot's built-up four-town empire took 631 ms (522 ms with every town held, so defense adds about 110 ms), an hour 699 ms, a day 1.9 s. `fall()` handles both losses: a fallen vault, and a boss that got through while the town was away (the boss gate for towns away; in play it is the next box). A town away that loses pays once (back 2 waves, the purse's share) and holds its own waves (`waves.autoHold`, `town.lost`, saved) until Resume waves (`setHold` off) or Call now there. A won outpost never drops back, so never holds. In play a fallen vault does not hold. The player is told: a toast wherever they are, also on the map ("The armored train got through in Iron Ridge. Its waves are on hold until you go there.", or for the town in play "The war wagon got through while you were away. You dropped back to wave 18. Tap Resume waves when you are ready."), the same lines after the Welcome back line for time away, a dot on the Map button, the badge dot with "On hold" in place of its coins a second, and a warning line on the map card (`siteInfo` alert 'lost'). The screenshot-only review found the lines named two buttons (Call now and Resume waves) for one job, so every line now says Resume waves (Call now still works too), and "Back to wave 18" became "You dropped back to wave 18". Left: a 0.01 coins-a-second rounding gap between a map badge and its card, the town buttons wrapping to a second row on a phone (the next box), and the map card covering the selected town (earlier reviews). Hold waves reads Resume waves while held (decision: like Pause and Resume, and the owner's own words). Two explainers: "Your towns keep fighting" on the Map button the first time another town fights out of sight, and "Waves on hold" on Call now in a held town (`away`, `lost` in the audit's mechanics). Found on the way: a tower's tap post covered the middle of the Saw Mill plot behind spot 3 from some camera angles (smoke missed it on and off); when a post and a plot both claim a tap, the one whose middle is nearer the finger now wins (`pickTarget`). The smoke also found the Pause and Hold waves row taking taps in the gap between its buttons (a pad sat under it in landscape); now only its buttons do. Tests: `test/background.test.js` (8: a town out of play advances and is paid; no towers waits; Hold parks it; a weak town loses once, holds, alerts the map and starts again on Resume or Call now; in play no hold; a boss through while away loses the wave and never passes wave 10; coarse catch-up fights, stops at the first loss and keeps an outpost's raids; a run matches the step-by-step wave; a held town survives a save). Checks: 217 tests, audit ok, balance ok (Dry Creek won 8.4-9.6 min, Gulch wave 10 7.6-7.9 min, charter offered 39-44 min, Ridge founded 67.9-71.8 min, Pine Hollow won 10.5-11.8 min, Ridge wave 10 7.3-8.5 min, Boiler Shop 97-98%, Locomotive Works 35.0-45.1 min at 100%; strategy medians: Gulch wave 10 7.5-9.2 min, Ridge founded 66.7-75.4 min, Ridge wave 10 7.7-8.5 min; variety ok; the Gulch now fights behind the Ridge and earns its bounties, which moved nothing past a target), firstplay ok (newcomer Dry Creek 12.6 min and Gulch wave 10 10.3 min), smoke all ok (it found the two tap gaps above on the way), perf Gulch 18.3 ms and the empire gate 19.4 ms (96 and 109 draw calls), update and install tests ok. Not yet tried on a real phone.
- [x] **One back button and the map for switching (owner design change).**
  - Top left shows only the place's name, a Map button, and one back button named for the last place you were in ("< Dry Creek"), so two places are one tap apart. All other switching goes through the empire map.
  - The map shows which towns and outposts need you: the red dots and raided-while-away alerts move onto the map badges, and the Map button gets a dot when any place needs you. Locked towns and towns ready to found stay on the map with their Start sheet.
  - First time: a short explainer on the Map button. Update GDD.md (Switching, empire map), the goal strip line that says to tap Iron Ridge at the top, smoke, shots and What's new.

The top left is the place bar (`#placeBar` in `src/ui/play.js`): the town's name, Map (moved out of the side column) and a back button with an arrow and the last place's short name (`#backBtn`, aria-label "Back to Dry Creek"), all on one row. The core keeps the last place: `game.prev`, set by `game.go`, and `game.back` (the last place while it is open and not in play); save v13 adds it, an old save starts with none. Map shows once the map is open, which is now also when a town to start is in sight with only one town open (`mapOpen`); Map carries a dot when another place lost and holds its waves, was raided while you were elsewhere, or a town is ready to start, and the back button carries one for its own place. The map badges gain a `raided` alert (raiders broke in while you were elsewhere, until you go there) with a card line. Decision: a device with no 3D view has no map, so there Map opens a Places sheet (Go to each town, and each town in sight opens its Start sheet). Decision: with a back button beside it the name is the short one ("Dry Creek", not "Dry Creek Outpost"), so name, Map and back fit at 400 px; tighter padding under 480 px; in landscape the place bar has its own row so it never squeezes the wave chip. Goals that send you elsewhere spotlight the back button when it goes there, else Map (`at().go`, which the newcomer bot reads): "Tap Map, Copper Gulch, then Play Copper Gulch" and "Tap Map, Iron Ridge, then Start Iron Ridge" (the strip holds 48 characters), and the Ridge's Toolsmith goal is "Go to Copper Gulch and build a Toolsmith there". Explainers: "Go back" on the back button (new, replaces "Your towns"), the Map card now says how to go to a town and what its red dot means, and "A new town" points at Map. Found on the way: a tower's tap post in front of another empty pad always won the tap (a pad half off the top of a landscape screen opened the tower in front); now, as with a plot, the middle nearer the finger wins (`pickTarget`). The screenshot-only review rewrote the back card (it said "this button"), the goal lines (they named Start, the button reads Start Iron Ridge) and the first What's new line, and the map card of a town ready to start now says Ready to start, not Not open yet. Left as kept by earlier reviews: a toast over Hold waves right after Start, "Vault 100%", and the Dry Creek pin under the map card. Tests: `test/places.test.js` (5: the back button both ways, founding keeps it, the save and the v12 migration, the raided badge, goal spotlights); goal, loco, ridge-bot and town tests updated. Smoke: the place bar on one row, inside the screen, with nothing cut short, in the Gulch, Dry Creek, Iron Ridge and Pine Hollow at every size; the back button both ways; Places and its Start sheet; Map opens the map and its Start card founds Iron Ridge. Shots add the Go back card and the Iron Ridge card. Checks: 222 tests, audit ok, balance ok (Dry Creek won 8.4-9.6 min, Gulch wave 10 7.6-7.9 min, charter offered 41.7-44 min, Ridge founded 66.7-75.4 min by strategy median, Ridge wave 10 7.7-8.5 min, Boiler Shop 97-100%, variety ok), firstplay ok (newcomer Dry Creek 12.6 min, Gulch wave 10 10.3 min), smoke all ok twice, perf Gulch 18.5 ms and the empire gate 18.7 ms (96 and 109 draw calls), update and install tests ok.
