A thin bar on the label shows raiders left in a fight or the countdown.
  - The town's map card says it in words ("Fighting wave 23, 8 raiders left", "Next wave in 24 sec", "Waves on hold. Tap Play to go back.", for a won outpost "Won. Light raids keep coming every 40 sec. Your towers hold them."). A won outpost's own wave bar says Raids, not a stuck wave number.
  - Owner decision: won outposts keep it simple (light raids at the winning strength, no boss, no climb, no drop back). Only make it visible, and say so in GDD.md. Labels stay small and fixed width. Screenshots of every state, and two map shots a few seconds apart to prove nothing jumps. games/CLAUDE.md has the lasting rule "Every place shows its state from the map".
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.20-18. `siteState(game, id)` in `src/core/map.js` (pure): fight (raiders left of the wave's size), next (timer), raids (a won outpost's countdown), hold (needs: the player must go there; not while Keep trying the boss waits for Auto-towers) and wait (out of play with no tower, so its clock is stopped). A wave keeps its size (`waves.size`, set in `startWave`; save v21 migrates a wave on the road). Words and marks in `src/ui/state.js`: a third label row with a 7x7 pixel mark (swords pulsing gently, a clock, a pause sign, a flag) and one word in a fixed 10ch tabular cell (Fighting, Next in 0:24, On hold, Retry soon, Raid in 1:52, No towers), red when the town needs the player, and a 3 px bar along the label's bottom that fills as raiders fall or the countdown runs (the countdown's full length is seen in the UI when it starts). A crowded label keeps the mark and the word on one row beside its name. The word cell is 12ch; the income cell has a fixed 9ch width so a label keeps one size as rates change. Decisions: a hold that Keep trying the boss will lift by itself reads Retry soon in gray, so On hold always means go there; a won outpost shows Fighting during a raid, like any town; the clock caps at 9:59 (breaks are minutes). **Card**: the waves in one sentence with the wave number ("Fighting wave 23, 8 raiders left.", "Wave 24 comes in 24 sec.", "Wave 18 is on hold until you tap Resume waves there.", "Wave 1 waits until you build a tower here."), and for a won outpost "Won. Light raids keep coming every 2 min. Your towers hold them." over "Next raid in 1 min 52 sec." The Wave cell left the card's grid, so the number is said once; a loss adds no state line, since its own line says what happened. **Wave bar**: a won outpost's wave number reads Raids and its sheet "Won. Light raids keep coming and your towers hold them." (`waveTitle` in `src/ui/wave.js`). GDD.md (Empire map) has the label row and the card's words; the owner's decision on won outposts was already there. Checks: `test/mapstate.test.js`; smoke "map states" reads every open place's label twice 2 s apart (a known word and a mark, Raids at a won outpost, no change in size); `tools/shots.js` adds map-states, map-states2 (fails the run if a label changed size) and map-raids. No explainer: the labels were taught with the map, and this adds a row to them, not a new feature. A screenshot-only review of this release led to Retry soon, the word kept on crowded labels, Next in and Raid in ("Raids 1:56" read as a score) and red have lines on short Capital prices; the rest is in the box below. Checks: 476 tests, audit ok, smoke phone, land and desk ok (twice, after a first run caught a broken regex in the new map check), perf ok (Gulch 22 ms, 7.3 ms script, 96 draw calls; empire 22.6 ms, 8.1 ms script, 109 draw calls; falls-empire 27.2 ms, 9.4 ms script, 104 draw calls, 70k triangles), balance ok and idle ok (see Gate margins), shots ok with no label changing size between map-states and map-states2. Firstplay was not rerun (no change to the simulation beyond counting a wave's size). Not yet tried on a real phone.

The Goods sheet's pill is now "Save for towers" with two plain buttons, 2 upgrades and 4 upgrades, the picked one gold (`STOCK_CHOICES`, `.seg`), and the building cards' switch has the same name (`STOCK_LABEL`), so it is one idea in two places. Research's line-up button says Line up (then Up next). The outpost research line ends "Go to Copper Gulch to research." The retry line says what happened first ("The war wagon got through. Once a tower goes up a level, Copper Gulch fights the war wagon again."). **One loss, one telling**: the toast away names only the town and where to go ("Copper Gulch lost wave 20. Tap Map to go there."), the map card says what happened. **Income**: a row too small for one step reads "a little" in every size (`LITTLE` in `src/ui/format.js`), and the Income sheet is titled Coins a second, which teaches the /s on the coins. Decision: the coins keep "+3.91K/s" in their fixed-width cell, since the Coins a second sheet is one tap away. **Perks**: After signing shows only the coins ("100% more coins"); the deeds signing pays are in the list above it. **Goods**: the price bar is gone, since it had no label and said what "Full price" or "Down 60%" says. **Mortar**: a shell's landing mark is a dark spot that grows as it falls, like its shadow, not a red ring that cut the Mortar's own ring, and the Mortar explainer says "A dark spot shows where each one will land." **Map**: a red dot means a problem the label does not name (a short input, a low vault); On hold says itself in red with no dot; the Capital and a place ready to start get a gold dot. The label's bar has a visible track, and the map explainer says "The thin bar under a town fills as its wave goes on." **Raiders and the vault**: the first explainer says "They ride down the road to rob you" (it shows first in Dry Creek, which has a stockpile), and the Gulch's vault explainer says the vault works "like the stockpile in Dry Creek". Decisions under the new rule (no third rewording): "Down 99%", the stock buttons, "Won last time", the map dots, "a little" and Line up stay as they shipped here, since each answers an earlier review and the reviewer's fixes went back to words already tried; the level pills and Next in 0:31 are the owner's quiet-label and fixed-width rules; a card's last switch below the fold scrolls into view. ### M5b. The look pass (owner request, 2026-11-20, after the Silver Falls target)
The owner asked whether the graphics can be better, in towns and on the empire map, and chose to do it once M5's target passes, before M6 (GDD.md, Look and sound).
- [x] **Towns, part 1: ground, frame and pads.** Ground with subtle color and sand ripples (shader noise or vertex colors, no outside textures), darker packed dirt round buildings, scrub and grass tufts, and ruts and edge stones on the road. Low hills, cliffs or a dry creek round each board, so the town sits in a valley. Wooden platforms or stone footings for the pads, and soft contact shadows. From the review of 2026.11.20-17: on a phone the home view cuts the outermost pads at the left and right edges (every outpost, Iron Ridge, Silver Falls) and the south ones under the goods strip, and a few pads look pressed against a building (Pine Hollow's Logging Camp, the Ridge's Quarry) or the road's edge. Widen the frames or tighten the footings so every pad reads whole and apart.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-2. Decision: the box was too big for one release, so it is split in three (ground, frame and pads here, then life, then buildings that grow). **Ground**: `src/render/dress.js` adds sand ripples (a lit crest and a shaded trough) and fine grains in the ground's own shader (`groundDetail`, through `onBeforeCompile`, no textures and no extra draw call); a per-vertex `rip` keeps the ripples off yards, pads, the road and riverbanks. Darker patches smaller than the dunes, and a ragged band of packed dirt round every yard. Grass tufts gather on the road's verges, the riverbanks and the yards' edges (few in the open), and small flat stones run along both edges of the road, all merged with the other scenery. **Frame**: a rim of rock ledges in the town's mesa colors round each board (`rim`), tall behind it where the entrances are and lower at the sides, standing on the ground there (a first try set them at height 0, so on the hills only their tips showed, like shards). It is four meshes, one a side, and the side the camera looks across sinks to a third, like an entrance, so it never hides a pad. **Pads**: a stone footing (eight cut blocks round four flagstones, still 0.5 across) in place of the gray octagon; the goal text keeps "stone pad". Soft contact shadows (one instanced mesh, `Blobs`) under every pad, the vault and every built plot. Every pad now stands at least 1.15 from the road's middle (it was 1.0, so a footing sat on the road's light edge); 26 pads moved 0.05-0.15 outward, Pine Hollow's first pad moved 0.35 back from the Logging Camp and its pad 3 0.2 up, and six pads 0.1 along so they stay 1.5 apart. A saved tower keeps its index, so no save change. Every building model is scaled to stay within 0.96 of its plot's middle (`FIT`; only the Logging Camp reached past, 1.09). **Home view**: a town's frame grows to take in every pad, the goods strip's top is the frame's lower edge, the smallest pad is 31 px (was 32, the touch rule is 30, and taps already take a pad within 24 px), and when the town is wider than the screen the view slides sideways up to 3 units so no pad is cut by the edge (`unclip` in `src/render/camera.js`). Decision: on a phone the Gulch, the Ridge and the Falls are still wider than the screen at that pad size, so the far column is a pan away (GDD.md, Camera framing); the outposts now fit whole, and a cut building plot at the edge is left as is, since only pads were counted. **Found on the way**: smoke caught the Ridge's goal tag one frame past the screen's edge after the slide; a tag's text and style now change before it is placed (`src/ui/play.js`). Two fresh-eyes reviews of before and after shots (`tools/look-shots.js`, new) judged the after set better and led to the rim on the ground with mixed sizes, darker shadows and dirt, fewer tufts, props kept 1.35 from pads, the model fit and the slide. Words review: the What's new lines say "stone pads" as the game does; it also flagged the landscape "Sell 60" (moved to the box below) and the dim title footer. Checks: 478 tests, audit ok, smoke phone, land and desk ok, perf ok (Gulch 25.1 ms, 8.7 ms script, 99 draw calls; empire 26.1 ms, 8.5 ms script, 115 draw calls; falls-empire 28 ms, 9.5 ms script, 112 draw calls, 84k triangles, was 70k), balance ok (Gulch wave 10 at 7.2-8.2 min; Falls founded at 6.63-7.79 h; Falls wave 10 7.7-8.3 min; variety ok), firstplay ok (Dry Creek won 12.5 min newcomer median against 9.4 careful; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1), idle ok on all 6 seeds, update and install ok. Gates within 5%: economy's Gulch wave 10 now exactly 25% behind the best (limit 25%, was 24%), the economy Cannon in the Falls at 35.6% (limit 36%), the Capital at 231 min on seed 1. Not yet tried on a real phone.
- [x] **Towns, part 2: life.** A little life, pooled and cheap: townsfolk walking between buildings, a horse and wagon on the road, tumbleweeds, birds. Nothing stands on a pad or blocks a tap (pick.js ignores them), and perf stays in budget on the med tier.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-3. `src/render/life.js` draws it and `src/render/life-plan.js` (no three.js, so `node --test` reaches it) plans it. **Townsfolk**: two instanced meshes (clothes tinted per person, then skin, boots and hat), walking between a door at every built plot and the vault, on walks found by A* on a 0.25 grid that keeps 0.72 from every pad, off every building, the vault, the river (the bridges carry the road) and the entrances, then pulled straight. Decision: they use the road as a street, since raiders are only on it during a wave, and during a wave they hurry indoors and no one comes out. Each keeps to one side of the way, so two on one walk pass instead of standing in each other; a town has at most one more walker than it has doors, so Dry Creek is not a crowd. **Wagon**: a freight wagon (blue bed, cream cover, a pair of horses, wheels turning) rides the road from inside the entrance to a stop before the vault and back; a town opens with it already on the way in. Decision: it is blue and cream so it never reads as the bandits' patched brown boss wagon, and it shrinks away when a wave starts. **Tumbleweeds** roll east in gusts on lanes clear of everything, **birds** circle high and flap, then glide. Low shows up to 3 people, the wagon and one tumbleweed; Medium 8, 2 and 4 birds; High 12, 3 and 6. Nothing is in `board.pickables` or `board.hits`. **Perf**: a full tumbleweed lane search in one frame was a hitch (a raider jump over 3x the median), so lanes are tried once a frame and kept (4 per town, ground heights stored once), a new walk is worked out at most every 0.4 s, and an instanced mesh with no one out draws nothing. A/B runs with the life switched off (`--eval`) read the same (falls-empire script 10.6 and 9.8 ms without it, 10.1 and 10.2 with it), so the Silver Falls script gate was already at its edge (9.5 last release); `Mods.value` and `factor` now keep each stat's sums until a source changes instead of building and sorting a list on every call (same arithmetic, so the bots match), which took `core/mods.js` from 14% to 5% of the game's own time. `test/life.test.js` checks every walk and lane on every board; smoke checks that townsfolk or the wagon are out and that none of it can be tapped. **The screenshot-only words review** flagged "Towns feel alive" (marketing), "Taps go right through all of them" (a manual line with an unclear "them") and the wagon told twice; the What's new lines now say what shows, once each. It also saw people at the road's edge in a shot that read Fighting, which a player could take for raiders: during a wave everyone out of doors now fades away within a second instead of finishing their walk, and people are 15% bigger so they read as people on a phone. Checks: 490 tests, audit ok, smoke phone, land and desk ok, perf ok (Gulch 24.9 ms, 8.7 ms script, 103 draw calls; empire 22.5 ms, 7.3 ms script, 111 draw calls; falls-empire 28.5 and 26.5 ms, 9.9 and 8.9 ms script, 113 draw calls, 85k triangles), balance ok (variety ok, close lines unchanged from last release), firstplay ok (Dry Creek won 12.5 min newcomer median against 9.4 careful; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1), idle ok on all 6 seeds, update and install ok. Not yet tried on a real phone. **Released by the next run**: the run that built it stopped before its commit, so the next run checked everything again (490 tests, audit, smoke, balance, firstplay, idle, update and install all ok, the same numbers). Its Silver Falls script part first read 10.4 ms, then 9.3 and 9.4: `tools/perf.js` retried only a near-miss frame, not a near-miss script part, though the rule above says a close script gate is measured twice, so it now does both. A count of layout reads during the run (`--report`, new) found no per-frame ones; the game's own files are 6% of the profile, the rest is three.js and the browser, so real room on that gate needs fewer objects to draw and fewer page changes a frame (new box, "Room on the Silver Falls script gate").
- [x] **Towns, part 3: buildings that grow.** Visible steps at a few level marks (an extra floor, a sign, lit windows), on top of today's crates, lamps, flag and gold trim, still inside the plot (`FIT` in `src/render/board.js`).
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-4. The same steps on every building (`GROW`, `milestones`, `foundation` and `darkWindows` in `src/assets/models/gulch.js`): at level 10 crates and a barrel; at 25 two lanterns and the windows light up (dark glass before); at 50 the main part of the building (every part taller than a prop standing on the ground) goes up onto two courses of laid stone and grows a course taller, with a flag, while yard props stay on the ground; at 100 a wide gold band over the line between the courses and a gold flag. Moving parts, chimney smoke and lamp lights go up with the building. Decision: a shop sign on its own post was tried and dropped (it read as a gallows or a road barrier, and no recipe says where its wall is). **The screenshot-only review** of the step shots (`tools/look-shots.js` now takes `steps`, every plot at 1, 10, 25, 50 or 100 in turn) found the flag standing by a pad or a neighbor (it was placed at the plot's back corner in the air), lanterns on the barrel and crate, and the two stone courses reading as one slab with a thin gold strip: the flag now stands on the foundation's front corner, the lanterns at the plot's front corners clear of the props, the top course is set back 0.04 a side, and the band is 0.09 tall. Decisions: level 10 and 25 look alike from far above on a phone (the level pill says it), and a pit or a mine with no windows shows 25 by its lanterns only. The run that built this stopped before its commit (the second run in a row to do so), so this run checked it all again. Smoke checks that every building at every mark builds, stands taller from 50, and that some light windows at 25. - [x] **Shadows that stay still (found on the way, 2026.11.21-4).** Raiders cast no sun shadow and have a soft round one under them on every tier (one instanced mesh, `blobs` in `src/render/battle.js`), the buildings' moving parts cast none, and the sun's shadow map is drawn only when a building or tower changes, a tower head turns (at most every third frame), an entrance sinks as the camera turns, the tier changes, and every 60 frames (`src/render/town-view.js`).
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-4. Found while the Silver Falls script gate read red with nothing changed: the shadow pass drew every casting object a second time every frame. A/B: Silver Falls 98-110 draw calls before, 69-71 after, and about 1.5 ms off the frame (see the box below for the real cause of the red gate). The first try hid the raiders' shadows under the road, which pulls itself toward the camera, and a comment swallowed the line that put them in the scene; a new audit rule (`swallowRules`) fails code after a // comment, the second time that bug was written. Smoke checks that raiders have a soft shadow and cast none, that the map is drawn on under half the frames, and that it is drawn again after a level up.

The economy strategy's Gulch wave 10 sits exactly at its 25% limit: look at it before the next change to towers or pads tips it.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-4. The landscape Sell in the card's head shows the coin square before the number, as the coins at the top do. The title's footer sits in a dark pill with light text, and the slot line is bold. The review of this release read the footer as clear. Decisions: "Save for towers" and "Slot" were flagged again; the first is under the rule against a third rewording, and "Slot" names the save slot the Menu shows. The economy Gulch wave 10 is still exactly 25% behind, with no tower change here; the level-20 fork box (part 1) now says to look at it first.
- [x] **Room on the Silver Falls script gate.** `--town falls-empire` reads 8.9-10.4 ms against 10, and the empire map in M5b and Thunder Mesa in M6 will add to it. Find where the time goes (the game's own files are 6% of the profile: look at three.js work per object, matrix updates of still objects, and how many HUD nodes change a frame) and bring the median under 9 ms, so the gate is not a coin toss. Judge it by A/B runs.
  - Notes: done with 2026.11.21-4. **The cause was outside the game**: a headless Edge that an old version of the test driver left running on 2026-11-16 (profile folder `gulch-cdp-`) kept about four cores busy for five days, so every perf reading since then was 3-8 ms high and the Silver Falls script part read 8.9-11.6 ms. With it stopped, Silver Falls reads 20.4-22.1 ms a frame and 5.9-6.8 ms script (Gulch 20.3 ms and 6.4 ms, empire 19.6 ms and 6.1 ms). Lasting fix: `tools/cdp.js` stops its own headless browsers older than 3 hours once a run (it stopped 11 more on its first run), and How the task works says to check the machine's load before judging a gate. Real savings on the way: the still shadows above (A/B on the quiet machine: 98 draw calls and 22.6-23.8 ms a frame with the shadow map drawn every frame, 69-70 and 21.2-22.1 ms as shipped), and no tag writes `hidden` again when it is already hidden (two tags did it every frame). `tools/perf.js --profile` now prints the time by kind (on Silver Falls GPU wait 59%, the browser's own work 20%, three.js 10%, the game's files 7%; about half the browser part grows with pixels, so it is the test browser's software compositing).
- [x] **The empire map.** No beige rectangles under towns, which blend into land that fits each one (pines at Pine Hollow, rocky ridges at Iron Ridge, a river and falls at Silver Falls). Routes drawn as real roads, then rails, with wagons and trains. Varied mesas, drifting cloud shadows, and locked sites shown as surveyed land with stakes and a tent.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-5. **Land**: no plate under any town; the ground round each board takes its town's colors and fades into the desert (80% at most, so a pale board like the Ridge's reads as land; a town not open yet takes a third of that). Each town's land (`FLAVOR` in `src/render/empire-plan.js`): pines at Pine Hollow (and pines in place of cactus near it), red hoodoos at the Gulch, gray crags at the Ridge, flat crescent dunes at Sandy Bend, sage at Dry Creek, reeds on Silver Falls' banks; a river comes over a rounded rock cliff in the east and runs into the Falls' own river, which a locked Falls plot already shows; a dry creek bed winds west from Dry Creek's wash. **Entrances**: each town's entrance stands on the map at about half its board length. Decision: the board's own entrance models (blocks, for the close view) were tried first and the fresh-eyes review read them as crates, a brick fence and a fortress, so the map draws each in rounded rock (`entranceModels` in `src/render/empire-land.js`), with a rock hill and a dark portal where the board's entrance closes (the canyon, the tunnel, the chute). **Routes**: laid by `routeLine`, the shortest curve that leaves each end in one of eight ways and touches no other board, no entrance (measured sizes in `ENT_BOX`, its own too) and not the plaza; every pair is clear (`test/empire.test.js`). A road has stones along its edges and a plank bridge over the river. Decision: routes become rails when the Rail Office is founded (it is the transport institution, and it already has a depot with an engine in the Capital), with ties, steel rails and telegraph poles, and trains (an engine and 2-4 cars, one train for each two wagons' worth) run in place of wagons. **Mesas**: 16, five kinds (mesa, butte, spire, two-step, long ridge), each in the colors of a town within 17 units or the desert's reds. **Clouds**: soft shade drifting across every map material, worked out per vertex (`cloudy` in `empire-land.js`), on every tier since it costs nothing per pixel. **Locked land**: stakes with red tips and string, the sign, a canvas tent, a fire ring and a tripod. `tools/look-shots.js <dir> map` takes an early and a full empire at every size; smoke checks no plate, the camps on locked land, roads before the Rail Office and trains after. Two fresh-eyes reviews of the before and after shots: the first said mixed, leaning better, and led to the rock entrances, the softer tint, red distant mesas, flatter dunes and browner rails; the second is in the target's notes, and led to rocks of mixed shapes in place of one copied shape, a pond where a locked Falls plot's river ends, and rails with wider dark ties that read as track. Words review: "tile", "round" and "wash" left the What's new lines. Left as is: labels still cover parts of the middle of the map on a phone (the badge placement, not the land). Checks: 497 tests, audit ok, smoke phone, land and desk ok, perf ok on a quiet machine (map 19.8 and, on the final build, 21.1 ms, 4.3-5.0 ms script, 56-57 draw calls, 162-165k triangles, was 17.4 ms, 44 and 110k; Gulch 18.5 ms; empire 17.6 ms, 5.0 ms script, 65 draw calls; falls-empire 20.7 ms, 6.0 ms script, 70 draw calls), balance ok (no simulation change; the same close lines: the Capital at 231 min on seed 1, Ridge wave 10 at 7.1 and 7.3 min, economy Cannon in the Falls 35.6%, economy Gulch wave 10 25% behind), firstplay ok (Dry Creek won 12.5 min newcomer median against 9.4 careful; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1), idle ok on all 6 seeds. Not yet tried on a real phone.

Perf on the med tier at phone size, CPU slowed 4x, on a quiet machine (13% load): map 19.8-21.1 ms, Gulch 18.5 ms, empire 17.6 ms, Silver Falls with every town behind it 20.7 ms (limit 33). Nothing in the pass needed the high tier: the clouds are per vertex, the life and the shadows got cheaper, and the steps of growing buildings are merged. The towns were judged better in the reviews of parts 1-3; the empire map's second fresh-eyes review: "AFTER is now clearly better than BEFORE. The towns sit in land that fits them." Left for later boxes: map labels covering the middle of the map on a phone (new box at the top of M6a), and the falls' cliff top reading as a flat gray strip.

### M6a. Late-game depth: the level-20 fork, Call to arms and the market
Split from M6 on 2026-11-21 (the note of 2026-11-20 asked for it once M5b was done): six boxes and two towns would not fit one target, and the fork and Call to arms need their own bot gates before new towns stack on them.
- [x] **Map labels on a phone (two fresh-eyes reviews of 2026.11.21-5).** On a phone the labels cover the Capital, the falls and most of Iron Ridge at the home view. Keep every label small and fixed width (the quiet-labels rule), but move or shrink them so each town's middle and the Capital's dome show; smoke checks that no label covers more than a third of its own town on screen at phone size.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-6. The labels keep their size and words; only their place changed. `spotBadges` in `src/ui/map.js` tries seven places for each label (above or below its own town's box on screen, straight or slid a quarter of the town's width, and its old place over the town's far side) and takes the one that covers the least town ground, never a town's middle or the Capital's dome, never a label already placed, the top bar or the open card, and close to its town; the open card's label goes first, then the nearer towns, and then three passes let each label pick again against all the others, so one placed early moves aside for one it boxed in. A label keeps its side unless another is clearly better, so labels do not flip as the map moves. `liftBadges` still runs after it as a safety net. The map view gives each place's box on screen (`siteBox` in `src/render/empire-view.js`: the board with its entrance, the plaza with the city's height, or a surveyed plot). Decision: the check counts every label over a place, not only its own, since a label over the next town is the same problem. `CG.mapui.cover()` measures it; `tools/look-shots.js <dir> map` prints it for every shot, and smoke fails a share over a third or a middle under a label at the home view, before and after the Capital is built. Before: the Capital's dome and Silver Falls sat under labels on a phone; after: every home view on a phone covers at most 27% of any place (the top of Iron Ridge, with every town open), most 0-9%, and no middle. Left as is: in a low view across the map in landscape, Iron Ridge at the horizon is a sliver under the top bar and the labels cover 62% of that sliver. Checks: 501 tests, audit ok, smoke phone, land and desk ok (with the new label check), perf ok after stopping two orphan browsers (Gulch 17.9 ms, 5.1 ms script, 70 draw calls; empire 17.8 ms, 5.0 ms, 66; falls-empire 19.0 ms, 5.2 ms, 69 draw calls, 64k triangles), balance ok (variety ok; close lines unchanged: the Capital at 231 min on seed 1, Ridge wave 10 at 7.1 and 7.3 min, the economy Cannon in the Falls 35.6%, economy Gulch wave 10 25% behind), firstplay ok (Dry Creek 12.5 min newcomer median against 9.4 careful; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1), idle ok, update and install ok. New `tools/news-shot.js` takes the What's new card as a returning player sees it, for the words review. **The screenshot-only review** found labels that now sat nearer another town than their own and read as its name (Silver Falls over the Gulch's loop, the Capital inside Iron Ridge's ring, Copper Gulch on Dry Creek): a label now pays heavily for sitting within 14 px of another place nearer than its own, the far side (where every label sat before) is preferred, the box takes in the town's entrance, and labels no longer go beside a town (a second screenshot-only review found Copper Gulch's label out on the land by Silver Falls, read as that plot's name). `cover()` lists labels nearer another place and `tools/look-shots.js` prints them; decision: smoke gates the box's own measure (a third of a place, no middle) and not "nearer", since on a phone the Gulch and Dry Creek sit so close that every spot touches the other's entrance. `test/spots.test.js` checks the measure and the nearer rule on a model layout. `tools/look-shots.js map` has a third state, the smoke test's map with the Capital built. It also flagged "the falls" in What's new (now "Silver Falls"). Left as is: "Next in 0:43" (the state cell is fixed at 12 characters, and the town's card says "Next wave in"), "Won · Sends goods · On hold" (only the shots hold every outpost; a won outpost in play reads Raid in), and "Take a contract" (the Capital's contracts are named on its card).
- [x] **Second fork at level 20, part 1: the Snake Oil Still (owner idea, 2026-11-20).** At level 20 a tower picks one of two more upgrades on top of its level-5 path, so it has 4 ways to finish, with both sides shown before the pick, as at level 5. The Still first: Slow Rot (a weak poison that never wears off until the raider falls, best at the start of the road) or a strong burst for the end of the road. The core (the fork level, the pick, the stats), the card's choice, the bots' pick, an explainer the first time a tower reaches it, and a save migration if the tower's shape changes. Check with the bots that towers reach level 20 around the Capital or Silver Falls, and move the level if not. First look at the economy strategy's Gulch wave 10, which sits exactly at its 25% limit (2026.11.21-2 to -4). Gates: the strategy bots stay within about 25% of each other and each new option is the best buy somewhere.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-7. **Level**: the bots' Ridge stands at level 17-21 when Silver Falls is founded (7.3-7.8 h) and the Falls' own towers reach 16 an hour after, so the fork stays at 20 (`FORK_LEVEL`, `FORKS` and `NEAR` in `src/data/towers.js`). **Slow Rot** trades the normal poison and 30% of the hit for a poison worth 7% of the hit damage a second that never wears off (`f.rot`, apart from the 4-second dose so a normal Still cannot cut it short; Spreading Fever passes it on as a normal dose). **Last Call** keeps its poison and hits 0.4 times as hard at the lane's start up to 3 times at the vault (`nearFactor`). Decision: both are measured on the whole tower, not on the poison alone, since by level 20 the hit is about 8 times the poison. The numbers come from runs of the waves on the active bot's late Ridge and Falls with one level-20 Still moved from pad to pad: at 14% Slow Rot won every pad, at 7% with Last Call at 3x it wins the first pads (Ridge pad 10: 2.0M against 0.37M) and Last Call wins from about the middle on (Ridge pad 5: 2.75M against 1.41M, and 5 fewer leaks in 4 waves). **The card**: a level-19 Still shows "Pick an upgrade" with both sides and one price, as at level 5; after it the card is named for the fork and lists its path, "Poison lasts · Until the raider falls" or "Near the vault · Up to 3 times the damage". The model gets a green vat (Slow Rot) or red bottles (Last Call). The damage breakdown names the fork. **Auto-towers** never picks a fork: a level-19 Still waits like a level-4 tower, and the Auto sheet says to tap it. **Explainer** `fork` on the first tower one level short; the audit counts the fork as a feature. **Save v22**: towers get `fork: null`, raiders `rot: 0`. **Bots**: `options` offers both forks, `worth` counts Slow Rot over the road left after the pad (`along`, `ROT_LIVES` 1: at .5 it picked Last Call on 34 of 36 Ridge pads, against the runs) and Last Call by where its reach sits; `forkCheck` puts a level-19 Still of each path on every pad of the Ridge and the Falls at the end of each active run and fails a side that is the best pick nowhere: Slow Rot 3-7 and Last Call 29-33 of 36 in the Ridge, 9 and 29 of 38 in the Falls, on all 6 seeds. The active bots took one fork on the way (Last Call). `test/fork.test.js`: the pick, the stats, rot that never wears off, Last Call by distance, each side the best buy at its end of a straight road, the bots' options, Auto-towers waiting, the migration and the explainer. Smoke picks Slow Rot through the card with two taps. **The screenshot-only words review** found the What's new lines said "Build it" for a tower already built and "one more choice" with no tower in view (now "Tap a level 19 Snake Oil Still to pick Slow Rot or Last Call" and "Best near..."), and that Slow Rot's softer hit was not said before the pick (its line now starts "Softer hits and"). Left as is: "105/s poison" (the game's form for poison on every Still card) and the landscape tower card whose cost bar covers the lower stats until it scrolls (every tower card, not the fork). Found on the way: the yard's cap trims goods set by a test before the tap, so the smoke check pauses the town. The economy Gulch wave 10 is unchanged at 25% behind (this box does not touch the first hour); economy's payback at 300 s instead of 400 was tried and was worse (8.9 against 8.6 min). Checks: 513 tests, audit ok, smoke phone, land and desk ok, perf ok (Gulch 19.7 ms, 6.1 ms script, 70 draw calls; empire 18.6 ms, 5.5 ms, 65; falls-empire 21.2 ms, 6.1 ms, 69 draw calls, 64k triangles), balance ok (variety ok; Falls founded 6.63-7.79 h; Falls wave 10 7.7-8.3 min; close lines unchanged: the Capital at 231 min on seed 1, Ridge wave 10 at 7.1 and 7.3 min, economy Cannon in the Falls 35.6%, economy Gulch wave 10 25% behind), firstplay ok (Dry Creek won 12.5 min newcomer median against 9.4 careful; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1), idle ok on all 6 seeds, update and install ok. Not yet tried on a real phone.
- [x] **The Still late in the game (found with part 1).** The active bot builds no Still in the Ridge and its Stills do 4% of the damage in the Falls (economy 4%, defense 7%), so the fork it now has is reached once in six runs. Its poison grows 1.15 a level against 1.3 for its hit, so by level 15 the poison that was its job (getting past armor) is a small part of it. Give the Still a late job the bots value (poison growth, the armored share of late waves, or its goods), so it is the best buy somewhere after the first hour too, and check the strategies stay within 25% and no kind passes 36%.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-8, from the branch `wip-still-armor` (try 5: half the armor from level 10) plus one change. The three late misses had one cause: with poison that eats armor the active bot keeps 2-3 Stills in Iron Ridge to level 18-20, and their next two levels kept thousands of iron in the reserve, which the Blast Furnace then could not use, so the Capital's contracts (pig iron, steel, rails, boilers) filled slower (16 against 23 on seed 3) and the institutions came later. Decision: the Ridge Still is paid in stone and coal, not iron and coal (no Ridge workshop uses stone). One-seed probes (`empire()` per seed, 2 min each, six side by side) found it in four runs instead of four full bot runs. Bots: balance ok (the tree an upgrade every 10.9-13.3 min, the Wire Mill on Gulch copper 99-100% on every seed, the Falls' economy Cannon 34%), firstplay ok (newcomer 12.5 against 9.4 min for Dry Creek, 10.0 against 8.1 for the Gulch's wave 10), idle ok. Close: economy's Gulch wave 10 25% behind (limit 25%, as before), the Falls' swarm Mortar 35.1%, the Capital at 235 min on seed 1, Ridge wave 10 at 7.1 and 7.3 min, idle seed 6's sales at 93-94% of hour 1. Late in the game the active bot keeps 2-3 Stills at 18-20 in the Ridge and 1-2 at 16 in the Falls, 6-16% of the late damage (none in the Ridge before), and takes Last Call twice in six runs (once before).
  - Earlier notes (2026-11-21, run of 2026.11.21-8, not released): the chosen job is **poison eats armor**. A raider carrying a Still's poison or Slow Rot loses part of its armor against every tower's hits, and the breakdown counts what that adds for the Still. It is built, with tests, on the local branch `wip-still-armor` (core in `hurt` and the cloud in `src/core/defense.js`, `EATS_ARMOR` in `src/data/towers.js`, save v23 with `f.eat`, the card line "Poisoned raiders · Lose half their armor" or "At level 10 · Poison eats armor", the bots' `worth` support term with `EAT_REACH` and `PLAN.level`, `test/eats.test.js`, the GDD line and a What's new entry). The next run starts from that branch. Five tries, each a full bot run: (1) half the armor from level 1: the active bot kept 2-3 Stills to the end of the Ridge and the Falls (none before) and they did 10-17% of the late damage, but minmax's Gulch Still did 37%, seed 2's Ridge wave 10 came at 6.8 min and the newcomer was only 18% slower than the careful player. (2) 3% a level and (3) 20% plus 2% a level: a new Still late in the game ate too little and no bot built one in the Falls. (4) 40% from level 1: four first-hour misses. (5) half from level 10, with the bots counting the share for a Still they mean to raise to 10: the first hour is exactly as before (firstplay ok, idle ok, the Gulch strategy table unchanged), the active bot builds Stills late again (3 at level 18 in the Ridge, 16-21% of its late damage), but balance misses three late gates: an Empire tree upgrade every 17.1 min on seed 3 (want 10-15, the same in tries 4 and 5), the Wire Mill on Gulch copper 78% on seed 1 (want 90%), and the economy Cannon in the Falls at 36%. Next: find why the tree pace on seed 3 slows (coins spent on Still levels in the Ridge, by the look of it) and fix those three, or lower the share to about 40% from level 10. Use `tools/late-towers.js` (new) to see late damage by town.

  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-8 with the Still box. `lateShares` in `tools/balance.js` adds up, over the active seeds, what each kind did after each town's first hour (the Ridge's plays through the Capital and the save for the Falls, and the Falls after its own first hour) and holds it to `SHARE_MAX` with a close line; `test/late-shares.test.js`. Decision: the Falls is counted after its own first hour, the same rule as the Ridge, not past `FALLS_STRATEGY`, since `r.late` already splits there. With the Still change the late shares are the Ridge's Frost Lens 30.2% and the Falls' Frost Lens 34.0% (38-44% before the Still change). The release's bot run started before the gate was written, so it was checked on the same six active runs by a probe script; the next full run prints it.
- [x] **Second fork at level 20, part 2: the Cannon or the Mortar.** A Rocket Battery (a long reach and a slow volley of seeking rockets, good against spread-out raiders) or a heavier shell (one big hit for bosses and bunched raiders), on the same fork as part 1, with the rockets drawn in flight. Later boxes add the other towers one or two at a time. Same gates. The fork card on a landscape phone shows both Choose buttons without scrolling, and a stray bar under the Path row goes (fresh-eyes review of 2026.11.21-8, fork-land and fork-rot shots).
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.21-9. **Which tower**: the Cannon. The bots raise one Cannon to level 20 in Iron Ridge on every seed, while their Mortars stop at 15-17 (`tools/late-towers.js`), so a Mortar fork would seldom be reached; the Mortar waits for a later part. **Rocket Battery** (`FORKS.cannon.a`): a volley of 4 rockets (`ROCKETS`), each at its own raider in reach, the ones nearest the vault first, each 0.65 of the hit with a small blast (0.35 of the Cannon's), 1.6 times the reach and 0.55 times the shots; a rocket finds its raider wherever it has ridden by the time it lands (`sh.foe` in `fight`), and it can hit what a lobbed shell cannot (airships, once Thunder Mesa comes). **Big Shell** (`FORKS.cannon.b`): 2.2 times the hit, half the shots, a quarter wider blast, and twice the damage on any boss (`st.boss`). The card shows "Rockets · 4 at once" or "The war wagon · Twice the damage" (the town's own boss), and a fork can bring its own Best against. **Numbers from runs of the waves**: `tools/fork-runs.js` (new) puts a level-20 Cannon of each side on every pad of the bots' late Ridge and Falls and plays the next waves. At 0.5 of the hit and 1.5 times the reach Big Shell won 31-33 of 36 Ridge pads; at 0.65 and 1.6 the runs split them (Ridge 10/8, 11/7, 10/8 for Rocket Battery on seeds 1-3, the Falls 11/8, 9/10, 10/9), Rocket Battery a little ahead on the mean. **The bots' guess**: `worth` counted every blast on a dense column, so it picked Big Shell on 35 of 36 pads; each rocket now counts `ROCKET_HITS` (1.3) of the raiders its blast would find, which splits the guess 20 of 37 to Rocket Battery and agrees with the runs on 25-26 of 37 pads (`--noruns` tried 1 to 3). **Model**: a rack of four tubes with red rockets, or a fat long barrel; rockets fly on their own arcs, nose first, follow their raider and leave puffs of smoke (pooled). A volley makes one launch sound. **Explainer** `fork-cannon` the first time a Cannon is one level short; the audit asks one per kind with a fork. **Landscape**: on a short screen Choose sits beside each side's name and Can't be undone beside Pick an upgrade, so both Choose buttons show (smoke checks every Choose is inside the card, for the Still and the Cannon). Found on the way: a stat just under one shot a second read "One every 1 seconds" (now "About 1 a second"). No save change (the fork field is from v22; a rocket in the air is a shell with a raider id). Tests: the pick, the stats, a volley at the 4 nearest raiders that each land on their own raider, Big Shell twice on the war wagon, each side the best buy on a test road (raiders far apart, a packed group, wagons), the bots' options and Auto-towers waiting, the explainer. **The screenshot-only words review** led to "you pick" in both fork tips (the tower does not pick), Big Shell's Best against without the war wagon (its own row says it), and "Reloads slowly" in place of "slow shot". Left as is: "Path · Heavy Cannon" (the game's word for the level-5 choice), "/s poison" and Last Call's line (part 1, no third rewording), the half-cut Sell button and the stats under the price bar (new box below). Checks: 525 tests, audit ok, smoke phone, land and desk ok, perf ok on a quiet machine (Gulch 18.1 ms, 5.5 ms script, 70 draw calls; empire 22.0 ms, 6.7 ms, 65; falls-empire 21.3 ms, 6.6 ms, 71 draw calls, 64k triangles; the Gulch with its Cannons as level-20 Rocket Batteries 18.6 ms and 91 draw calls with rockets and smoke in the air), balance ok (Falls founded 6.89-7.77 h, Falls wave 10 7.7-8.3 min, variety ok; the fork check: Rocket Battery 28 of 36 Ridge pads and 28 of 38 Falls pads, Big Shell 8 and 10, on every seed, with both level-5 paths counted; the active bots took Rocket Battery six times; late damage: the Ridge frost 30%, mortar 25%, cannon 20%, the Falls frost 33%, mortar 23%, cannon 19%; close lines as before: the Capital at 235 min on seed 1, Ridge wave 10 at 7.1 and 7.3 min, the Falls' economy Cannon 34.5%, economy's Gulch wave 10 25% behind, minmax 21%), firstplay ok (Dry Creek 12.5 min newcomer median against 9.4 careful; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1), idle ok on all 6 seeds (close: seed 1's Capital at 3.92 h, seeds 1 and 4's hours 7-12 growth, seed 6's sales at 93-94%), update and install ok. **Found on the way**: smoke's sweep for leftover browsers stopped the browser of a screenshot run going at the same time, since Edge's main process outlives its launcher and the parent test called it an orphan; the screenshot run hung for half an hour and smoke failed once on a card check it shared the machine with. `tools/cdp.js` now names the driver in each browser's folder (`cg-cdp-p<pid>-`) and stops one only when that driver is gone. Not yet tried on a real phone.

Flagged twice, so change the layout, not the words: for example three stats to a row, or the price in the card's head as Sell is. Batch it with the next box's release.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-1 with Call to arms. The layout changed, not the words. **Sell** sits in the card's head at every size ("Sell ■ 450K" beside Close), so the Sell row that was cut under the bar is gone. **The pinned bar** holds only the price and Upgrade: what a buy is short of ("You will have enough copper ore in about 9 minutes. Upgrade the Copper Mine...") moved out of it into the card, over the bar. **Landscape**: three stats to a row, and a tower card is as wide as the path card (min(54%, 480px)), so its name and stats fit on one line each; on a short landscape screen the short-of-goods note shows its first sentence only (how long), since the red have in the price bar says what is short. The note is a little smaller (.85rem). Smoke checks, on level-20 Big Shell, Rocket Battery and Slow Rot cards short of goods and a level-3 Crossbow, at phone, landscape and desktop sizes: Sell whole in the head, every stat row above the bar, no paragraph half under it. Shots before and after: on a phone the Big Shell card now fits whole (before, Sell was half under the bar), and in landscape every stat sits above it (before, the bar took half the card).
- [x] **Call to arms (owner idea, 2026-11-20).** A button in each town: its production stops and the workers come out as a militia that fights along the road for about 30-60 sec, then a cooldown of several minutes shown as a timer. It opens late through the War Office tree at the Capital, and Auto never uses it unless the player turns on a switch for it.   - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-1. **Opens** with the War Office's third upgrade, the Armory ("Towers hit 15% harder. Every town can call its workers to arms."), in every town and outpost with a built building (`armsOpen`, `ARMS.opens`). Decision: an existing upgrade rather than a sixth one, so no tree save changes and the bots reach it (the Armory costs 25 Influence; all 6 active seeds buy it between the Capital and Silver Falls). **A call** (`callToArms`, `src/core/arms.js`, data in `src/data/arms.js`) stops every building (`produce` makes nothing) and sends two workers from each built building to a post by the road next to it (`armsPosts`, the road point nearest the plot) for 40 sec; the town can call again 5 min after the call. Each post shoots once a second, at a boss in reach first (decision: at the raider nearest the vault it barely touched the war wagon, which comes last), for 0.035 of a plain raider's health on that wave times (level / 10) to the 0.4, at most 3 times. Its damage is kept apart from the towers' (`game.armsDealt`). A wave played away is never helped (the background battle's copy has no militia). **Cost shown before the tap**: the To arms side button opens a sheet with Workers from (buildings), You lose (coins of goods: what the buildings would make in 40 sec at today's prices, less what the town's own workshops would use up), They fight for, Call again after, and the Call to arms button; while out or waiting the sheet says so and the button shows the time on a second line in a fixed m:ss cell, gold while they fight. Workers are drawn (`src/render/militia.js`, one instanced mesh, every tier, never picked) with quick tracers, one rifle sound a volley and the boss horn on a call; a toast says when they are back at work. **Auto** has its own switch, Off: it calls when the vault is under half, or a boss is past 40% of its road with over 30% of its health (decision: calling at every boss wave stopped the buildings for waves the towers win, and the idle bot's growth fell). **Explainer** `arms` with a spotlight on To arms. **Save v24**: `town.arms`. **Tests** (`test/arms.test.js`): opens with the Armory; a post per plot; a call stops production, then the cooldown; the lost goods; the militia grows with levels; a town with no towers never holds (more raiders get through than fall, on 15 waves in the Gulch and the Ridge at about the building levels it has then); at the Gulch's wave-20 gate with raiders 1.1-1.4 times tougher the militia, called when the war wagon is out, stops it where the towers alone do not, and never makes it worse; never in a wave played away; Auto only with its switch and only as above; save and migration. **Bots**: the active bot calls it whenever a boss is on the road (`ARMS_BOT`, `--noarms` for an A/B). `armsCheck` in `tools/balance.js`: every seed reaches it and calls it; Iron Ridge 4-9 calls a seed, 31 of 38 of those boss waves won; the Falls 3-4 calls, 17 of 19 won; the militia does 3.5-6.7% of the Ridge's late damage and 0.1-2.6% of the Falls' (limit 12%). Balance ok (Falls founded 6.83-7.37 h, Falls wave 10 7.7-8.3 min, variety ok, late damage frost 29% and 32%); firstplay ok (newcomer 12.5 min against 9.4 careful for Dry Creek; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1). **Idle, decided in the open**: with Auto Call to arms on, idle seed 1 lost no boss wave (4 without) and beat 38 (32 without), but earned 1.8% less over 12 hours, and since it won early its hours 7-12 earned 18.04M against hours 1-6's 18.16M, under the growth gate. Under both, the idle Gulch sits held at wave 49-50 from hour 3. Auto Call to arms is a boss tool that pays goods for wins, not an income tool, so `tools/idle.js` leaves it Off (`--arms` turns it on), and a new box below fixes the idle Gulch wall and turns it back on. Idle ok on all 6 seeds that way (close: seed 1 18.66M against 18.20M, seed 4 15.62M against 15.20M). You can call them again 5 min after."), "Goods lost · About 11.6K coins" on the sheet, the sheet titled To arms like its button (the big button stays Call to arms), Big Shell's row "Against the war wagon · Twice the damage", and no Path row on a level-20 card ("Path · Heavy Cannon" was flagged a second time, so under the no-third-rewording rule the row goes; the model shows the path). Left as is: the goal "Tap Research and start one" and Best against reading "Bunched-up raiders" on a Cannon against "Big groups" on Big Shell (older words, not this release's), the two-line Rocket Battery name on a phone, and the landscape card's size. - [x] **The idle Gulch wall (found with Call to arms, 2026.11.21-10).** In the 12-hour idle run the Gulch sits held at wave 49-50 from about hour 3 on seed 1 (and the growth gate on seeds 1 and 4 has been within 5% for several releases), while Auto-towers raises its towers and Keep trying the boss waits. Find why the climb stops there (the wave-50 boss against Auto-towers' cheapest-first raises, the Gulch's reserve, or coins going to the Ridge) and fix it so the idle growth gate has room, then run `tools/idle.js --arms` and turn Auto Call to arms back on in the idle run if the gate holds.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-2. **The cause**: Keep trying the boss held a town's waves after every loss until a tower went up a level, and from about hour 3 of the idle run a raise took 15-60 minutes (6 of the Gulch's 9 towers wait for a hand pick, a path at level 4 or a fork at 19, and the rest cost half the shared purse), so the Gulch sat on hold 45-60 minutes an hour and earned no bounty (a probe of held minutes, losses, bounties and raises by town and hour, seeds 1, 2 and 4). Goods were not short: the reserve held 5-10 times what the next raise needed. **The fix**: with the switch on a town never sits on hold after a loss. It plays the wave before the lost one again and again, for its bounty, and tries the lost wave again once a tower has gone up a level (`retry` and `againWave` in `src/core/automation.js`). Decision: it takes up every loss, in play too (an in-play vault never held, so the town in play fell again and again at its wall), learned from the 'breach' event; a loss on the wave it plays again moves the try down to that wave, so no wave it cannot beat is played in a loop. A town held after a loss plays on once the switch is on (old saves). Words: the Auto sheet says "Stays at wave 46 until a tower goes up a level", the map card "Copper Gulch lost wave 47. It stays at wave 46 until a tower goes up a level.", the toast and the lost card "Your waves stay at wave 49 until a tower goes up a level. Then the war wagon comes back.", the switch "After a loss, this town keeps playing and tries again by itself.", and the explainer says it plays the wave before again and again. **The screenshot-only words review** found "plays wave 19 again" beside "Fighting wave 18" on the same screen (two wave numbers, since the town climbs back first), so the lines say "stays at"; the What's new lines used a switch name the game does not show and "waits on hold", now an example with wave 20 and 19. Left as is: the landscape Auto sheet cuts the last line until it scrolls (a scrolling sheet), and the lost card with the switch off does not name the switch (the retry explainer does, once). Map alerts: Retry soon while it plays again, Retrying once a tower went up, the red dot when stuck. Save v25: `auto.fell` and `boss.go`. **Idle**: every seed's idle hours 7-12 now earn 27-61% more than hours 1-6 (before 1-5%, seeds 1 and 4 within 5% of the gate for several releases): 22.1M to 32.8M, 18.9M to 30.4M, 19.3M to 25.3M, 15.7M to 19.9M, 16.5M to 21.5M, 15.5M to 20.1M. With Auto Call to arms on (`tools/idle.js --arms`) it holds as well (+26% to +61%, 0-2 boss waves lost a seed), so the idle run now turns it on (`--noarms` for an A/B). Left as is: the idle Gulch still stops at waves 42-51 by hour 12, since its towers that wait for a path or a fork are the player's to pick; the Ridge, in play, still falls 5-9 times an hour at its wall. Tests: `test/retry.test.js` rewritten (no hold, wave 9 again pays its bounty and never brings the war wagon, a raise brings the try, one loss per try, the in-play vault moves the try down, the words, the migration); the review test that pinned "On hold until..." now checks the reason words. **Found on the way, the two flaky smoke checks of 2026.11.22-1**: the printed boxes showed the cause of each. The phone tower card over the goods strip: opening a card adds its goods to the strip, which wraps to a second row (84 px), and `--striph` was measured only 5 times a second, so for up to 0.2 s the card sat over the new row; a ResizeObserver on the strip now sets it in the same frame (`src/ui/play.js`). The map labels off screen on rotate: a label shown for the first time was placed with a guessed 80 px width until the next 0.25 s measure, so one by the edge stuck out; it is now measured when it first shows (`placeBadges` in `src/ui/map.js`). Smoke passed phone, land, desk and rotate after each. Checks: 539 tests, audit ok, balance ok (no change for the careful bots, which never use the switch: Falls founded 6.83-7.37 h, close lines as before: the Capital at 235 min on seed 1, Ridge wave 10 at 7.1 and 7.3 min, the Falls' swarm Mortar 35.5% and economy Cannon 34.6%, economy's Gulch wave 10 25% behind, minmax 21%), firstplay ok (Dry Creek 12.5 min newcomer median against 9.4 careful; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1), idle ok on all 6 seeds (close: seed 1's Capital at 3.92 h, seed 6's sales at 92-94% of hour 1), smoke phone, land, desk and rotate ok, perf ok on a quiet machine (Gulch 20.6 ms, 5.9 ms script, 91 draw calls; empire 18.4 ms, 5.3 ms, 65; falls-empire 20.3 ms, 5.7 ms, 70 draw calls, 64k triangles), update and install ok. Not yet tried on a real phone.
- [x] **Market depth, part 1: booms and busts and sell modes** (GDD.md, Market). One good at a time booms or busts every 3 to 5 minutes, shown on its goods chip and the Goods sheet with the time left; each good gets a sell mode (Off, Always, Skip busts, Booms only), Always by default, and every mode keeps the tower reserve. An explainer the first time a boom shows. The test the M6a target names: no buy-and-sell loop earns coins. Bots: the idle and newcomer players leave every good on Always, so the pacing targets must hold with no tuning.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-3. **Booms and busts** (`BOOM`, `boomTick` in `src/core/market.js`): a new one every 180-300 s from the start of the last, on a good some town or outpost makes (`boomable`, never power, never the same good twice in a row while there is another), lasting 90 s; a boom is 1.6 times the price, a bust 0.6 (45% of them). The market has its own random stream in its save, so a town's luck never moves it, and catch-up chunks give the same booms as fine steps. Foremen leave booms out (`marginal`): a level lasts, a boom does not. **When they start, decided in the open**: first 8 minutes into a new game, the balance run failed two strategy gates (minmax's Gulch wave 20 38% behind, economy's Ridge wave 10 30%). An A/B probe (`tools/boom-probe.js`, booms on and off, same seeds) showed why: minmax's wave 20 splits in two on the same seeds, 16-19 min when it beats the war wagon at the first try and 21-24 min when it loses, and any change to its coins flips a seed (more boss prep does nothing, so coins bind); and the Ridge gate was a median of 3 seeds that moves about 10% with any change (over 6 seeds, economy reads 12% behind without booms and 15% with them). So the clock runs only once a second town is open (`boomsOpen`): the first boom comes 8 min after Iron Ridge is founded, the first hour plays as before, and the market depth comes with the second town's goods, which is the late-game depth this milestone is for. The Ridge strategy gate now takes 6 seeds (`RIDGE_SEEDS`, the limit stays 25%), and minmax's wave 20 gets its own box below. **Sell modes** (`SELL_MODES`, `sellMode`, `setSellMode`): kept in `town.sell` as before (false is Off, no entry is Always, plus 'skip' and 'boom'), so old saves need nothing; every mode keeps the workshops' buffer and the tower reserve, and only market sales wait (routes, the Capital's contract and an outpost feeding the towns go on). **Screens**: the boom's good on the goods strip is ringed in green or red with a corner arrow (the chip keeps its size); its Goods row says "Boom 1:11" or "Bust 0:45" beside its name and its price word reads "Up 60%"; Sell spare on each row shows the mode and opens the four choices under the row (two by two on a phone); the building card's switch shows the mode and goes between Always and Off. **Explainer** `boom`, the first time one falls on a good this town's strip shows, with the spotlight on its chip. **Save v26**: the market's boom clock. **Tests** (`test/boom.test.js`, 13): the timing, one good at a time, only goods made, same seed same booms, coarse equals fine, the price and its rows, each mode against none, boom and bust with the reserve kept, a good on Booms only always gets a boom, no boom before a second town, the save, the words, and **no buy-and-sell loop** (no market action turns coins into goods, a split sale never pays more than one, and any buy the market gets later must lose coins on a buy and sell at once). Smoke: each of the four modes through the sheet, the buttons inside the sheet, the chip's ring and arrow with its box unchanged, the row's Boom and Bust words, and the explainer's spotlight on the chip. **The screenshot-only words review** found "Boom 1:11" beside "Down 99%" (the price word now counts the boom), the mode shown twice while the choices are open (the pill hides then), the arrow alone easy to miss (now a ring too), and a clumsy What's new line; it found the four choices clear and the tip card fine. Left as is: "The price comes back over time." (an older line, flagged once). Found on the way: with "Always" the Sell spare button widened and the Goods sheet scrolled sideways on a phone; the button now stacks its mode under the label at one width, and the boom word sits beside the name. Checks: 552 tests, audit ok, balance ok (variety ok; the Gulch strategy table as before: wave 20 minmax 19.2, defense 16.2 min; the Ridge over 6 seeds: minmax 7.2, defense 7.7, active 7.9, economy and swarm 8.4 min; late damage the Ridge frost 29%, the Falls frost 34%; close: Ridge wave 10 at 7.1 and 7.3 min on seeds 2 and 5, the Falls' economy Cannon 34.3%, minmax wave 10 21%, economy wave 10 25%), firstplay ok (Dry Creek 12.5 min newcomer median against 9.4 careful; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1), idle ok on all 6 seeds (close: seed 6's hour 3 sales at 94% of hour 1), smoke phone, land, desk and rotate ok (with the new sell mode, boom chip and explainer checks), perf ok on a quiet machine (Gulch 19.4 ms, 6.2 ms script, 91 draw calls; empire 19.5 ms, 6.0 ms, 66; falls-empire 20.0 ms, 5.7 ms, 71 draw calls, 64k triangles), update and install ok. Not yet tried on a real phone.
- [x] **Market depth, part 2: town orders.** Orders with a deadline that build reputation (0 to 100), rush orders from reputation 60, and what an order needs kept from sale like the tower reserve. The goal strip and Auto take no order the player has not taken by hand.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-4. **Orders** (`src/core/orders.js`, `ORDERS` in `src/data/orders.js`): each town (not an outpost) keeps 2 offers, each about 3-6 minutes of a good's spare output with twice that time to fill once taken; one order at a time per town, filled by the town itself from what it has above its workshops' buffer and tower reserve, after routes and the Capital's contract and before any sale (`claim`), so what an order needs is never sold. A filled order adds 8 reputation (rush 12), a miss or Give up takes 15; reputation is per town, 0-100, and kept through a charter. Rush orders from 60 (4 in 10): 2-3 minutes of output, 1.4 times the time, 1.6 times the pay. An offer nobody takes is replaced after 10 minutes. Nothing but the player takes one (a test plays 3 hours and sees none taken). Decision: a deadline that runs out inside a catch-up chunk is judged after that chunk's goods go in, so time away never misses an order the town filled. **Pay, decided in the open**: at 1.2 times the base price, a probe (`tools/order-probe.js`, new, one active empire run with `--noorders` for the A/B) paid 16-20M coins a run and Silver Falls came 0.6 h sooner (6.05-6.12 h, window from 6); an order now pays 1.3 times what the market pays for the good now (no boom), up to half more with reputation, so 2-3.7M a run, 9-17% of the coins. **When they open, decided in the open**: a 10-minute clock from the second town opened them while the bot was still in Pine Hollow, and seed 4's Ridge wave 10 came at 11.5 min (want 7-10); they now open a minute after two towns have each beaten wave 10 (`ordersReady`), and the Ridge wave 10 reads the same with orders on and off. **Screens**: an Orders part at the top of the Goods sheet, under Save for towers: Reputation 24 of 100 with a bar and "Rush orders come at 60", the running order with Sent so far, a bar and "6:16 left" in a fixed cell, Give up, and each offer with Pays, "Deliver in 11 min" and Take (Take instead while one runs; that and Give up take two taps, "Lose 15 reputation?"); a Rush chip on rush offers. Toasts when an order is filled or runs out, in any town. The Goods button gets its dot until the player has looked at a new board. Income names it "Orders filled". **Explainer** `orders` with the spotlight on Goods. **Save v27**: the orders clock. **Bots**: the active and strategy players take, in every town, the offer that pays the most a minute that their spare output fills with 20% room (`tendOrders`, `ORDER_ROOM`; 1.5 left every rush order untaken); `ordersCheck`: every seed fills 188-219, misses 11-19 (5-8%), takes 70-97 rush orders and reaches reputation 100. `--noorders` turns it off. **Found on the way**: on a phone the Goods sheet snapped past the Orders heading to the first offer (the heading was not a snap point); the Orders block is now one, and smoke checks the heading and reputation show when the sheet opens. Tests: `test/orders.test.js` (17). Smoke: the board, Take with one tap, Take instead with two, Give up, every button inside the sheet with no sideways scroll, the fixed time cell, the heading in view, and the explainer's spotlight. Split out: the bots' sell modes go to their own box (2b), since orders filled this release. Checks: 568 tests, audit ok, balance ok (Capital 2.94-3.23 h; the tree an upgrade every 10.9-13.3 min; Silver Falls founded 6.48-7.19 h, window 6-10; Falls wave 10 7.8-8.3 min; Ridge wave 10 the same as before, close at 7.1 and 7.3 min on seeds 2 and 5; variety ok; late damage the Ridge frost 29%, the Falls frost 33%; close lines: the Falls' swarm Mortar 35.1%, economy's Gulch wave 10 25% behind, minmax 21%), firstplay ok (Dry Creek 12.5 min newcomer median against 9.4 careful; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1), idle ok on all 6 seeds (idle takes no orders), smoke phone, land, desk and rotate ok, perf ok on a quiet machine after OneDrive's sync settled (Gulch 19.5 ms, 6.2 ms script, 70 draw calls; empire 17.9 ms, 5.0 ms, 65; falls-empire 20.2 ms, 5.6 ms, 70 draw calls, 64k triangles), update and install ok. **The screenshot-only words review** led to "Deliver in 11 min" (it read "11 min to fill it" as how long the town takes), Take instead (Switch did not say what), "Rush orders are open", a gold mark on the reputation bar at 60, and plainer What's new lines. Left as is: "Sent 96 so far" (the row's name says 240, and the Capital's contracts read the same), Give up shows its cost on its second tap, and the landscape sheet shows the board and one order above the fold (the sheet's size, decided before). Not yet tried on a real phone.

I was in Dry Creek and it shows what was being researched in another town").** Research is per town (each town its own list and one slot) and the Auto switches are per town, but an outpost's Research sheet listed other towns' research with no frame. Every sheet heading names its place ("Research · Copper Gulch", "Auto · Dry Creek"). In an outpost: "Dry Creek has no research. Each town researches on its own.", then "Your towns", one row per town with its running item and time left or "Nothing yet", a tap going to that town's Research. The Research explainer adds "Each town researches its own list, one at a time." Lasting rule for games/CLAUDE.md: "Say where a thing applies. Decision: the old "Researching now" box above the list is gone, since above the town's own list it read as shared. **Auto sheet**: each row says "in this town" or "this town's workers", and the line over them says only "Auto never spends more than half your coins." The button now shows only its own town's time (smoke checks it in every place). It also led to "Not researching" (was "Nothing yet"), an arrow on the town rows that open another town, "Auto never spends", and What's new lines that say what changed rather than which headings did. Decision: the outpost line keeps the owner's own words ("Dry Creek has no research. Each town researches on its own."), not the reviewer's "Outposts have no research". Found on the way: the sheets' scroll snap hid a sheet's first line (the Auto sheet's scope line, as the Orders heading the release before); every sheet now opens on its first line (`.sheetbody > :first-child`), with a smoke check. Checks: 568 tests, audit ok, balance, firstplay and idle ok with the same numbers as 2026.11.22-4 (no simulation change), smoke phone, land, desk, rotate and research everywhere ok, update and install ok. - [x] **Level-20 upgrades: the bug, Last Call and the words (owner, 2026-11-22: "if I already upgraded a tower past level 20, it never got this option" / "I don't like the actual bonus to Last Call for being near the vault. The strategy should be implied, not direct. It's a bit confusing and AI slop wordy now").** (1) `forkNext` in `src/core/defense.js` is true only at `t.level + 1 === FORK_LEVEL`, so a Still or Cannon already at 20 or more when the forks shipped never picks. Make it `t.level + 1 >= FORK_LEVEL && !t.fork` for kinds with FORKS, let `upgradeTower` set the fork on that next level, keep Auto-towers waiting at those towers and fire the coach card for them; test on a save with a level-25 Still and a level-22 Cannon with no fork (the pick shows, is taken, the level is kept). (2) Last Call loses `near` and NEAR: in their place a plain effect such as hurt raiders taking much more damage (up to about 2.5 times as health runs out), tuned with `tools/fork-runs.js` so Slow Rot and Last Call each win somewhere (`forkCheck`); towers keep `fork: 'b'`; explain rows, GDD.md and the comment block follow. (3) Every fork's `about` is one short sentence of what it does, no advice on where or when (Slow Rot "Softer hits. Its poison never wears off.", Last Call "Hits hurt raiders much harder.", Rocket Battery "Fires 4 rockets, each at its own raider.", Big Shell "One huge shot. Hits {boss} twice as hard."); drop a `bestText` that restates it, check base tower and path texts in the same pass, and rewrite the `fork` / `forkReady` explainer that says "best near the start ... hardest near the vault". The audit fails fork text over 10 words or with "Best at". A fresh-eyes read of every fork card. Lasting rules (already in games/CLAUDE.md): "New choices reach old saves" and "Choices say what they do, not how to use them".
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-6. **The bug**: `forkNext` in `src/core/defense.js` is now `level + 1 >= FORK_LEVEL && !fork` for a kind with FORKS, and `upgradeTower` sets the fork on whichever level that is, so a Still at 25 or a Cannon at 22 from before the forks shows "Pick an upgrade" on its next level and keeps its levels; the coach cards (`src/ui/coach.js`) and Auto-towers use the same `forkNext`, so they find those towers too and Auto waits for the pick. No save change was needed (v22 already set `fork: null`). Test: `test/fork.test.js`, an old save with a level-25 Still and a level-22 Cannon. **Last Call**: `near` and NEAR are gone; it hits a raider by the share of health it has lost, `HURT` = 1 + 2 times that (a normal hit on a fresh raider, up to 3 times on one nearly down), `hurtFactor` in defense.js, the card row "Raiders low on health · Up to 3 times the damage". Runs of the waves (`tools/fork-runs.js --kind still`, seeds 1 and 2): the Ridge splits 19-17 (Slow Rot at the first pads, Last Call from about the middle) and the Falls 8-30 and 4-34, so each side wins somewhere without a bonus for its place; mean damage over the pads Slow Rot 5.1M and 6.5M, Last Call 4.7M and 6.2M, no fork 4.3M and 5.7M in the Ridge. **The bots' guess** picked Last Call on 33 of 36 Ridge pads with the old numbers; ROT_LIVES 2.2 and HURT_AT .5 in `tools/balance.js` agree with the runs on 107 of 148 pads (98 before); `fork-runs.js` gained `--against <earlier output>` to count that, the rule under Copper Gulch says so. **Words**: every fork's line is one short sentence with no advice (Slow Rot "Softer hits. Its poison never wears off.", Last Call "Hits raiders low on health much harder.", Rocket Battery "Fires 4 rockets, each at its own raider.", Big Shell "One huge shot. Hits {boss} twice as hard."), the Still's explainer no longer says where, and `choiceRules` in `tools/audit.js` fails a path or fork line over 10 words or with advice on where or when; the Cannon forks keep their "Best against" (which raiders, not where, and not said on the choice). The fresh-eyes read of the fork cards flagged "hurt raiders" as readable two ways, so it says "low on health". Its other points stay as they are, each for a reason: the choice lines are the owner's own (the Shots row shows the slower fire); "105/s poison" and the "You will have enough..." note are older card lines outside this box; the landscape card scrolls with the choice first by design. **Checks**: 570 tests, audit ok; bots all ok (balance 26 min, firstplay, idle), close lines unchanged from before (economy Gulch wave 10 at 25%, minmax 21%, Ridge wave 10 at 7.1 and 7.3 min on seeds 2 and 5, the Falls swarm Mortar at 35.1%); the active bots took no Still fork in these runs (Cannon: Rocket Battery 3, Big Shell 1). Smoke phone, land, desk ok; perf 23.1 ms (Gulch), 21.6 ms (Ridge with three towns), 23.5 ms (Falls with every town), all ok; update and install tests ok.

Today `src/render/board.js` draws `roadRibbon(B.road)` along the whole road, across the river too, then `riverRibbon` and `bridges()` on top, so from the top-down camera the wide deck and the ribbon under it read as road on the water. (2) A deck no wider than the road, spanning only the water and a short ramp on each bank, with posts in the water and a small ripple ring, a shadow on the water under the deck and a visible gap between deck and water; boats and the steamer still pass under (BRIDGE height), and raiders' `bridgeLift` matches the new deck.
