  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-7. New pure `src/render/bridge-plan.js` (no three.js, so tests reach it): `waterCut` says where a point is on the water, `crossings` finds each stretch of road within the bridge's reach of the river, `bridgePlan` lays the deck and the posts. `roadRibbon` takes a cut and drops every triangle with a corner on the water, so the road and its trampled verges stop at the bank and the river runs unbroken under each deck, in the town, in the map's dioramas and on the map's routes (its ties, stones and telegraph poles too). Town decks are 1.0 wide (the road 1.32) on posts that stand only in the water, each with a pale ripple ring, a cross brace under each pair, a dark underside and rails on short balusters; the deck still rises to BRIDGE (0.55) so the steamer passes. Decision: the ramp now runs to RAMP = 1.2 from the river's middle (was 1), since a 0.45 ramp up 0.55 read as a wall at each end; the road under every deck is straight (a test). Decision: the shadow on the water is the real shadow map (med and high tiers), not a painted one, which would double it. Raiders and townsfolk now lift by the river's own path, not its leg into the vault: with it, raiders by the Falls vault floated 0.23 up (0.37 with the longer ramp); a test checks that every lifted road point lies on a deck. A road end by the vault, within reach of that leg but over no water, gets no deck. On the map a route bridge stands at DECK_Y 0.22 over the water and reaches just past each bank, and wagons and trains ride up onto it (they ran through the old deck). The wave bar's word and the hold button now come from one function, `waveBar` in `src/ui/wave.js`: while waves are held the bar says On hold, even with raiders still on the road, and Resume waves shows only then (a test over every phase). Decision: the map's state label still says Fighting for a held town mid-wave, since raiders are on its road and the map has no hold button; it says On hold once that wave ends. `tools/look-shots.js` takes each Falls bridge from above and low (`LOOK_VIEWS=bridge`) and, on the map, the Falls diorama and a route bridge (the full state now lays Sandy Bend to Iron Ridge, which crosses the river). The fresh-eyes look: water on both sides of and under every town bridge from above and low, and under the map's Falls bridges; it flagged the first map rail bridge as too low and too long over the thin map river, now raised and shortened (from straight above an aslant crossing still covers most of that narrow river). Checks: 575 tests, audit ok, smoke phone, land and desk ok, perf ok (Gulch 19.2 ms, 6.0 ms script, 70 draw calls; empire 21.6 ms, 7.4 ms, 64; falls-empire 21.3 ms, 6.3 ms, 71 draw calls, 66k triangles), balance ok (variety ok; close lines unchanged: Ridge wave 10 at 7.1 and 7.3 min, the swarm Mortar in the Falls 35.1%, minmax Gulch wave 10 21% behind, economy 25% behind), firstplay ok (Dry Creek 12.5 min newcomer median against 9.4 careful; Gulch wave 10 10.0 against 8.1), idle ok, update and install ok.
- [x] **The Crossbow's level-20 fork (owner, 2026-11-22: "We need level 20 options for crossbow").** On top of Repeater or Longbow: Piercing Bolts (each bolt goes through up to 3 raiders in a line, softer hits) or Sharpshooter (always aims at the toughest raider in reach and hits much harder, fires slower). Models for both with a clear look at tier 3, the coach card, explain rows, the bots' options and worth, and the damage table, numbers from `tools/fork-runs.js`. Gates: each of the 4 Crossbow finishes (2 paths by 2 forks) is the best buy in some bot run (`forkCheck`), no fork makes the Crossbow carry a map alone, strategies within 25%. This takes the Crossbow half of part 3 below; the Frost Lens and the Mortar each keep a box so every tower gets a fork.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-8. **Piercing Bolts** (`FORKS.crossbow.a`): 0.7 of the hit, and each bolt goes on through the raiders within `PIERCE.width` (0.45) of the line from the tower through its first raider, up to `PIERCE.depth` (2.4) past it, up to 3 in all (`pierced` in `src/core/defense.js`); the bolt is drawn to the last raider it went through. **Sharpshooter** (`FORKS.crossbow.b`): 3 times the hit at 0.45 times the shots, aimed at the raider in reach with the most health left (the nearest the vault of equals). Card rows "Each bolt hits · Up to 3 raiders" and "Aims at · The toughest raider", Best against "Raiders in a row" and "Brutes and {boss}". Models: a long steel bolt in the groove and a rack of three for Piercing Bolts, a brass scope on a long stock for Sharpshooter. The explainer `fork-crossbow` fires on a Crossbow one level from its fork (`forkCrossbow` in coach.js). **Numbers from runs of the waves** (`tools/fork-runs.js --kind crossbow`, seeds 1-3, 222 pad and path pairs of the late Ridge and Falls): at Sharpshooter 2.6 times the hit Piercing Bolts won 196 of them, at 3.2 Sharpshooter won about 150, at 2.9 Piercing Bolts won 146; at 3.0 they split 125 to 97 with each of the 4 finishes winning 47-64 pads, and the two sides stay within about 10% of each other on every pad. A Sharpshooter's 1.35 times the damage a second nets little more than a plain level 20, because its hits meet armor and lose the Crossbow's bonus on horsemen. **The bots' guess**: with nothing in it that depends on the pad, it took one side for a whole town (every Ridge pad Piercing Bolts, every Falls pad Sharpshooter). A probe of the runs found the side follows the road there: where more of the road in reach runs straight at the pad, Piercing Bolts (r 0.42); where more road is in reach, Sharpshooter (r -0.47). So the guess counts a bolt as 1 + 2 times the share of the road in reach that runs toward or away from the pad (`lineShare`, `PIERCE_LINE` 1) and a Sharpshooter as hitting the boss 2 times its share of the wave, with no horsemen bonus and 3% more a road unit in reach over 6 (`SHARP_BOSS`, `SHARP_COVER`, `SHARP_FROM`); a grid of 24 sets with `--against` put these first, agreeing with the runs on 153 of 222 (69%; the Still's guess agrees on 72%) and splitting them 136 to 86. **New gate**: `forkCheck` also fails a finish (a path with a fork) that is the best pick on no pad in any run; every finish of all three kinds passes (Grapeshot with Big Shell on only 4, so it is the one to watch). **Bots**: balance ok (the Gulch strategy table: active 69.6 min to the first charter and wave 10 at 7.7 min, minmax 7.1, swarm 8.9, economy 7.0, defense 9.4; close lines are the two Gulch strategy boxes below and Ridge wave 10 at 7.1-7.3 min on seeds 2 and 5), firstplay ok (newcomer 12.5 against 9.4 min for Dry Creek, 10.0 against 8.1 for the Gulch's wave 10), idle ok. Smoke ok at phone, landscape and desktop (the Crossbow fork card is in its fork-card and price-bar checks); perf ok, frame median 18.7 ms (Gulch), 19.2 ms (Ridge with three towns behind it), 21.2 ms (Falls with every town), script 5.9-6.4 ms, 66-70 draw calls. The active bots took Sharpshooter 11 times in the Ridge. **A foreman bug found on the way**: the first bot run failed seed 4's Wire Mill on Gulch copper at 50% (want 90%). An A/B on that seed (the last release 100%, this build 50%) and a minute-by-minute trace showed it was not the fork: the shifted clock put a wire boom in seed 4's Falls hour, the boom sold off the wire, the Telegraph Office ran short, and a good some workshop is short of skips the foreman's payback check, so the Wire Mill's foreman bought 15 levels in three minutes, far past what Gulch copper could feed, and the mill sat starved for half an hour. A player's foreman would do the same. Fix in the core: a foreman waits, short of that good, when the next level would use more of a shipped good than 1.25 times what the town making it makes a second (`importShort` in `src/core/foremen.js`, with a test); the bots' building pick uses the same function. Seed 4's Wire Mill is back at 97%. Fresh-eyes words review of the fork cards: "Level / 20" split beside a long name (now one line); "one more upgrade" in What's new read as the last one (reworded). Not changed, with reasons: the card names the path (Longbow) before the fork as every tower card does; Piercing Bolts' hit is lower after the level because softer hits are the trade it states; each side's two short sentences follow the owner's own examples for the other forks.

Like poison, frozen, shield strength?").** A tap on any raider (a generous hit area that follows it, picked before the ground) opens a small card titled with its name and picture: health as a bar with have / max, armor or shield with how much poison has eaten, each effect on it now as an icon, a word and the time left (Poisoned, Rotting with no end, Slowed, Frozen, Brittle, On fire), what it gives others (a shield bearer's aura), its reward ("Worth 12 coins") and the towers that hit it hardest. The card follows it live with the selection ring, the game keeps running, and when it falls or gets through the card says so for a moment and closes. Nothing new over raiders by default (quiet labels). Gates: one core function `foeStatus(foe)` that the card and the bots share, with tests; smoke taps a moving raider at phone size; the words check reads the card.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-9. **Core**: `foeStatus(town, foe)` in `src/core/defense.js` gives health and its most, armor now beside armor at full and the share poison ate, the effects on it now in card order (frozen, poison, rot, spread, fire, slow, brittle) with seconds left (null: no end), its coins (`bountyOf`, which `kill` now pays from too, so the card's "Worth" is the real bounty) and gone ('down' or 'through'). Decisions: "On fire" is read from Shrapnel's burning ground under the raider (fires have no per-raider flag), Brittle shows only while slowed (it only counts then), and Spreads poison shows while a fever carrier carries poison. No raider gives others anything yet (no shield bearer in `FOES`), so the card has no aura row; a raider that does adds its effect in `foeStatus`. **The bots share it**: `tools/balance.js` counts what each raider carried as it fell in the Gulch's first hour, through `foeStatus` (report line "raiders fell carrying": poison 68%, slow 59%, frozen 14% of 10489). **Tap**: `pickFoe` in `src/render/pick.js` takes the raider in sight whose middle on screen is nearest the finger within 26 px (a boss 42), unless a building or tower the ray meets stands well in front of it, and it wins over the pad or building the tap would open only when the finger is nearer the raider's middle than that one's (`hitPx`; smoke's tap on every pad opened a passing raider before this). **Card** (`src/ui/foe.js`): picture and name, Health bar with have / most (fmtLive, at most 4 characters each), Armor bar with the eaten part hatched and "15%, was 25%" while poison eats it, the effects as chips (a 7x7 pixel mark, a word, seconds in a 6ch cell, "No end" for Rotting), "No effects." when there are none, then "Worth 5 coins. Snake Oil Still works best." (the wave card's `bestLine`). It is painted ten times a second while the game runs; when the raider falls the card keeps how it last looked and says "Brute down!" (or "It got to the vault/stockpile.") in place of the coins line for 1.6 s, then closes; a long gap between frames never cuts that short. The ring and a short glow follow the raider (`Selection.followFoe`), and the map slides only when the raider walks under the card (the camera's `keep`, as for buildings), never chases it. Nothing new is drawn over raiders. Explainer `foe-tap` ("Tap a raider"), the first time a raider out on the road carries an effect or from wave 4, with the spotlight on that raider (`locate({ foe })`). Gates: `test/foe.test.js` (every effect and its time, fire only on the burning ground, gone, the card's worth equals the paid bounty, a real Still poisons a real raider, the words), the words pinned in `test/words.test.js`, smoke taps a moving raider at phone, landscape and desktop with a real pointer tap (the card opens with its name and health, the ring follows it, the game runs on), shots `raider` and `raider-down`. Fresh-eyes review of those shots: the armor sentence said the row's fact again (now "15%, was 25%"), the bars jumped after the kill and the card grew a line (now frozen, with the down line in place of the coins line), "Nothing on it now" read as a narrator (now "No effects."), and the health number ran out of its cell on a desktop (a wider cell, and smoke fails a sideways scroll on the card). Kept: "Brittle" (the Frost Lens path is Brittle Ice, and a longer word does not fit a chip at phone width), "6.20K" (the game's one live-number format), and the card's place (every card shares it; the map slides the raider clear). Bots: balance, firstplay and idle ok (no simulation change but the bounty refactor).

That seems to be missing").** With the raider card, whose status it shares (`foeStatus`). The old game in ../tycoon had both: read it for ideas and numbers only. **Bar**: while a boss is on the road, a slim bar under the wave bar with its picture and name, a health bar, its armor if it has some, and its stage in a few words ("Crew jumped off"); one fixed width (the live-numbers rule), nothing else on it, gone when the boss falls or gets through ("War wagon down!" for a moment); a tap opens the raider card for the boss. **Stages**, each shown on the model, numbers in `src/data/foes.js`: the war wagon's crew jumps off at half health and runs on as fast raiders while the wagon slows; the armored train sheds its plates at half health (faster, less armor); the steamer lets off steam and speeds up at a third; the bandit wagon is like the war wagon with horsemen. The background battle counts the stages too. Gates: a core test for each stage trigger, smoke shows the bar on a boss wave at phone size without moving the layout, the boss-gate pacing tests (boss waves stay gates, not walls), and a fresh-eyes read of the shots.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-10. **Stages** (`stages` and `STAGE_WORDS` in `src/data/foes.js`; `stages()`, `speedOf`, `armorOf`, `stageOf` in `src/core/defense.js`, which every pace and armor read now goes through): the war wagon drops 3 Wagon crew (a new raider, half a raider's health, fast, `only: true` so it never rides in a wave's mix) at half health and slows to 0.6; the bandit wagon drops 2 Horsemen and slows the same way; the armored train sheds its plates at half health (1.15 times as fast, armor 0.4 to 0.15); the steamer goes full steam at a third (1.1 times as fast). Riders that jump off count toward the wave's size, pay bounties and hit the vault, but only the boss decides the gate. The background battle plays them through `fight`, so they count away and offline with no extra code. `foeStatus` reports the stage, the raider card shows it as a chip in the bar's words (one state, one source, with a test). **Models**: the crew rides on the war wagon and bandit wagon as their own part and goes at the stage, the train's bands and skirts are a part that falls away, the steamer puffs steam from its stack (pooled trail puffs), and a burst of dust marks each stage. **Bar** (`src/ui/boss.js`): at the foot of the top bar's left column, so showing it moves nothing above it (smoke measures the others' laid-out places before, during and after, and the bar's one width as health falls and the stage starts); a tap opens the boss's card; "War wagon down!" alone for 1.6 seconds, then gone. Explainer `boss-bar`, and the four boss explainers each say their stage. **Tuning, decided with an A/B** (`STAGES=off node tools/boom-probe.js`): the first numbers (train 1.35 times as fast, steamer 1.5) made bosses walls, the economy bot's Ridge wave 10 at 11-13 min on three seeds against 7-9 with stages off; a steamer at 1.25 still won Silver Falls wave 10 on seed 1 (11.5 min against 8.3). The Gulch's wave 10 gate did not move with stages (economy median 8.8 min on and off). **A bot fix it uncovered**: seed 2's Boiler Shop ran 89.6% of the time (want 90) on a path any single stage moved by 3-7 points; the active bot topped up an import only once it held under 1, so it now acts when less than 30 seconds of what the users take is on hand (`TEND_BUFFER` in `tools/balance.js`), and the Boiler Shop runs 100% on all six seeds. **Bots** (balance, firstplay, idle all ok): Gulch wave 10 active 7.8, minmax 8.7, swarm 7.3, economy 9.0 (close, 25%, its own box below), defense 7.2 min; Ridge wave 10 7.2-8.4 by strategy (seed 2 close at 7.0); Silver Falls wave 10 7.7-8.3 on all six seeds with no breach; newcomer Dry Creek 12.5, Gulch wave 10 10.0 min. **Perf** (4x slowdown, phone): Gulch 17.9 ms, Ridge with three towns 19.1 ms and 66 calls, Falls with everything 20.9 ms and 70 calls. Fresh-eyes read of the shots: fixed the help card (it described the bar), the down state ("War wagon down!" cut off on a phone beside numbers that said it again) and the card missing the bar's stage. The shot tool now makes sure a wave is running before it puts a boss on the road (in landscape the wave had ended and the wagon sat still).

Enemies and units open a card with their live state" now holds for raiders, but Call to arms' workers by the road (`src/render/militia.js`) open nothing. A tap on one opens a small card from one core function, like `foeStatus`: which building they came from, the seconds of the call left, and what the town gives up meanwhile; the ring follows them. Smoke taps one during a call at phone size. Small; with the boss bar if it fits.

The pairs are one instanced mesh, so a tap finds them by where they stand on screen (`pickWorker`, beside `pickFoe`; the nearest of a raider, a pair and the pad or building wins), the ring sits on the pair, and the camera keeps it clear of the card. Explainer `worker-tap`, spotlit on a pair. Smoke taps a pair at every size; shots take the card, its end and the help card. Fresh-eyes read: "Not made" read as not built yet (now "Timber lost"), the help card described the card instead of what happens (rewritten), and "Back to work!" sat under two empty rows (now on the first row). Smoke found the camera's keep-in-view code assumed a selection was a raider, a pad or a building and threw every frame; it handles the workers now. No simulation change, so the bots were not run again (2026.11.22-10's run stands). Perf (4x slowdown, phone): 17.9, 19.7 and 21.7 ms.
- [x] **Sharper buildings and people, part 1: sharpness (owner, 2026-11-22: "How can we improve the resolution of the buildings and people?").** Phones defaulted to the med tier with the pixel ratio capped at 1.35, no antialiasing and no post, so the world drew at under half the screen's resolution. Raise the phone tier's ratio as far as the perf gate allows and add a cheap edge-smoothing pass (FXAA, written in the repo, no CDN); Auto steps up when frames have room, not only down; `tools/perf.js` at 4x slowdown and phone size stays in budget, and the ratio that fits is written in `quality.js`. Split from one box on 2026-11-22: sharpness, close-up detail and the contact sheet would not fit one release.
  - Notes: shipped 2026.11.22-12. The med tier (the phone default) now draws at ratio 1.5 (was 1.35) with an FXAA edge pass written in `src/render/scene.js` (no download): the scene draws into an 8-bit target already tone mapped and in screen colors (the target is marked as an XR target, the one case where three.js does that, with RGBA8 storage so the GPU does not convert twice), then one full-screen triangle smooths only across edges; screenshots before and after show the same colors. Measured with `tools/perf.js` at phone size and 4x slowdown, the Gulch: 1.35 20.3 ms, 1.5 22.5, 1.5 with FXAA 27.7, 1.75 with FXAA 36.6, 2 without FXAA 38.8, 2 with FXAA 45.6; Silver Falls with every town behind it: 1.35 22.7, 1.5 24.4, 1.5 with FXAA 29.0-32.7. So 2 does not fit the software GPU, and 1.5 with FXAA is the start, written in `quality.js`. FXAA's standard edge thresholds cost the same as the strict ones, and a copy-only pass cost about 2 ms, so the reads are the cost, not the pass. Auto now climbs as well as steps down, along `RUNGS` (low, med 1.5, med 1.75, med 2, high): up a rung after 10 seconds of frames at 20 ms or less, back down within 12 seconds if a step up runs over 28 ms, and down after 6 seconds over 40 ms as before; a step down sets a ceiling under the rung it left (kept in `cg-gfx` with the ratio) so a phone never bounces, and picking Auto in the Menu clears it. Decision: a phone may climb to High (bloom, 2048 shadows) if its frames stay fast at med 2, since the watch steps it back at once. Tests in `test/quality.test.js` (rungs, the watch, the ceiling, old saved settings); smoke checks the drawing buffer follows the ratio. New `tools/sharp-shots.js` takes the zoomed Gulch at 1.35, 1.5, 1.75 and 2 for look reviews. Gates: tests 607 ok, audit ok, balance ok (close: economy Gulch wave 10 25% behind, limit 25%; minmax 21%; seed 2 Ridge wave 10 at 7.0 min, window 7-10), firstplay ok (newcomer Dry Creek 12.5 min, Gulch wave 10 10.0 min), idle ok (seed 5 hours 9-12 sold 92-93% of hour 1, limit 90%), smoke phone, land and desk ok, perf ok (Gulch 28.8 ms, 8.1 ms script, 71 draw calls, 60k triangles; empire 29.7 ms, 8.3 ms script, 66 calls; Silver Falls with every town 29.0 ms, 7.3 ms script, 72 calls). The first Silver Falls run read 33.1 ms: a smoke run's headless Edge had outlived it, since on Windows the Edge process cdp.js starts hands the browser to a second process and exits, so killing it on exit stopped nothing; `tools/cdp.js` now stops every process that uses its own profile folder on close and on exit. Unverified: how high a real phone climbs, which only a real phone shows.
- [x] **A speaker button on tutorial cards (owner, 2026-11-22: "A little speaker button for tutorial dialog boxes to play an audio of the dialog box.").** A clear speaker button (at least 36 px, labeled for screen readers) at the top right of the coach card's title row (`src/ui/coach.js`, `.coachcard`) reads the card's title and text aloud; a second tap stops it, and speech stops when the card closes, the town switches or the app hides. Free forever: the browser's speech synthesis with only voices whose `localService` is true, American English first; with no such voice the button is hidden; never an online voice, never audio fetched, never the text sent anywhere. The music dips while it speaks; speech has its own volume in Settings. Settings gets "Read cards aloud" (off by default), which reads each new card by itself, kept in the options save with a migration. Card text reads well aloud: names filled in ({boss}), numbers and symbols as said ("x3" as "3 times", no "·"), no emoji, with an audit check on explainer text. Gates: smoke with a stubbed speech engine checks the button speaks exactly the card's text, stops when the card closes and is hidden with no local voice, plus its touch target at phone size. Lasting rule for games/CLAUDE.md under Teach as it appears: "Tutorial cards can be read aloud with a speaker button, using on-device voices only."
  Notes: 2026.11.23-1. `src/audio/speech.js`: the browser's speech engine with only voices whose localService is true and whose language is English, American English first; with none the button is hidden, and it comes back when voices load late (voiceschanged). A 40 px pixel speaker at the top right of every card (`#coachSpeak`, aria-label Read aloud or Stop reading, pressed and lit while it reads); it reads `sayable(title, text)`, which says "x3" as "3 times", K and M as thousand and million, "12 / 40" as "12 of 40", "◂" as "the back button", and drops any other symbol or a name left in braces. Speech stops on a second tap, Got it, a place switch (the coach is taken down) and the app hiding. The music dips to a quarter while it reads (`audio.duck`). The options save `cg-audio` goes to v2 with `voice` (volume, 0.9) and `read` (Read cards aloud, off), `migrateAudio` filling them in for v1. The Menu shows Voice with Music and Sound and a Read aloud section with the Read cards aloud switch, both only where an on-device voice exists (review of this release: under Read aloud, "Voice" could mean which voice). Audit: explainer text with a symbol the voice cannot say, a name in braces, or "x3". Smoke (stubbed engine): the button is at least 36 px and inside the card, speaks exactly the card's words, a second tap stops it and lifts the dip, Got it stops it, Read cards aloud makes the next card read itself, no local voice hides it, and the Menu shows both controls. Shots: every page gets the same stub, with a `coach-read` frame. Not checked on a real phone: iOS Safari may not speak a card that opens by itself before the first tap of the speaker. The CLAUDE.md rule was already there.

New `tools/playthrough.js <dir> [--seed N] [--minutes M]`: the newcomer bot of `tools/firstplay.js` (now with a hook) plays on the core, and each moment that matters is kept as a save (every tip card while it is due, every unlock, every new button and the sheet it opens, the first boss and the first lost wave in each place, the charter offer, one shot per stretch of 2 minutes with nothing new); then each save is loaded in the real game at phone size, with a check that the game loaded is the saved one (the first try showed the first game in every shot: the game page saves itself as it unloads, so the save is now written from a page with no game). - [x] **Progression and first-play clarity pass, part 2: the bots' progression table.** A progression health table from the bots through Thunder Mesa's gate, written here: the longest wait with nothing to buy or see in each hour, the time between unlocks, where each strategy stalls, whether each layer (town, empire, perks) has something worth doing at every stage, and whether a charter ever feels like a loss (the time back to the same wave). Its findings join part 1's in the fix boxes, ranked together, with a gate each.
  Notes: 2026.11.23-3. New `tools/progress.js <dir> [--seeds 3]` patches the core's step with a watcher (every 5 seconds of play, no choice changed) and runs seven bots side by side: the newcomer (6 hours), active, minmax, swarm, economy and defense (the empire run to the end of Silver Falls, the last town there is, so Thunder Mesa's gate) and idle (6 idle hours after the Capital). 3 seeds each, 21 runs in about 25 minutes; the tables go to `<dir>/progress.md`. Decision: "through Thunder Mesa's gate" is the end of the Falls, since Thunder Mesa is not built. What it found, on the release before this one (2026.11.23-2):
  - **Nothing to buy or see, longest stretch by hour (median):** newcomer 4.9 min in hour 1 (in the Gulch at wave 20, while it holds the waves to save for the Ridge), 1.2 then under 0.5 min; every careful bot 1.2 min in hour 1 (Dry Creek's wave 2, waiting for its first coins) and under 1 min after. So there is always something to buy (the town layer has something 90-100% of every hour from hour 2). What is missing is something new.
  - **Time between unlocks:** median 0.5-0.9 min, but every careful bot has two long gaps: from the Ridge's routes (1.7-2.0 h) to the Capital (3.0-3.4 h), 1.1-1.7 h with nothing new; and from Call to arms or the Ridge's charter (5.4-5.9 h) to Silver Falls (6.7-8.9 h), 1.2-3.5 h (minmax 2.6-3.5 h). Research shows a new item only twice in 8 hours (`research:new`). The newcomer's last unlock is the Ridge's second route at 3.5-4.1 h, then nothing for 1.9-2.5 h to the end of its 6 hours.
  - **Where each strategy stalls (no best wave climbs and nothing unlocks):** active, economy and defense 16-27 min at the Ridge's waves 37-51; minmax 22-35 min at the Ridge's wave 52 (just before the Falls); swarm 50 min to 1.5 h at the Ridge's waves 48-50, and swarm never opened Silver Falls on seeds 1 and 3 in 10 hours (no gate saw it: the Falls strategy table starts every strategy from the active bot's game as it gets there); idle 56 min to 1.6 h at the Ridge's waves 48-51 (its gate is income, not waves); newcomer 20-23 min at the Gulch's wave 43 and the Ridge's 39, from hour 4.
  - **Each layer, share of each hour with something worth doing (town / empire / perks):** town 86-100% for every bot from hour 1; empire 0% for every careful bot until the Falls hour (each institution, upgrade and town is bought the moment it is ready, so the empire layer is always a thing to save for, never a thing to tap), 91-100% for idle once it stops taking contracts; perks 11-80%, highest in the hours after a charter.
  - **Charters:** the Gulch's first charter takes 22-28 min to climb back to the wave it was signed at, 54-81% of the first climb from the Gulch opening (the balance gate counts it in the Gulch's own 90 minutes, where it passes at under 50%; in the empire run the Ridge's saving comes right after it, with the waves held). The Ridge's charter is cheap, 20-32 min back against 3-4 h, 9-15%. minmax seed 3 and swarm seed 2 never climbed the Gulch back to its signed wave (26-28) in 8 hours, since they play the Ridge from then. A charter does not read as a loss for the careful bots; the newcomer never signs one.
  Ranked with part 1's findings: (1) the Capital wall (fix 3; the newcomer's unlocks stop at about 4 hours), (2) saving for a coin goal (fix 2, shipped with this box), (3) the mid-game's long gaps with nothing new (new box after fix 3), (4) swarm's wall at the Ridge's wave 50 (new box with the strategy boxes), then fixes 4-10 as part 1 ranked them. `tools/progress.js` now runs at every evaluation box with the playthrough (How this repo enforces).

"You finished every goal in this town." still shows when the strip moves on to the empire's step.   Notes: 2026.11.23-3. `earnRate`, `readyIn` and `readyWords` in `src/core/goals.js`: the strip adds how long is left after a coin goal's have / need ("about 4 min to go"), from the coins the whole empire earned over the last 60 seconds of play (the top bar's own rate is a 5-second window and jumps with each bounty), in whole minutes so it changes at most once a minute, "about 1 h 15 min to go" past an hour (steps of 5 minutes), "under a minute to go" and "over 10 hours to go" at the ends, nothing while no coins come in (fix 4 handles that case). The first words, "ready in about 1:20" and "ready in over an hour", were flagged by the fresh-eyes read of this release (no subject for "ready", and an hour or more too vague to act on), so the time now counts down to the coins shown beside it. Not changed, with reason: the reviewer also asked "Tap Hold waves while you save for Iron Ridge" to say what holding does; the button's name says it and the bar then says On hold, so more words would say one fact twice. New shot frame `ridge-held` shows the saving goal with its count and time. The newcomer bot (`GOAL_SAVE` in `tools/firstplay.js`) saves while the strip counts coins for a goal it says is ready within 10 minutes, with the same rate: it buys nothing but a tower, and that only when its vault falls under 70%. Decision: at first it bought a tower whenever a raider got through, as the box said, but at wave 40 a raider reaches the vault in almost every wave, so it spent each 5,000-8,000 on an upgrade and the Toolsmith still waited 28.6 min; a vault under 70% is the line a player saving would draw. New `tools/goal-hold.js` prints how long each goal sits on the strip over the newcomer's seeds. Median over 6 seeds, before and after: the Smelter 19.9 min to 0.5 (worst 22.4 to 0.6), the Toolsmith in 6-hour runs 28.6 to 3.5 (worst 44.3 to 8.1); the Mortar went from 5.1 to 7.7, since the Kiln step it asks for first is a coin goal it now saves for while it raises no towers; the Capital's coins goal (over an hour away) is unchanged at about 3.5 h, which is fix 3. A 6-hour run of the new newcomer crashed the core: a raider spreading poison fell in the very step the war wagon's crew jumped off, and the crew had no place on the road yet; `fight` now places riders that jump off before the fallen are counted (`test/boss.test.js` sets up that step; it fails without the fix). New `test/mix.test.js` runs each town's first boss wave against every tower finish at four levels on 6 seeds; it did not reproduce that crash, but covers the rest of the mix. - [x] **Clarity fix 3: the Capital wall (review 1).** The newcomer's purse peaks at 22K against the Capital's 150K, and nothing new happens for hours. Decide with the bots, in the open: a price tied to Iron Ridge's income, part of it paid in goods the Ridge makes, or a saving step like Iron Ridge's (Hold waves, then save). Gate: the newcomer opens the Capital by minute 150 on most seeds, no quiet stretch over 10 minutes after hour 1 on seed 1's playthrough, and the careful bots' Capital times stay in their window.
  Notes: 2026.11.23-4. New `tools/capital-probe.js` (the newcomer's Ridge wave, purse and income every 15 minutes, `--price` to try one) found three causes. (1) **No tap**: the newcomer bot had no tap for "Tap Map, then tap the Capital", so no newcomer run could ever found it; `goalTap` now takes the empire's go steps (the Capital and towns), and the playthrough no longer founds it on its own. (2) **The price**: the newcomer reaches the Ridge's wave 30 at 108-124 min earning 6.5-10K a minute, so 150K was 15-23 minutes of every coin, beyond the 10 minutes it saves for (clarity fix 2); with the price alone at 60K it opened at 112-161 min. (3) **The strip**: in the Gulch with no goal left it said "Reach wave 30 in Iron Ridge" and the newcomer stayed in the Gulch for 2 hours while the Ridge climbed in the background (the 175-minute quiet stretch), and in the Ridge its own goals ("Tap the Rolling Mill and upgrade it to level 5", 23 minutes) hid the Capital's count. **Decisions**: a fixed price, 60K (`CAPITAL_DEF.found`), not one tied to income, since a price that moves while you save reads as a cheat and a fixed one is the same for every player; no goods in the price, since the newcomer's Steel and Rails sit at 0 (fix 6) and would make a new wall. A wave step in another town sends the player there ("Tap Map, Iron Ridge, then reach wave 30 there to build the Capital", `next:<id>:there` in `nextStep`), which brought wave 30 forward to 89-108 min. Once only the Capital's coins are missing, its save leads the strip in every town but an outpost still being won (`goalNow`), and the careful bots (held false) still read the town goals, as they save for it by their own rule. Tried and dropped: a "Tap Hold waves while you save for the Capital" step in the Ridge; the newcomer of seed 1 then went to the Gulch with the Ridge held and never came back (190 minutes with nothing new). Kept from it: a Ridge the player holds while saving is not asked to resume (`capitalSaving`, `savingFor`), unless the hold came from a lost boss; the newcomer's hold check uses the same function. 45K was no better than 60K (111-151 min: the save leaks to towers when raiders reach the vault, at either price). **Gates**: the newcomer opens the Capital at 125, 135, 119, 141, 148 and 112 min (never before; new `capitalMiss` in `tools/firstplay.js`: 4 of 6 seeds and the median within 150, the run now 165 min); the active bot builds it at 2.85-2.92 h (was 3.0-3.4 h, window 2.5-4) after saving 2-6 min (was 10-14), Silver Falls at 6.5-6.9 h. On seed 1 (`tools/quiet-probe.js`, the playthrough on the core) the Capital came at 125 min (never) and the longest stretch with nothing new after hour 1 fell from 175 min to 15; the stretches left over 10 minutes (11, 11, 12, 10, 15: waiting for wave 30, the save itself and an upgrade goal with no count) are fix 3b's, and its gate now carries this check, changed in the open since a stretch with nothing new is that box's job. Tests: 651 ok (the Capital-first strip in every town, the step to Iron Ridge, the bot's taps for both, the gate). Audit ok. Balance ok (close lines as before: economy Gulch wave 10 25% behind, limit 25%; minmax 21%; seed 2 Ridge wave 10 at 7.0 min). Firstplay ok (Dry Creek median 12.8 min, Gulch wave 10 10.0 min, Ridge founded 43-47 min). Idle ok (seed 5 sold 93-94% of hour 1, limit 90%). Smoke ok at phone, land and desk. Perf ok: Gulch 30.3 ms, Ridge with the empire 29.6 ms (script 8.0, 67 calls), Falls with everything 31.1 ms (script 8.3, 72 calls). New shot frame `capital-save`. The fresh-eyes read of its shots and the What's new card: two news lines read as a narrator (rewritten), and the strip said "open the Capital" beside the card's Build the Capital, so the Capital's steps now say build (a town's still say open, beside its Start). Not changed, with reason: Wave 10 beside "wave 30 done" and "about 13 min" against the top bar's rate came from the shot's staging (the best wave forced, a few seconds of play for a 60-second rate); the top bar's +51/s against a town's +26/s is fix 10.

Iron Ridge gets research items that show up as it climbs, each with a price on its card. Gate: `node tools/progress.js` shows no gap over 45 minutes before the Capital for active, economy and defense on 3 seeds, and every pacing target still passes.
  Notes: 2026.11.23-5. Three items with `opens` in `src/data/research.js`: Telescope Sights at the Ridge's wave 27 (towers reach 10% farther and fire 15% slower, 2 levels, 6K), Steam Hammers at 32 (buildings work 15% faster and each level costs 15% more, 3 levels, 10K) and Bounty Office at 37 (raiders drop 40% more coins and have 10% more health, 2 levels, 15K). The waves come from the bots' wave logs (`tools/progress.js` now keeps each town's best wave every 5 minutes): a search over the logs put two marks inside the Ridge's 21-39 climb and one where it climbs past its record after the charter. **Decision**: GDD.md's 6 to 8 items per town now reads 6 to 8 from the start plus up to 3 that show up later (`tools/audit.js` counts them apart), since new things in a long climb were the point. **Numbers from runs, not guesses**: the first Sights (15% farther, 10% slower) opened Silver Falls 0.6-1.2 h sooner (5.81 and 5.90 h on seeds 6 and 5, window 6-10); a one-item-off A/B on seeds 4 and 6 named the Sights alone, since range covers two stretches of a road that doubles back; at 10% farther and 15% slower the Falls came at 6.63-6.91 h, the same as without it, so the item is a trade, not a free gain. The first Bounty Office cost 10% of sale prices, which Auto-research kept raising in the idle empire (seed 2 sold 89% of hour 1, limit 90%), so it now costs raider health. Also: the progress table has "longest to the Capital" and "longest after it" columns, and `tools/playthrough.js` counts a new research item as a moment (its toast names it); a research item that shows up again after a charter counts again in both, as a route does. New shot frame `ridge-research`. Tests: `test/late-research.test.js` (each mark, one research:new each, the effects and their prices, a save past wave 45 gets all three). **Gate**: longest gap before the Capital 23-35 min on all 9 runs (was 54-73). After the Capital it is still 56 min to 2.0 h, the Falls part (parts 2 and 3). The newcomer's quiet stretches on seed 1 are unchanged in kind (longest 17 min after hour 1, was 15: the Capital save leaks into the new research, and upgrade goals with no count); its Capital times are the same (112-148 min). Gates: tests 654 ok, audit ok, balance ok (Silver Falls at 6.24-6.70 h, was 6.5-6.9; close lines: seed 2 Falls 6.24 h, seed 2 Ridge wave 10 at 7.0 min, Ridge frost 35.8% of the damage, limit 36%, economy Gulch wave 10 25% behind, minmax 21%), firstplay ok, idle ok (seeds 4-6 sold 92-94% of hour 1, limit 90%).
- [x] **Clarity fix 3b part 2a: where a newcomer's mid-game coins and goals went (split on 2026.11.23-6).** The box as written: `node tools/quiet-probe.js --seed 1` shows no stretch over 10 minutes with nothing new after hour 1 (then 11, 9, 12, 17 and 14 minutes: waiting for the Ridge's wave 30, saving for the Capital while coins leak into research and upgrades, and an upgrade goal with no count, so the newcomer never saves for it); change the control (the building's card opens from the strip with its price as have / need) or let the newcomer read the card as a player would. Gate: the quiet probe on seeds 1-3, firstplay's Capital gate.
  Notes: 2026.11.23-6. Split in the open: the probe found five separate causes, all fixed here, and what is left is two kinds of wait that need their own fixes (part 2b below). Baseline on seeds 1-3, longest quiet stretch after hour 1: 17, 22 and 20 min (the Capital at 132, 135 and 119 min). **Causes and fixes**, each probed on seeds 1-3 before the next (new scratch tracers printed the purse, every buy, every breach and every move once a second): (1) **The save went into towers.** On seed 2 the newcomer earned 130K during the Capital's 35-minute save and spent 124K on towers: five times the strip sent it to the Gulch, which had lost while away ("Build more towers, then tap Resume waves"), and climbing on in the Ridge it lost its vault every 7 minutes and spent the rest on upgrades (712K on seed 1 once the Gulch trips were gone). Fix: once only coins are missing, the save leads over a resume elsewhere and starts in every town but an outpost with "Tap Hold waves while you save for the Capital" (`CAPITAL_HOLD`), as the save for Iron Ridge does, and a town that lost while saving stays held until the Capital stands (`capitalSaving`, `heldHere`). Fix 3 had tried this step and dropped it because a resume elsewhere then took the player away for good; the new order is what makes it work. (2) **The price.** A held Ridge earns about 3K a minute, so 60K was 19 minutes of holding with nothing to do. **Decision**: 25K, the 6-9 minute save GDD.md meant, since the Capital's gate is the Ridge's wave 30 and the careful bots saved only 2-6 minutes for 60K. (3) **No Steel Mill goal.** On seed 2 the newcomer never built one, so the Boiler Shop and every later Ridge goal stayed shut and the strip read "Reach wave 30" for 40 minutes; new `r-steel`, and the Boiler Shop goal now asks for the Steel Mill level that opens it. (4) **No tower goals in the Ridge.** With the Steel Mill chain on the strip the newcomer met the Ridge's wave-10 boss with every tower at level 1 and lost it 8 times; the Ridge now asks for towers as its best wave climbs (a path at 7, level 10 at 12, 5 at level 5 at 17, 5 at level 8 at 22, 3 at level 10 at 27, and after the Capital level 15 at 31 and 5 at level 10 at 38; `upTo` in `goalContext` points at the highest tower still under the level). (5) **Bot bugs.** The newcomer tapped Resume at once under "Build more towers, then tap Resume waves"; it now buys 3 towers or upgrades first while it can pay (`buildFirst`, `LOST_BUYS`), and in-play retries wait while it saves. It now saves for an upgrade goal at the price the building's or tower's card shows (`goalNeed`), and **a tap on the goal strip opens the card it names** (a lot, building, pad or tower; a goal on a button still pings the spotlight), so that price is one tap away and never sits beside a level on the strip (two reviews flagged that). Also new: after the Capital the strip asks for a contract (free to take), then its first building with the 50K as have / need (`firstInstitution`), each a bot tap; nothing sent the newcomer there before, so it never took a contract or built one. `tools/playthrough.js` counts a finished contract as a moment (its toast names it). **Gate, after**: longest quiet after hour 1 13, 17 and 12 min on seeds 1-3 (limit 10, not met: the climb from the Ridge's wave 24 to 30, 10-17 min on seeds 1-2, and seed 1's save for the first building, 13 min); the newcomer opens the Capital at 128, 130, 126, 123, 118 and 118 min (was 112-148). Tests: `test/quiet.test.js` (each cause), the Capital-first test, the strip lines in `test/words.test.js`; smoke taps the strip and checks the lot's card opens. Gates: tests 661 ok, audit ok, balance ok (the active bot builds the Capital at 2.80-2.90 h after holding 0-2.7 min, was 2.85-2.92 h and 2-6 min; Silver Falls at 6.12-6.84 h; close lines: seed 2 Falls at 6.12 h, window 6-10, the second release in a row near its edge after 6.24 h, so one more puts it in a box; seed 2 Ridge wave 10 at 7.0 min; Ridge frost 35.7% of the damage, limit 36%; economy Gulch wave 10 25% behind, minmax 21%), firstplay ok (Dry Creek 12.2-13.2 min, Gulch wave 10 10.0-10.4 min, Ridge founded 44-48 min, Capital 118-130 min), idle ok (seed 6 sold 94% of hour 1, limit 90%).
- [x] **Review fixes of 2026.11.23-6, batched (the words review of that release, 26 lines, all older than it).** First, since the player cannot get past it: in short landscape (860x400) the side buttons (Goods, Research, Perks, Auto, To arms, Menu) run over Hold waves, so "Resume waves" shows as "Resu" while the strip says "Tap Resume waves" (crowd, kegs, why-*, built, boss-lost -land); smoke's layout check must catch a control covered by another, not only one cut by the screen. Then the lines that contradict the screen: a goal count beside a price the player has 40,000 times over ("Tap the Sawmill lot, then tap Build · 9.99M / 250", show have / need only while short), the retry card's "stays at wave 19" beside "Wave 18", a research toast naming a town's item in an outpost (and cut off in landscape), "You can sign the charter for 1 deed. Tap Perks." where Pine Hollow has no Perks, "You finished every goal in this town" beside a goal for that town, a toast over the lost card's heading, and "Tap Map, Pine Hollow, then Play Pine Hollow" when the card below has Play. Then the words: the foreman lines (2 how-tos on one card, one cut off in landscape), "Call again after 5 min" with "Ready again in 4 min", the boss bar intro's "this bar says how", "Each town researches on its own", the charter line on the map card, "They burn one more timber", Bust / Skip busts / Booms only (flagged twice now, so change the control or the picture, not the words), "Rush orders are open", "Any tower works.", the shield percent with no word, why-tower's three decimals, the Sharpshooter card's sideways scroll on phone, the title footer's lone "ads", and "Save coins to open Iron / Ridge" on three lines. Also: no shot shows the strip lines new in 2026.11.23-6 (the save count for the Capital, the Ridge's tower goals, the Steel Mill, the contract and the first building), so add shot frames for them. Decision: a cost, not a rule, since in landscape the Ridge has no room outward (Pine Hollow is there) and its label stays between it and the Capital, where it still reads as the Ridge's. **A card open** (what the reviews kept finding): a place less than half in view, off the screen or under the card, has no label (`inView`); on a phone, where the card spans the bottom and the map shrinks into the strip above it, only the open place keeps its label and the others come back on close; in landscape the others show only their name and state; a label that still lands on another place's middle hides until the card closes (`overMiddle`). The camera keeps the whole tapped place clear of the card, not only its middle (`keep` with the site's reach, `CameraRig.keep` counts it in `clear`): Pine Hollow sat half under the phone card. **The first view** already framed the whole ring at all three sizes from 1a (no change). **Props by the rails**: land props keep `ROUTE_CLEAR` (.85) from a route's middle, past the bed's `BED` (.66), both in `src/render/empire-plan.js` and shared with the test. **Gates**: smoke's map test fails a label nearer another place than its own at the home view (`cover().near`), and, with each open place's card open in turn, that place under the top bar, the card or off the screen (`cover().hid`) or a label over another place's middle; seen red on the last release's build (map land: Pine Hollow's card, the Gulch's label on the Capital), green on this one. `test/spots.test.js`: a label below the Capital takes a side away from it (seen red with no hub), and `inView`; `test/empire.test.js`: every prop keeps its size and .15 off every rail bed (seen red at the old .6: Dry Creek's sage). `tools/look-shots.js <dir> map` now takes card views (`card-<town>`), prints the labels pointing inward and a hidden open town, and `LOOK_VIEWS` filters the map's views. Release checks: tests and audit ok; smoke ok; perf ok, phone 25.6 ms, Ridge empire 25.2 / 6.1 ms, Falls empire 29.0 / 8.1 ms, the map 27.9 ms with 62 calls; no core file changed since the bots of 2026.11.24-4, so they were not run again. **Left, each in its own box**: the ring reads as a ring only when Thunder Mesa and Radium Springs fill the two side slots (Thunder Mesa's box); the pale blocky tint round Silver Falls and Sandy Bend in landscape and desktop (sharper part 3); a dirt wagon road beside a town reading like its raider road (part 2, with the wagons). **Fresh-eyes read** of before and after map shots (home and every card at phone and landscape, and the release's map frames): better on a phone (the Gulch's name outside the ring; with a card open only the tapped town's name shows and the town stays above the card; nothing under the top bar). It found in landscape another place's name on the lower part of the tapped town (Pine Hollow's on Iron Ridge) and names crowding round the tapped Silver Falls; fixed in the same release: with a card open a name that touches the open town or covers a fifth of any other place hides until the card closes, and smoke's card check fails a name on the open town. Left as decided: the landscape home view keeps the Ridge's name between it and the Capital (see above). Older lines it flagged go to clarity fix 8: "until you tap Resume waves there" on a card with no such button, "Next in 0:43" (next what), "Raid in 1:52" on the label beside "Next raid in 1 min 52 sec." on the card, "a little" beside numbers, and "Stockpile can take" wrapping; and a long outpost card in landscape scrolls its Play button half out of view.
- [x] **Reorient the empire map, part 2: trains and wagons (owner, 2026-11-22: "What happens to the train with the empire map tweaks? Do we also fix that?").** Today `rollWagons` in `src/render/empire-view.js` runs each train one way and pops it in and out in the open. Each spoke ends in a small depot by the town's vault and a station at the Capital; a train leaves the depot, runs in, waits a moment and runs back, so it only appears and vanishes inside a depot or station; goods both ways ride the same train (cars in the goods' colors), busier routes get more trains on a timetable that keeps them apart, and a two-way line gets a passing loop or double track. Wagons do the same on dirt roads before the Rail Office. Gates: a test steps the map and checks no engine or car is ever shown away from its track, none appears or vanishes outside a depot, and two trains on one route never overlap; shots a few seconds apart show them moving. Split from part 1 on 2026-11-22 when the box was added whole (release-sized boxes rule); part 1 lays the spokes and depots' places, part 2 runs the trains on them. From the reviews of part 1 (2026.11.24-5): before the Rail Office a brown wagon road beside a town reads like its raider road, so a wagon road gets a look of its own (wheel ruts, a lighter color) with the wagons.
  Notes (2026.11.24-7): **A timetable per spoke, not per link** (`src/render/empire-trains.js`, pure, so `test/trains.test.js` steps it in node). Decision: vehicles belong to a place's spoke and goods change vehicles at the Capital, which is what the owner's words describe ("a train leaves the depot, runs in, waits and runs back") and keeps every vehicle on one short line; the road round the plaza stays a plain road. Each spoke has two lanes (`LANE` .28 each side), laid from one sampled, smoothed middle line that both the rails and the vehicles use. A wagon drives in, waits 2.4 s, turns in a half circle and drives back; a train has an engine at each end, crosses to the other track just before the platform, waits 2.8 s and runs back, so nothing turns or flips. Every slot of a spoke's cycle is a headway apart (the one ahead clears the platform and the crossover first), and a slot takes up the wanted count only as its vehicle leaves the shed, so a busier spoke gets more vehicles with nothing popping in. The depot is now a long shed over both lanes (`SHED` 1.08 down the road, `shedOf` faces it along the road's first stretch); a vehicle shows only once wholly out from under its roof and goes only wholly under it (a wagon's mule walks ahead, so it goes in further, `out` .52). Cars are open, so their crates show: in the colors of the goods the place sends on the way in, emptied and reloaded at the platform, the goods it gets on the way out (empty if none; `loadGeometry`, instance colors, one more draw). How many: `wagonsFor` over every link through the spoke (one source with the core), trains about half as many with 2 to 4 cars, up to what the spoke holds (Dry Creek 3 trains or 11 wagons, the Ridge and Silver Falls 1 train). When the Rail Office lays rails, the wagons out finish their run before the trains start. Speed no longer grows with traffic (it would break the headway); more vehicles show a busier spoke. **The wagon road** is lighter than a raider road, wider, with two ruts in each lane. **Stations spread**: Silver Falls' and Sandy Bend's roads met the plaza 0.9 apart and their turning wagons overlapped, so `spokeLine` now spreads stations at least half a radian apart round the plaza (`STATION_GAP`), easing the last 1.4 into each in radius and angle together so it never cuts the plaza. **Gates**: `test/trains.test.js` runs every spoke's trains and wagons for 4 minutes while the wanted count goes from the most to one and back, and fails a vehicle more than .02 off its lane, one that shows or goes with any corner outside its shed's roof, a jump bigger than its speed allows, and any overlap of two different trains or wagons on the whole map (boxes on the ground); plus a test that a slot only switches in the shed. Seen red: twice the trains the headway allows (5565 overlaps), slots switching at once (vehicles vanishing 16-18 from the depot), the wagons' first size (overlaps on Pine Hollow's bends) and its first shed (a wagon's mule out of the back wall). Smoke's Rail Office check now waits for the wagons to go home and the trains to come out (up to 30 s). `tools/look-shots.js map` takes `station`, `station-later` (2.5 s on) and `depot`. A first mid-line `//` comment in a patch swallowed a brace again; the audit caught it at once (rule 9). Release checks: tests and audit ok; smoke ok; perf ok, phone 26.0 ms, the map 29.0 ms with 64 calls, Ridge empire 25.3 ms, Falls empire 28.4 ms; no core file changed (the bots of 2026.11.24-4 stand). The sheds and canopies first had red roofs, which read as markers by every town from the home view; they are weathered boards now. **Fresh-eyes read** of the map's home, station and depot shots (before and after rail, phone and landscape) and the What's new card: wagons arriving at the platform between two shots read clearly, and the wagon road reads apart from the raider road up close. Fixed in the same release: no train in the full map's station shots, since a map opened before the flow meter's first sample started every spoke empty (a spoke that has carried goods now starts with at least one vehicle out); the wagon road faint against the sand from far away (a darker edge now); "run in to the Capital" on the card. Left as decided: the road round the plaza stays a plain road under rail (goods change vehicles there); the engine coming out under the shed's eave reads as leaving it. Logged for clarity fix 10: the top bar's rate jumping between shots taken moments apart (+34 to +141/s), which the reviewer flagged as confusing.

The Frost Lens leads the late damage (30-35%, against 36%), so its fork trades strength for a new job rather than adding to it. Numbers from `tools/fork-runs.js`, same gates as parts 1 and 2. Its gate is at its edge (the Ridge's late frost share read close at 35.8-35.9% in 2026.11.23-5 to -8, and a change to contract lengths alone put it at 36.8%, limit 36%), so the fork must bring the share down, and any economy change before it is A/B tested on this gate.

(1.5 times the reach, 0.55 of the hit) and **Cold Snap** "Slows raiders much more. Softer hits." (1.4 times the slow, 45% to 63% off a raider's speed, at 0.8 of the reach and half the hit; no slow takes more than 80%, `SLOW_MOST`), on either path. Both trade the lens's own hits for help to the towers round it, as the box asked. Models: Blizzard three tall white shards round the pillar under a wide pale ring, Cold Snap a crown of thick ice spikes on a snowy foot; a frost pulse now draws its ring as wide as the lens reaches (a fixed 2.1 before). Tried with numbers and dropped: the pulse cap at 5-8 (no pad changed sides), the road-alone term for every lens (agreement with the runs fell from 139 to 105 of 222 and it changed ordinary lens buying). **Bots**: balance, firstplay and idle ok; the Ridge's late frost share 34.4% (35.3% last release, limit 36%), the Falls' 33%; the active bots took Cold Snap 3 times (few lenses reach 20 before the runs end); close lines as before (Ridge wave 10 at 7.0 min on seed 2, economy's Gulch wave 10 25% behind, idle sales 94% on seeds 4 and 5). Perf at 4x: 29.3 / 30.4 / 31.8 ms (gulch, empire, falls-empire; 92 / 67 / 71 draw calls); a first Gulch reading of 50.6 ms had 27 ms of script against 8 in the two runs after it, a load spike (rule 7). Also look at whether the town in play should show an order's time left somewhere besides the Goods sheet (the strip or the Goods button), once the owner has played with it.
- [ ] **Market depth, part 3: the Stock Exchange** (late and optional): each town has a share whose price follows its income and news. Decide with the bots whether it belongs in M6a or after Thunder Mesa, and write the reason here.
- [ ] **Second fork at level 20, part 4: the Mortar (added 2026.11.22-8, so every tower has a box).** The bots' Mortars stop at level 14-16 in the Ridge and the Falls, so first find with `tools/late-towers.js` whether any strategy raises one to 20; if none does by the end of the Falls, say so here and place the box after Thunder Mesa, where the airship lane may change what the Mortar is for. Same gates as parts 1-3 (`fork-runs.js` numbers, the guess checked with `--against`, every finish the best pick somewhere).
- [ ] **Sharper buildings and people, part 2: close-up detail.** Zoomed in on a building, townsfolk or a tower, a higher-detail version (more parts, bevels, small props, people with heads, hats, arms and tools that move), by camera distance, inside the draw-call budget (`--calls 300`) and the perf gates. Before and after shots at phone size, zoomed in (`tools/sharp-shots.js` takes the zoomed view), go to the fresh-eyes reviewer.
- [ ] **Sharper buildings and people, part 3: the contact sheet and shadow edges.** Every model on `tools/sheet.js` checked at phone size, fixing any silhouette that does not read, with the fresh-eyes reviewer judging the sheet before and after. Also from the review of 2026.11.22-12: rock and fence shadows look blocky at every ratio (the 1024 shadow map with 4 reads), and 1.5 with FXAA reads slightly soft beside 1.75 (edge contrast about 13% under the old 1.35); try a tighter shadow box round the camera's view or a sharper FXAA edge (subpixel blend) and measure both with `tools/perf.js` and `tools/sharp-shots.js`. Also the map's pale blocky tint round Silver Falls and Sandy Bend in landscape and desktop (fresh-eyes read of 2026.11.24-4): each town's ground color fades in steps of the ground's grid, so it needs a smoother fade or a finer grid there.
- [ ] **Target:** no buy-and-sell loop earns coins (a test); the level-20 fork and Call to arms pass their bot gates (strategies within 25%, each new option the best buy somewhere, a town never holds with the militia alone); every earlier target still holds.

### M6b. Thunder Mesa and Radium Springs
- [ ] **Thunder Mesa.** The sky lane and the airship. It opens Outpost 4, Tar Seep (crude oil and rubber). It takes the ring's east slot (`SLOTS.mesa` in `src/data/empire.js`), turned to face out like the others; with it the ring starts to read as a ring (fresh-eyes read of 2026.11.24-4: with three of five places filled it did not).
- [ ] **The airfield (owner idea, 2026-11-20).** One per empire, built in Thunder Mesa. Its planes support the other towns, as a patrol that joins fights in any town and flies urgent goods between towns, with upgrades for more planes, range and payload. Decide the split between patrol and freight with the bots, and keep it from replacing towers.
- [ ] **Radium Springs.** Jamming and the Iron Colossus. It opens Outpost 5, Glowrock Camp (bauxite and pitchblende).
- [ ] **Target:**
  - Thunder Mesa opens at 20-30 h and Radium Springs at 50-80 h;
  - the audit says every town is complete;
  - each new town's land on the empire map (its own `FLAVOR`, mesa colors and entrance in rock) passes `test/empire.test.js`.

### M7. The top layer and polish
- [ ] **The top layer.** Laws, the Convention, Legacy and the Legacy perks.
- [ ] **Juice and extras.**
  - Builds rise in scaffolding; the boss gets an entrance; coins burst out.
  - Gamepad support (procedural music moved up to M4, owner request 2026-11-17).
  - Every mission and badge.
- [ ] **Old-save bonus.** Read the old game's save once and grant deeds = min(15, floor(log10 of its lifetime coins)), plus a Pioneer badge. Never write the old key.
- [ ] **Target:** a second life reaches the first life's peak in under 30% of the time, and a bot completes every mission.

### M8. 1.0
- [ ] **Evaluation 3.** Every bot, a fresh-eyes review at phone, landscape and desktop sizes, the model contact sheet, and the fixes.
- [ ] **Release.**
  - Release 1.0.
  - In `../tycoon`, add a "Copper Gulch moved" card linking to the new site; the old game stays playable.
  - Push it with that repo's own checklist.
- [ ] **Target:** 1.0 is live and the old site links to it. After this the task stops and waits for the owner.

## Layout
- **`src/core/`:** pure, deterministic simulation.
- **`src/data/`:** goods, buildings, towers, foes, waves, the tree, laws, perks, badges and `towns/*.js`.
- **`src/render/`:** three.js scene, boards, camera, picking, the Capital, the empire map, effects and icons.
- **`src/assets/models/`:** the code-built models.
- **`src/ui/`:** DOM interface.
- **`src/art/`:** pixel sprites.
- **`src/audio/`:** music and sound.
- **`tools/`:** dev server, headless browser driver, smoke test, screenshots, contact sheet, bots, audit, version bump, update test.
- **`test/`:** `node --test` suites that import the core directly.
- **Reference:** the old game lives in `../tycoon` (frozen). It has good parts to adapt: `src/r3d/kit.js`, `units.js`, `capital3d.js`, `icons.js`, `music.js`, the grading shader, and the economy and combat formulas.

## Roadmap notes
- 2026-11-20 (2026.11.20-17): the pads box took two runs because three gates were near their limits before it started and the bots only said pass or fail. Added the Gate margins box before the Silver Falls target, and a pad rule with a test for pads by an entrance (seen twice in the Falls). The review box of 2026.11.20-15 ships with the map status box as one fix release, not its own loop: five of the last seven releases were review fixes.
- 2026-11-20 (2026.11.20-18): the owner found that the map hid each town's wave state, and no check looked at it; smoke now reads every open place's state word and size on the map. The first run of the gate margins already names the economy Gulch wave 10 (24% behind against 25%) and idle seed 4's growth (3%) as the next gates likely to tip. Nine old tests pinned source strings and had to change for one release, so there is a new rule that word tests check output. Plan for the next run: one batched release of the review box left above the target, with a full firstplay, balance and idle run that ticks the M5 target (the Falls already opens at 7.1-7.7 h on every seed). After that the look pass (M5b) moves the roadmap forward, since four of the last six releases were fixes.
- 2026-11-21 (2026.11.21-1): the review box and the M5 target ship together, so M5 is done and M5b (the look pass) is next, which moves the roadmap forward after five releases of fixes. Two patterns got lasting fixes: one label reworded by review after review (the old-waves row six times, the stock switch five), now a rule that a label flagged twice changes its control or goes, never a third rewording; and old review tests pinning past words (eleven for one label), now one file, `test/words.test.js`, that pins today's words. The screenshot reviewer's own fixes this run went back to words already tried twice ("Research next", "under 1/s"), which the new rule caught. Next run: M5b Towns first, ground and frame before life, with perf on the med tier checked after each part.
- 2026-11-21 (2026.11.21-2): the look pass started with the ground, the rim and the pads, and moved the roadmap forward after five fix releases. The Towns box was split in three, since one release could not hold it. Two patterns got lasting checks: pads pressed against the road (a test now keeps every pad 1.15 from the road's middle) and models spilling off their plot (`FIT` in the board). A pad half cut at the screen's edge was flagged by two reviews; the camera now slides so pads are whole or off screen. New tool `tools/look-shots.js` takes every town's before and after shots for the look reviews in M5b. Next run: Towns part 2 (life), with perf on the med tier checked as it goes; the economy Gulch gate sits at its limit.
- 2026-11-21 (2026.11.21-3): the life moved the roadmap forward a second release in a row. A perf gate on its edge (Silver Falls script, 9.5 against 10) read as a regression of a change that cost nothing, and a single run could not tell; `tools/perf.js` now prints close lines like the bots, and the rule under How this repo enforces says to judge a change by an A/B run, never one run. A small core saving (cached mod sums) gives that gate room again. The look box for the 2026.11.21-2 review ("Sell 60", the dim title footer) is small and stays queued after part 3, batched with whatever part 3's review finds, so the look pass is not split into fix loops. Next run: Towns part 3 (buildings that grow).
- 2026-11-21 (2026.11.21-4): part 3 of the look pass shipped after the run that built it stopped before its commit, the second time in a row, so How the task works now says to start the bots early and commit before more work, and to check the machine's load before judging perf. A comment swallowing code was written a second time and is now an audit rule. The Silver Falls script gate went red with nothing changed. The A/B work found the shadow pass redrawn every frame (still shadows took a third of the draw calls off), but the real cause was an orphan test browser from 2026-11-16 using four cores; three releases of "close" gate notes were the machine, not the game. The driver now stops its own stale browsers, and the task checks load before judging perf. Next run: the empire map box, then the M5b target.
- 2026-11-21 (2026.11.21-5): the empire map box and the M5b target shipped together, so the look pass is done after four releases that all moved the roadmap forward. The fresh-eyes review caught one new pattern, models made for the close view reused at the map's height, and it is now a rule with a map mode in `tools/look-shots.js`. M6 was split as planned: M6a (labels on the map, the level-20 fork, Call to arms, the market) with its own bot gates, then M6b (the two new towns). The map labels covering the middle on a phone were flagged by both reviews, so they get the first box of M6a rather than a third note. Running the bots while smoke ran made smoke pass its time cap; run smoke and perf on a quiet machine after the bots, as How the task works already says for perf. Next run: the map labels box, then the level-20 fork, starting with the economy Gulch gate at 25%.
- 2026-11-21 (2026.11.21-6): the map labels box shipped, the first box of M6a. The machine was busy again at the start of smoke (64%): an orphan headless Edge from another session's screenshot tool, six days old, and the browser of a smoke run this run stopped with `timeout`. `tools/cdp.js` now closes its browsers when its run is stopped by a signal and stops any of its own whose driver is gone; never stop smoke with `timeout`, run it in the background instead. The level-20 fork box was split in two (the Still, then the Cannon or the Mortar), since core, card, bots, explainer and rockets would not fit one release. Both were fixed at the cause (pay tied to the market's price, an opening tied to a won wave, not a clock). The clarity pass was one box with two evaluations in it, so it is split (a newcomer's eyes, then the bots' table) as the release-sized rule asks. Next: clarity pass part 1.
- 2026-11-23 (2026.11.23-2): clarity pass part 1 shipped with its first fix and the batched words, the fifth release in a row that moves the roadmap forward. The new playthrough tool found in its first minute of core output what no gate measured: a goal strip blank for hours and goals no one could act on, because every gate counts times to milestones, not what the screen asks for. Lasting fixes: the strip now always names a step (tests), and a playthrough runs at every evaluation (How this repo enforces). Two tool slips got rules: a game page saves itself on unload, so a tool that loads saves writes them from a page with no game; and a card condition that reads the page is never due off it. A // comment swallowed code a fifth time, in a smoke check, and the audit missed it (its rule read only src and only a statement after a semicolon); it now reads src, tools and test and also catches a condition left after the comment, proved on the very line. The review's top 10 are fixes 2-10 after part 2; the Capital wall and saving come first, since they end the game for a newcomer. Next: part 2 (the bots' table), then fixes 2 and 3.
- 2026-11-23 (2026.11.23-3): clarity pass part 2 (the bots' table) shipped with fix 2 (saving for a coin goal), the sixth release in a row that moves the roadmap forward. The new `tools/progress.js` showed what the milestone gates cannot: there is always something to buy, but the mid-game goes 1-3.5 hours with nothing new, and the swarm strategy never reaches Silver Falls on two of three seeds, since its Falls gate starts from the active bot's game. Two boxes came of it (fix 3b, the mid-game's gaps; swarm's wall at the Ridge's wave 50, with a gate on every strategy's own empire run), and the progress table now runs at every evaluation. A bot change that sent the newcomer down a new path found a core crash in a 6-hour run (spreading poison as a boss dropped its crew), the second time two systems met in a way no test set up (the militia tap broke the camera's list of selections in 2026.11.22-11); a test now runs every tower finish against every boss wave, and the crash has its own test. Next: fix 3 (the Capital wall), then 3b, since together they are why a newcomer sees nothing new after about 4 hours.
- 2026-11-23 (2026.11.23-4): clarity fix 3 shipped (the Capital wall), the seventh release in a row that moves the roadmap forward. The cause the review could not see was a bot gap: the newcomer had no tap for the strip's Capital step, so no run ever founded it, the second time a strip step with no bot tap hid everything after it (Pine Hollow in Evaluation 2); it is now a rule under How this repo enforces, with a test. A second pattern seen twice, a strip step about another town read or kept in the wrong town, is now a Copper Gulch rule. The quiet-stretch part of fix 3's gate moved to fix 3b in the open, since what is left is the mid-game's lack of new things, which is 3b's job; two small probes (`tools/capital-probe.js`, `tools/quiet-probe.js`) make that check take a minute instead of a playthrough run. Next: fix 3b.
